# Ezekiel

# ANNOTATIONS ON EZEKIEL

## THE ARGUMENT

Ezekiel, of the priestly race, was carried captive to Babylon, in the second captivity by Nebuchadnezzar; not accounting upon that time, when Manasseh was carried to Babylon by the captains of the host of Esarhaddon; or Asnapper, king of Assyria, Ezra 4:2, 10. 2 Chr. 33:11. as the beginning of the accomplishment of that prophetical denunciation of Isaiah, 2 Kings 20:17, 18. Isaiah 39:6, 7. But when Nebuchadnezzar carried away Jehoiachin, or Jeconiah, with his mother, divers of his princes, and many of the people. Namely, in the eighth year of Nebuchadnezzar's reign both over Babylon, and Judah, then was Ezekiel carried captive, 2 Kings 24:12. Ezek. 32:21. and 40:1. verses 1, 2, etc. In the first captivity by Nebuchadnezzar, Daniel and his three companions, among others, were carried away. And that was in the first year of the reign of Nebuchadnezzar, and in the fourth year of Jehoiakim king of Judah, Jer. 25:1. Dan. 1:1, 2. In the captivity of Jechoniah, and Ezekiel, say some. But much rather in the former, wherein Daniel was carried captive; then, as the most learned chronologers do justly hold, began the desolation of Jerusalem, and the seventy years of Judah's captivity, and Babel's reign, Jer. 25:1, 11, 12. In which time of distress, God did raise up prophets to tell his people of their woeful troubles; and to assure them, that his church should have deliverance at the time appointed of seventy years; and that such enemies as either afflicted them; or rejoiced at their miseries should be destroyed. Jeremy in Judah taught that Jerusalem should be as Shiloh, Jer. 7:12, 14. foretold, that Babel must reign for those seventy years; and writing to Judah that was then in Babylon, he bids them not to look for return till those seventy years were ended. Jeremy was opposed by false prophets; by Hananiah in Jerusalem, Jer. 28:1, 2, 3. by Shemaiah and others in Babylon, Jer. 29:8, 9, 24–29. wherefore to countenance him, God stirs up Ezekiel in Babylon, to second him, and confirm his prophecies; and to persuade the remnant in Judah by writing, to yield to Babel's yoke, to prevent greater judgments. This prophet's example, and two captivities already past, might have warned Judah sufficiently, not to stir up more wrath, by provoking God against them, to give them so up into the Chaldeans' hands, as to burn their temple, raze their city, kill their princes, disperse the people, and to extirpate out of Jerusalem all signs of Judah's church, and commonwealth; all which God, in these prophecies, does foretell should come to pass, did they still persist and resist. But all sufficed not: and upon their continued obstinacy and rebellion, all this did fall out accordingly. For his course and general method in this book, Ezekiel thus proceeds, upon these heads; after his vocation and calling to his prophetical office, he foretells the woeful siege and desolation of Jerusalem, and the calamities, and captivity of Judah; he shows the causes of such evils, to be the violation of God's laws; and that in a transcendent manner. And for them already in captivity, he reproves them also for many sins sharply; exhorting them to repent, and to be confident of their return to Canaan, and of the restoration promised at the time appointed. Furthermore, he prophesies against the neighbor nations, who had been enemies to the Jews; especially Tyre, Egypt, and Edom, that they should be involved in the same Chaldean deluge. Then he goes on to foretell the ruin of Gog and Magog, the last and cruelest enemies of the Jews, before the first coming of Christ, that those enemies should be subdued, and the church delivered. The effect of the one, and the other, should be chiefly performed by Christ, of whom in this book are many notable promises; and in whom the glory of the new temple, city, and kingdom, largely showed him in vision, and described, should be perfectly restored. He prophesied in Chaldea at the time Jeremy prophesied in Judah; wherefore there is very great agreement between these two prophets. But he began four and thirty years after Jeremy; namely, in the fifth year of Jehoiachin's captivity; and that was about the year of the world, 3400. and afore our Savior's incarnation, about 600 years. He continued prophesying two and twenty years at least, ch. 29:17. Of the manner of his death, what ever divers authors do record, yet we have nothing in Scripture.

### A VIEW OF THE SEVERAL TIMES, AND PARTS OF THIS PROPHECY. IN THE

- Fifth year of Jehoiachin's captivity, fourth month, fifth day, he begins to prophesy, ch. 1. and so on to the eighth chapter.
- Sixth year, sixth month, fifth day, he is carried in a vision to Jerusalem, chs. 8, 9, 10, 11. And after his return, he prophesies on, to the 20 chapter.
- Seventh year, fifth month, tenth day, he is consulted by the elders. Ch. 20. And prophesies on, chs. 21, 22, 23.
- Ninth year, tenth month, tenth day, the siege of Jerusalem by Nebuchadnezzar began, ch. 24. (2 Kings 25:1.) And he prophesies most woeful destruction against them. And in the 25 chapter, he prophesies against neighbor nations.
- Tenth year, tenth month, twelfth day, he prophesies against Pharaoh, and Egypt, ch. 29. to ch. 30, verse 20.
- Eleventh year, (first month) first day, he prophesies against Tyrus, 26, 27, 28 chapters, to verse 20. against Zidon, ch. 28, verse 20. to the end of that chapter.
- Eleventh year, first month, seventh day, he prophesies against Egypt, ch. 30, verse 20. to the end of that chapter.
- 11 year, third month, first day, he prophesies against Egypt chs. 31. and 32.
- 11 year fourth month, ninth day, the city is taken, Jer. 52:5, 6. 2 Kings 25:2, 3, 4. and in the fifth month, and tenth day, Nebuzaradan burnt the temple and city, Jer. 52:12.
- Twelfth year, tenth month, fifth day, the news of the taking Jerusalem is brought to Ezekiel, ch. 33:21. Whereupon he prophesies of further wrath, for their sins, ch. 32, verses 24–33. And against the shepherds of Israel; with a promise of the true shepherd Christ, ch. 34. Against mount Seir, ch. 35. Promising restoration of Israel, through Christ, and his kingdom, chs. 36 and 37. and foretelling the destruction of Gog and Magog, chs. 38. and 39.
- Twelfth year, twelfth month, first day, he prophesies against Egypt, ch. 32–17.
- Twelfth year, twelfth month, fifteenth day, he prophesies against Egypt, ch. 32:17–32. And of the watchman's duty, ch. 33:1–21.
- 25 year, first month, tenth day, he has that large vision of a new temple, etc. chapters 40, 41, 42, 43, 44, 45, 46, 47, 48.
- 27 year, first month, first day, he prophesies against Egypt, ch. 29, verse 17. and ch. 30:1–20.

## CHAPTER 1

**Verse 1.** *{Now}* Heb. *And.* So happily coupling his outward expressions in writing, with the inward notions and visions of his mind. Or else it is a Hebraism, only for grace of speech; very usual in that language: whereupon as here this book, so in like manner with this word do begin the books of Exodus, Leviticus, Numbers, Joshua, Ruth, Jonah, and most of the historical books of the Old Testament.

This chapter contains a vision of the glory of God, and of Christ.

*{in the thirtieth year}* Not from the Jubilee; for this time agrees not with that account: but thirty years after that the book of the law was found; which was the eighteenth year of the reign of Josiah, when also the covenant with God was renewed, religion reformed, and a very solemn Passover kept, 2 Kings 22:3, 8, 22, 23. 2 Chron. 35:19. The remembrance of such benefits gave the Jews occasion of this account. A thing not unusual with them in like cases. So are years reckoned from the calling of Abraham, the birth of Isaac, the coming out of Egypt, the division of the kingdoms of Judah and Israel, the captivity and deportation into Babylon: and others, that we need not here to fly and fetch it from an epoch and era of an heathen king Nabopolassar, not mentioned in Scripture; though that Chaldean account may agree with this: nor refer it to the prophet's age, which he would not take upon him in this manner to set down; as being without any example of such like reckoning in Scripture: and his birthday a time but obscure, and not vulgarly known abroad,

*{fourth month}* Answering to part of our June and July. See annotations on chap. 40:1.

*{fifth day}* Towards the end of our June. Thus particularly God would have us take notice of the times, and to observe the remarkable passages of God's providence and acts in church and states, which fall out in them, Hos. 14:9. That this fifth day was a Sabbath day, cannot be ascertained.

*{among the captives}* Heb. *in the midst of the captivity:* when Jehoiachin or Jeconiah was led captive to Babel, 2 Kings 24:12. then Ezekiel, chap. 40:1. Mordecai, Esth. 2:5, 6. and many of the people were led also away; ten thousand captives at least, 2 Kings 24:14, 16. of which number were three thousand and three and twenty of the tribe of Judah, Jeremy 52:28. In the midst, that is, among, as Joshua 7:13. thus the godly are mingled in this world with the wicked, Psal. 120:5. Apoc. 2:9, 13, 14, 15. and a lily among thorns, Cant. 2:2. yea, thus they are wrapped up in the same outward calamities and afflictions with the wicked, Eccl. 9:1, 2. 1 Pet. 5:10. And therefore we must not judge of men by their outward estate and condition. It was the great sin of Job's three friends, Luk. 13:1, 2, 3, 4. Psal. 73:15.

*{Chebar}* Chap. 3:15. a river of Mesopotamia, rising, as is thought, from the mountain Masius, and flowing into Euphrates; near to which it should seem, were appointed some habitations for the Jews, which were led into captivity, chap. 3:15. Psal. 137:1. This seems to be that Chabor in Ptolemy, though some more probably take this for Euphrates itself. Thus Daniel saw visions by the side of the great river Hiddekel, or Tygris, Dan. 10:4. That this is called a Sabbatical river, and the reason why, is all but a Rabbinical dream.

*{The heavens were opened}* To his thinking, and in human appearance; a kind of speech much used in the description of visions, Mar. 3:16. Act. 10:11. Revel. 4:1. and chap. 19:11. to note a sudden breaking forth of light from above, created by God miraculously, as if it came out of heaven itself; in and through the midst of which were shown the representation of divine things. Or, as Joh. 1:51. or Act. 7:56. God strengthening his eyes to see at such a distance.

*{I saw}* Hence prophets called seers, 1 Sam. 9:9. Esay 30:10. which points out their duty, Ezek. 33:3, 6.

*{Visions}* God has revealed himself to his prophets sometimes mouth to mouth, and face to face, Exod. 33:11. Deut. 34:10. Num. 12:6, 7, 8. sometimes in dreams, Gen. 28:12. & 31:11. sometimes in visions, as Gen. 15:1. And so here. And these visions are representations of visible things to the eye of the body, or at least of the mind, to a man awake, to signify things present, or to come; and that with such an irradiation of the mind, and impulse upon the spirit, as ascertains the mind, and will, and truth, or God in them. See also chap. 7:26. & chap. 12:12, 23.

*{of God}* That is, which God showed, or, wherein I saw God: or, notable and excellent visions; so that it might be known it was no natural dream, or illusion of Satan, or lying fancy: but that the visions in this chapter came of God, see annotations on chap. 40:2.

**V. 2.** *{Fifth year}* Of Jeconiah's captivity, and consequently Zedekiah's reign. And from Jeconiah's captivity does Ezekiel constantly begin all his reckonings of the times. This does in certainty denote the time of thirty years, and the beginning of it, if it were doubtful in the former verse: for if to these five of Jeconiah's captivity, you add eleven of Jehoiakim, and the fourteen last of Josiah, there will arise thirty, spoken of in the first verse: and so Ezekiel prophesies thirty years after the finding of the book of the law, in the fifth year of Jeconiah's captivity. And from this time to that, chap. 8:1. is a year, and two months: within which space all happened that is set down in the seven first chapters of this prophecy: and from this day and date of time, to the beginning of the siege, are four years, six months, and five days, chap. 24:1. Jer. 52:4.

*{Of King Jehoiachin's captivity}* This Jehoiachin was eight years old, when his father Jehoiakim began to reign; and himself likely in some sort joined with him, 2 Chron. 36:9. And 18, when he began to reign alone, 2 Kings 24:8. He was captive seven and thirty years, 2 Kings 25:27. He was called also Jechoniah, and Coniah; and, as some would (though not so likely) Shallum, Jer. 22:11. He is written *childless*, Jer. 22:30. Salathiel was his son, 1 Chron. 3:17. Mat. 1:12. only by legal succession, not by natural generation, Luk. 3:27. In him ended the kings, and royal line of David: our Savior came of Nathan, David's other son, Luke 3:31. The crowns, Zech. 6:11. are set upon the head of Joshuah the high priest. These five years of captivity and affliction mended them not.

**V. 3.** *{The word of the LORD}* That set down in the second chapter, and continued to the twelfth verse of the third chapter.

*{Came expressly}* Heb. *by being hath been;* or, *in coming it came:* to assure the entity, evidence, and truth herein.

*{Ezekiel}* Heb. *Jechezkel* signifying the strong God, or the strength of God, or one strengthened by God. A name by God's providence well suiting to his condition, and calling: as likewise the name Isaac, Solomon, John, Jesus.

*{the Priest}* So he was both priest and prophet: and so very few of the prophets were.

*{The son of Buzi}* And therefore not the son, or servant of Jeremy as some would.

*{Land of the Chaldeans}* That is, in Mesopotamia a part of Chaldea. Abram had dwelt there, Gen. 11:31. Act. 7:2, 4. whereof Babylon was the chief city, built by Nimrod. The land called the land of Nimrod, Mich. 5:6. and of Shinar, Gen. 10:10. It was a land of wickedness, Zech. 5:11. And they a martial, cruel people, Jer. 6:22, 23. & chap. 50, 41, 42. and yet here in this place God raises up a prophet in much mercy, for the good of his church, in this distress.

*{and the hand of the LORD}* The Spirit of the Lord God by his coeval and coequal Spirit, works as a man does by his hand, Act. 11:21. See the power, virtue, and efficacy of his Spirit, 1 John 4:4.

*{was there upon him}* Strongly; the intrinsical power of his Spirit, excited and brought Ezekiel to this prophetical office and function, as it were against his will, he not thinking of it, much less affecting or ambitiously desiring it. See chap. 3:12, 14, 22, 24. & chap. 37:1. For this John says, Rev. 1:10. *I was in the Spirit.* See 1 Kings 18:46. 2 Kings 3:15. Then God's word and ordinances are of power, and to purpose, when God's hand and Spirit goes with them, 1 Cor. 2:4, 5.

**V. 4.** *{A whirlwind}* The beginning of the vision, which continues to the end of the chapter. The sum of it is set down by the prophet himself, ver. 28. in these words, *This was the appearance of the likeness of the Glory of the Lord.* More fully and particularly, it shadows out the God of glory, and the Son of Man, the Lord Jesus Christ, guiding and governing the heaven and the earth, and all things in them; and their wheelings and turnings, and mutations, by his active providence, and more specially his church, and the affairs of it, by his eternal Spirit, using therein the ministry of his angels, and inferior creatures: this vision is oftentimes renewed, and mentioned in whole, or in part, chap. 3:23. ch. 8:4. & ch. 10:15, 20, 22. & chap. 40:3. This vision serves for the prophet's heavenly confirmation in his future calling, fitting and preparing him to it, and encouraging him in it; for the sight of God's glory is very efficacious upon the spirits of men: and it serves also for the divine credit of the prophecies hereafter following. The difficulties that arise in the explication of this vision, are acknowledged by all authors.

*{a whirlwind}* Heb. *a wind of tempest.* This, and what follows, is a forerunner of the glory and majesty of God; as Job 30:1. and as Act. 2:2, 3. and to rouse up Ezekiel's mind to due attention: and not unfitly figuring the enemies which God would use as instruments, for the punishing of his people the Jews. As a whirlwind, were the companies and their armies. And so God threatens, chap. 13:11, 13. & Jer. 4:11, 12, 13. See Prov. 1:27. Esay 41:16.

*{out of the north}* And thence came the Jews' enemies, the Babylonians, and other nations serving them. So Jer. 1:13, 14, 15. Hab. 1:6, 7, 8, 9, 10.

*{a great cloud}* A sign of God's presence: so Exod. 19:16. 2 Chron. 5:13, 14. And as a great cloud likewise were the armies of the enemies, for numbers and swiftness, as clouds covering the face of the earth, chap. 39:9. 2 Kings 25:1. Hab. 1:8. and flying as a cloud, Esay 60:8. bringing clouds of darkness and gloominess; as Joel 2:2. of trouble and distress, Zeph. 1:15. Clouds of blood; as a great cloud pouring down upon the Jews showers of destructions, storms and tempests, Esay 28:2, 10. Ezek. 30:18. & chap. 38:9. so Jer. 4:13. as clouds full of floods and fire, and hail and lightnings, and thunderbolts, Psal. 11:6. Thus God orders, and rules both meteors and men, Esay 19:1. Psal. 104:3. Amos 5:8.

*{and a fire infolding itself}* Vapors likely in the cloud, kindling, and burning into themselves, the flame turning inward, catching itself, as the Hebrew is, and making as it were a fiery globe: some would apply the signification of this to sin, and the nature of it. But we rather conceive, it represents the fiery wrath of God; or secondarily of Nebuchadnezzar, his instrument, who burnt up temple, city, and all.

*{and a brightness was about it}* As a shining about the fire; as rays about the sun. God's fiery wrath and judgment upon sinners make the splendor and glory of his justice to shine abroad, Exod. 15:6.

*{Colour}* Heb. *eye.* so Lev. 13:55. Num. 11:7.

*{of amber}* Or a most lively color, as of the purest burning brass; or rather, as of red, hot, fiery burning coals, ver. 13. whence angels are called seraphim, Esay 6:2, 6. as fiery and burning in the speedy and fervent execution of God's will: Psal. 103. See 2 Kings 6:17. Heb. 1:7.

*{out of the midst of the fire}* This clears the meaning of the word midst next foregoing; as also in the beginning of the next verse. See Apoc. 1:16.

**V. 5.** *{Also out of the midst thereof came}* The meaning of this vision seems to be; that the Son of God who had established the ark of the covenant, with all the things belonging to it, for a sign of his residence among his people, and for a figure of heaven where he dwells, and reigns in glory among his angels; does here reveal his heavenly majesty in forms and shapes answerable to the earthly figures of the temple; appearing in judgment against his people; purposing to depart from them, and to destroy them, as it is declared, Ezek. 10:17, 19. & 11:23. until the time appointed for the restoration of the church: at which time he will return in the same form, Ezek. 43:3. See the like visions, Dan. 7:9. Rev. 4:6, 7.

*{four}* Four-formed creatures; as God's ministering spirits in all the four quarters of the world: in themselves very numerous, Dan. 7:10. Mat. 5:9.

*{Living creatures}* Or, *wights:* shadowing out and signifying not the armies of Nebuchadnezzar, coming against Jerusalem to destroy it, as some would have it out of that, chap. 43:3. but the angels called cherubims, chap. 10:15, 20. See Gen. 3:24. which are God's ministering spirits, Heb. 1:14. for the good of the godly, to inform and guide them, to defend and guard them, to eye them in the assemblies, comfort them in afflictions, and attend them at their deaths: and for the wicked, to oppose and withstand them, and to execute God's judgments upon them. Here they are represented in these hieroglyphics. As then Ezekiel sees the glory of God, and of Christ; so also he sees the ministry of his angels which are God's great officers in the great family of heaven and earth; and which, in wit, excel, in might are strong, in service unweary, in quickness fleet. What some here write of the four elements to be shadowed hereby, or the four cardinal virtues, or the four faculties in the soul, or four chief passions in man, or four monarchies in the world, or else the four evangelists, *Matthew, Mark, Luke* and *John:* or yet of the name *tetragrammaton Jehovah*, consisting of four letters, or of Christ our Savior, his person, and offices as here mystically meant, are all rather wandering fancies, and imaginations, than notions suiting with, or proper to the words and context of this, and the following verses.

*{their appearance}* Differing from those four beasts, Apoc. 4:6, 7, 8. And their appearances we may see, their essence we cannot.

*{the likeness of a man}* For stature, proportion, and all things; saving such differences and alterations as are set down in the six verses following. They had such qualities in likeness as are set out by these parts of a man: principally, reason, which is the property and propriety of man among all visible creatures, 2 Sam. 14:20. Apoc. 4:6, 8. 1 Pet. 1:12. 1 Chron. 11:10.

**V. 6.** *{And every one had four faces}* Of this see more in the 10 ver.

*{four wings}* To signify their winged speed, in doing God's will. Of these, see more, ver. 8, 9, 11, 23, 24, 25. and chap. 10:12. Here they have four wings apiece, Esay 6. & Apoc. 4. every one has six wings. Their swiftness is beyond the swift motion of the sun, and of the stars, which yet is almost beyond all admiration.

**V. 7.** *{And their feet}* Comprehending the thighs and legs withal, as may be gathered by that which follows.

*{straight feet}* Heb. *a straight foot:* round, without turning, to run readily any way, as a round bowl or globe is ready for motion any way, Heb. 12:13.

*{sole of a calf's foot}* To signify, as it should seem, the indefatigable motion of angels in the service of God, and his church.

*{and they sparkled}* Themselves; or rather their feet sparkled with their speed, and purity in doing God's will.

*{brass}* Or, *Steel*, Apoc. 1:15.

**V. 8.** *{The hands of a man}* The hand notes action, Eccl. 9:10. Prov. 3:27. This is to signify their ability and action; as formerly their will and readiness of desire was shadowed out. Here is the virtue and diversity of the angels' operations, chap. 10:7, 8, 12. They are for work and service, they rest not, Apoc. 4:8. and are without weariness. We should do likewise, Joh. 13:17. Apoc. 22:14. See more, Ezek. 10:12.

*{under their wings}* Will and work go together. Some here do stretch the situation of their hands under their wings, to farther notions, than we can apprehend a warrant for.

*{four sides}* On the four sides of the wagon, on each side of which there was a cherub, as some say: but rather under their wings on the four sides, as the express words are. And so every one having four wings, ver. 6. there were sixteen hands under their sixteen wings; thus placed, in this order, chap. 18:21.

**V. 9.** *{Their wings were joined}* Touched each other in their utmost parts.

*{one to another}* Heb. *a woman to her sister.* To show their joint unanimity, harmony, and correspondency, in speeding to perform God's will enjoined; which ever lay ready before them; and they ready and prepared for it, of what nature or kind soever it were: such unanimity, and joining our helps together should be in us, for the doing of God's will. The wicked practice it against him, Psal. 2:2.

*{turned not}* See vers. 12, ready straight to all services, without need or use of so much let, as the turning of themselves. See our duty, Luk. 9:62. Phil. 3:23, 14. That returning ver. 14. was in the same manner as they ran, without turning of their bodies, after they had done their work; as if he should say, they ran to and fro without any winding or circulation.

**V. 10.** *{As for the likeness of their faces,}* They are likened to the faces of four principal creatures. They seem to represent, and hieroglyphically describe, (as earthly things may) the human understanding, the lion-like power, the ox-like usefulness, the eagle-like speediness of the angels, in their ministration and obedience to God's will, and for the service of his church and people, Apoc. 4:7. Thus angels are; and thus like should we be.

*{They four had, &c.}* Each one had four faces, ver. 6. and so sixteen in all. Each one, of a man, before: of an eagle behind: of a lion, on the right side: of an ox, on the left side.

**V. 11.** *{upward}* It may be both faces, and wings: so some translate upward, towards him that sat upon the throne above the firmament, that was over their heads, ver. 22, 23, 25, 26, to admire and adore him, Heb. 1:6. And as an hawk upon the wing, ready, and watching unanimously to receive his commands; to do his will. Mat. 18:10. & chap. 8:9. Psal. 123:1, 2. Thus it should be with us, Mat. 16:10. and our minds should be upward, Col. 3:1. Phil. 3:20. The church has the moon, and all sublunary things under her feet, Apoc. 12:1.

*{Upward}* Or, *divided above,* as it may be translated. Namely above the shoulders, where the four faces did begin to divide themselves; and where the four wings of the cherubims were also divided.

*{two covered their bodies}* Ver. 23. Every one had two which covered on this side, and every one had two which covered on that side their bodies. This might be so, by joining of the wings one to another: so ver. 23. Esay 6:2. the words are, *with twain he covered his feet.* Likely this for decency, and modesty; as in those cases, Exod. 20:26. & chap. 28:42. or else, as Moses put a veil on his face, Exod. 34:33, 34, 35. So providing to help and succor human infirmity; as we use to shadow our eye with our hand, when we would look up to the bright body of the sun.

**V. 12.** *{whither the Spirit}* The Spirit of God who is the guide of the holy angels, in all their functions, and performances; the Spirit of him that sat upon the throne, who did drive and put forward the live wights, (as the wheels to the same kind of motion, ver. 20.) for the angels are his ministering spirits, Heb. 1:12. Or, we may understand it, of the spirit of the living creatures, guided indeed by the Spirit of God.

*{They turned not}* See vers. 7, 12, 17. Chap. 10:11.

**V. 13.** *{Like burning, &c.}* In love, and zeal, to obey God's will. And it may be, in zeal and fury against sinners, ready in indignation, to execute God's vengeance upon them.

*{It went up and down}* This likeness and appearance of fiery burning coals, and lamps, was in continual motion, darting out rays incessantly.

*{and the fire was bright}* This fire that went up and down, was bright and shining, gave light.

*{and out of the fire went forth lightning}* That is, dreadful signs of God's fiery indignation, and judgments to be executed, by the angels, upon sinners.

**V. 14.** *{Ran}* With speed, as a flash of lightning. God uses the ministry of angels, in his administration of things here below; and particularly in the execution of his judgments against sinners: examples whereof are very plentiful in the Scriptures: and the angels as readily do his will, and have influence from Christ suitable to their administration.

*{and returned}* That is, ran to and fro without turning about, ver. 9. and they ran and returned; not in a tumultuary way, or by chance; or according to their own minds, but according as their head did guide them; and when they had executed his will; for afore they returned not, till God had changed the state of things; as Mat. 8:9.

*{a flash of lightning}* Most speedily, as it were in the twinkling of an eye, Mat. 24:27. Luk. 17:24.

**V. 15.** *{One wheel}* As one; yet more in number, ver. 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21. The cherubims covering the ark are called a chariot, 1 Chr. 28:18. And wheels are set to the celestial throne, Dan. 7:9. See Ecclesiasticus, chap. 49:8. The meaning here is, that the prophet did see a wheel by every one of the living creatures: so that they being four, there were also four wheels, ver. 16, 18. See chap. 10:9. what is meant by the wheels, is a thing full of obscurity and difficulty: yet most understand by them, this inferior world; which is as a wheel round, and subject to many wheelings, rollings, and turnings, various mutations, and changes. The turning of this wheel is daily seen in this world. And the story of Sesostris king of Egypt, may be a lively emblem and expression of it: and chap. 10:13. is translated and expounded by sundry learned divines, for a divine warrant, to understand by the wheel this mutable inferior orb. In which the church militant is tumbled and tossed. See that verse, and the annotations on it. And others not much differing, hereby understand the godly people that are in the world, and their various state and condition.

*{upon the earth}* Newly upon that floor which appeared in this vision, ver. 19, 21.

*{by the living creatures}* In, and upon which wheel the living wights exercised their offices and functions.

*{with his four faces}* The wheel had four faces or sides, ver. 17.

**V. 16.** *{The appearance}* The color and show.

*{of the wheels}* Therefore more than one: four in this verse, and verse 18, yet as one, being as a wheel in the midst of a wheel, as appears in this verse. See chap. 10:9.

*{like unto the colour of a beryl}* A precious stone, of a green sea-color; representing likely the unstable floating condition of this sublunary world, and of all things in it. The Hebrew word is Tarshish, and Tarshish in Scripture is often used to signify the sea: and it is also the name of the metropolitan city of Cilicia, near to the sea, whither Jonas fled, Jonah 1:3. the apostle Paul's country, Act. 21:39. & 22:3. But here it must be understood of a precious stone; as appears, chap. 10:9. and that of a green sea color.

*{had one likeness}* In all respects, so that he which saw one, saw all, and thereupon called *one wheel*, ver. 15.

*{a wheel in the middle of a wheel}* Not comprehensively one infolding another; then they could not be perfectly one like another: but transversely, all made up one spherical figure, touching themselves, at right angles; that without turning, or any delay, they might run and roll any way, on any side as is showed in the next verse, east, west, north, or south. This also may note happily the intricacy and perplexity of the carriage of the affairs of this world, to human understanding.

**V. 17.** *{went}* See chap. 10:11.

*{Returned not}* They had no stops or retrogradations; they went on constantly, went through, they did not move out of the direct tract, wherein the living creatures did lead them; but even as the live creatures were led by their chief, so did they also guide the wheels. See ver. 20. a figure of the agreeing harmony, which is in all the works of God's providence towards his church; and of the constant carrying of them on to their appointed ends, and periods, Esay 43:13.

**V. 18.** *{Rings}* Or, *rims,* or *strakes,* or *trendles,* or circumferences.

*{so high}* Of a fearful height and compass; and motion sequently: representing likely the high, wondrous, unsearchable administration of things in this world, and of the passages of God's providence in them; bringing fear and dread, amazement and astonishment to men, Job 21:7, etc. and chap. 12:6. Psal. 73:1, etc. Jer. 12:1. Hab. 1:ver. 2, 3. these wheels are of such a height, and their motions such as none can reach, Psal. 36:6. Rom. 11:23.

*{Rings were full of eyes}* Instead of great nails wherewith cartwheels are garnished, there were eyes, for to represent either the stars of heaven, or more particularly, God's all-seeing providence, in all the parts of his government, 2 Chr. 16:9. Prov. 15:3. thereby guiding, as the least, so the most high and formidable things of this inferior orb, and sublunary world; howsoever they may seem here to be carried blindly, rashly, confusedly. See Act. 4:27, 28. yea though the second causes be blind, and know not their own motions; or move otherwise, Esay 45:4. and chap. 10:7. yea or contrary ways; yet he makes them subservient to his ends. Yet here are not excluded the gifts of God's Spirit in the instruments which he employs to this end, under him. See Zach. 3:9. & 4:10. See Rev. 4:6. In Ezek. 10:12. the body, backs, hands, wings, of the four wights, were full of eyes also. God's providence reaches all: no place left to chance, or fortune, even in the least thing.

**V. 19.** *{And when}* See ver. 17. as the camp and host of Israel removed and rested, by the guidance of the pillar of cloud, and fire. See Apoc. 14:4. Their movings, forward, upward, every way; their rest depended upon the living creatures, was guided and ordered by them. God orders both the angels, and all second causes, in all their motions, by his eye of providence, and secret instinct.

*{were lift up}* See ver. 20, 21. Some do conceive hereby, that God does sometimes raise the spirit of the creature to more than an ordinary height, and enables it to unwonted services, Zach. 12:8.

**V. 20.** *{whithersoever the spirit, &c.}* Verse 12. the impression and impulse of God's Spirit was their guide and mover. Yea God acts in his just judgment, even in the motions of Satan and sinful men, not changing their wills, but making use of them to bring about his own designs. 2 Sam. 17:14.

*{for the spirit of the living creature}* Heb. *the spirit of life.* Verse 21. that spirit wherewith the living wights were moved, was in the wheels. That one Spirit of God works all in all. The same divine inspiration which did drive the live wights, did also give a motion to the wheels, not enliven them: to signify that God's Spirit, the vigor, virtue, and instinct of it, gives motion to all things according to their several principles, and services: that God is present effectually in all the instruments of his providence; and that Christ does do all in all in his church, 1 Cor. 12:6. Ephes. 1:11. Col. 3:11. Rom. 8:9, 14. 1 Cor. 6:17.

*{they went}* Without dispute, without delay, immediately, cheerfully. See Rom. 8:14. Joh. 14:17. Psal. 148:8.

**V. 21.** *{Those stood, these stood}* God puts stands to the motion of the wights, and of the wheels, at his pleasure. See Josh. 10:12, 13. 1 Kings 12:24. Esay 37:3.

*{for the spirit}* So verse 20. here redoubled to make it more remarkable; it is a lively Spirit, 2 Corinthians 3:5, 6. Romans 8:2. it moves, and works in all creatures, with instincts and qualities according to their kinds. It has choice operations in the elect; and common operations in the rest of mankind.

**V. 22.** *{And the likeness of the firmament}* Not the firmament itself; all this was in vision. See Gen. 1:6. By this is meant Christ's celestial glory, who is king of the church; for above this firmament Christ sits in his throne, ver. 26. Hence to the end of the chapter, Ezekiel sees in this vision, the glory of Christ the Son of Man, or of God in Christ,

*{upon the heads}* Vers. 26, so this was between the Son of Man, the Lord Jesus Christ, and these wights, and wheels: and this likely did supply the office of that pair of wings, Esay 6:2. all creatures are under Christ, he sits above them, Phil. 2:9. 1 Cor. 15:27.

*{was as the Colour}* Heavenly things are pure and glorious, Psal. 19:1. Job 37:18.

*{terrible Crystal}* Translucent strangely: even terrible to astonishment through the strangeness, the greatness, and the gloriousness of it: such are the things above, dreadfully glorious: and what then is the dreadful glory of God himself? 1 Tim. 6:16. and all things below are visible to him, as in and through crystal, Heb. 4:13.

**V. 23.** *{And under the firmament were their wings straight the one towards the other}* The angels at a distance, attend Christ's throne, with wings stretched out, ready to fly in his service; as Mar. 5:9. and unanimously perform it with mutual sweet agreement. See ver. 6, 11. and the annotations upon it.

*{every one had two which covered}* More than four we may not admit, because of vers. 6. either those two wherewith they did fly, did serve also to cover their bodies, at leastwise, when they stood, and let them down, ver. 25. Or else, out of ver. 11. the joining of two wings, one of each cherub or wight to another, might be counted two on a side: and thus covering, they show their reverence, and subjection to Christ; as 1 Cor. 11:10. Psal. 89:7. And may serve as a pattern to man, either of decency and comeliness, or of modesty and humility, to veil and cover the glory of our parts, lest we should grow proud, or others fall to idolize us. See annotations on ver. 11.

**V. 24.** *{The noise of their wings}* By several similitudes much amplified.

*{of great waters}* See Rev. 1:15. Psal. 46:3.

*{as the voice of the Almighty}* Psal. 29:3–18. Apoc. 1:10. as thunder. Psal. 18:13. Joh. 18:6.

*{the voice of speech}* An articulate voice of dreadful sound and greatness. God's judgments have the voice of speech in them Micah 6:9. we should learn the language thereof, Esay 26:9. Hos. 14:3.

*{as the noise of an host}* Of armies of men and horses, in their armors moving, fightings, cries, with drums, trumpets, cannons, (as the case is now) all this may serve to shadow out the swiftness, and the dreadfulness of God's judgments, chap. 13:13. Psal. 119:120. Esay 29:6. And that, of dreadful force, and efficacy are the angels in their motions and actions; specially in the execution of God's judgments. Some refer all this to the noise of Nebuchadnezzar's armies. See Esay 5:27, 28, 29, 30.

*{they let down their wings}* They kept them quiet and without noise, signifying, that they had no power of themselves, nor ought to do, but only waited to execute God's commandments.

**V. 25.** *{A voice}* Or noise, or sound; as a forerunner to prepare Ezekiel the more to attend God's word which should be spoken unto him. So was John prepared to attendance, and hearing, Apoc. 1:10. & so Exod. 19:16.

*{from the firmament}* Not from the firmament itself, or from anything under it; but from besides, and above it; so the Hebrew bears; from Christ on the throne.

*{when they stood and had let down their wings}* And so were silent without that noise, ver. 24. that the voice might be heard, Job 4:16. Sedate, and silent minds and spirits, are fittest then to hear Christ's voice.

**V. 26.** *{and above}* The last and best part of the vision.

*{likeness}* Likeness, and appearance, thirteen times used in this and the two verses following. To show that all here was in vision only; lest we should otherwise mistake: and God thus as it were, mantles up his majesty; else there could not be such communion between him and us.

*{throne}* Psal. 9:7. Dan. 7:9. Heb. 1:8. Apoc. 4:2–7. and chap. 20:11, 12. Heb. 4:16. See 1 Kings 22:19. The throne here may signify the judiciary power, kingly dignity, triumphant glory, and divine majesty of Christ.

*{of a Sapphire stone}* A most glorious throne of an heavenly color; of a sky, or pleasant blue color, with a little purple in it, Exod. 24:10, see Solomon's throne, 1 Kings 10:18, 19. 2 Chr. 9:17. See Lam. 4:7. Apoc. 21:19. this adds to Christ's glory.

*{of a man}* Christ, θεάνθρωπος, God and man; to be man by his incarnation in the fullness of time: the King of the universe and head of the church, see ver. 28. & Dan. 9:13. Esay 6:1. with Joh. 12:41. Apoc. 1:13. & chap. 14:14.

*{above upon it}* Sitting, most likely.

**V. 27.** *{and I saw}* See chap. 8:2.

*{of amber}* See ver. 4. and the annotations upon it. Here is Christ of an intense fiery color, like to the most hot burning glowing coals, Apoc. 1:14, 15. & chap. 2:18. & 10:1. & 19:11, 12, 13. Thus Christ is presented in a fiery way, when acts of wrath and judgment are to be executed upon a people. To show that God and Christ is a consuming fire to his enemies, Deut. 4:24. Heb. 12:20. Mal. 3:2, 3. Jer. 51:25, 26. Apoc. 6:17.

*{round about within it}* A fire, it seems, in a fire, within the utmost fire, reflecting upon itself, turning within the concave of it, round about; as fire in a furnace. Two particular applications, and explications of these types, we may not so safely venture upon. Christ is most glorious, Mat. 17:2. Act. 26:13. and then take up that, Phil. 3:21.

**V. 28.** *{bow}* Of this bow, and the use of it, we read, Gen. 9:12–18. Esay 54:9. This bow then is a sign of mercy: it is a bow whose back is upwards, towards heaven; not shooting downwards towards us: a bow without arrows at all; a bow of fresh and lively delightful colors, to intimate God's mindfulness of his covenant of mercy to his, in the midst of his fiery wrath and judgments against his enemies; that he will ark and save his, when he rains down floods and storms upon other sinners, chap. 38:22. Psal. 11:6. Thus justice and mercy do compass his throne.

*{of the brightness round about}* Of that brightness in the end of the former verse; though some extend it to be round about the throne; or the head of him that sat upon the throne: according to those places, Apoc. 4:3. & chap. 10:1.

*{This was the appearance}* The sum of the vision, as we formerly said.

*{of the likeness}* To see God and his glory as it is in himself, is not for this life; Exod. 33:20. See Esay 6:2. but reserved for the life to come; 1 Cor. 13:12. 1 John 3:2. And even then though visionally, yet not comprehensively; for so no creature can ever see the glory of the Creator, 1 Tim. 6:16. or comprehend his infinite essence: and therefore there is no doubt but with this clause, these places, Gen. 32:30. Exod. 33:11. are to be understood. This here was but the appearance of the likeness of the Lord. And we see it in the volume of the creatures, Psal. 19:1. and in his church: and of his divine dispensation towards it, Psal. 29:9. & 63:2.

*{of the LORD}* *Jehovah*, Christ is *Jehovah*; who as God has his being of himself, and gives being to all things Jer. 23:6. Mal. 3:1.

*{I fell upon my face}* In adoration with fear and reverence; at the glorious presence of the divine majesty. So Gen. 17:3. Lev. 9:24. Dan. 8:17. & 10:8, 9. Rev. 1:17. and it was so usually with God's saints, Abraham, Moses, Manoah, Esay, Daniel, and others. Or else, astonished with amazement at the sight of God's majesty always terrible to sinners, though saints, through sense of their own frailty, weakness and sinfulness, during this mortal life, Esay 6:5. Mat. 17:6.

*{a voice of one}* It was the Lord's voice, but the prophet in his astonishment knew not whose voice it was. However, he was then best fitted to hear the voice, when he was thus humbled first at the sight of his vision.

## CHAPTER 2

**Verse 1.** *{And}* Ezekiel's vocation to his prophetical function, and instructions therein, are the things contained in this whole chapter, and continues to the 22 verse of the third chapter.

*{He said}* He, chap. 1:26. as appears in the three verses following.

*{Son of man}* So often, and very often is Ezekiel styled by God in this book, some reckon 80 times and upwards. So Daniel once, Dan. 8:17. else this style is appropriated to our Savior Christ, where it is spoken of one man, and not spoken of mankind in general: and so our Savior styles himself above four times. This compellation might serve to put the prophet in mind of himself what he was, even man, earth, and ashes; of his original, and end; baseness, brittleness, worthlessness in himself, Gen. 3:19. Psal. 9:20. and so to humble him, that he should not be puffed up with this heavenly glorious vision, and so his pride to blossom, 2 Cor. 12:1, 7. but magnifies God's mercy in thus honoring him, Psal. 8:3, 4. & 144:3. Job 7:17.

*{Stand upon thy feet}* Chap. 1:28. Arise, recollect yourself, be ready to attend and hear. So Dan. 10:11. Eph. 6:14. Act. 9:6. See Numb. 23:18. Judg. 3:20. thus God in mercy and compassion, raises and comforts him.

*{And I will speak unto thee}* That speech which after follows, v. 3. etc.

**V. 2.** *{And the Spirit entered}* Either the Spirit of God, see chap. 1:20. & 3:14. or his own spirit of life which seemed lost almost by his former fear and astonishment; as that, 1 Sam. 30:12. However, God's *Spirit* received his languishing *spirit;* and here we see that God with the command, and by it, gives power and performance: see Dan. 10:10. compare and couple God's commands with his promises, Deut. 10:16. ch. 6:5. with ch. 30:6. and Ezek. 18:31. with ch. 36:25, 26, 27. and Psal. 22:23. with Jer. 32:40.

*{entered into me}* By its operation and impletion: filling and fitting him for his office: see Act. 6:3. and ch. 9:17. & 11:24. & 13:2. The use and necessity of the *spirit*, 1 Cor. 2:14, 12. The word is not sufficient without it. But the word is the chariot of the *spirit*, as here. See 1 Thess. 1:5. 2 Cor. 3:6, 8. Gal. 3:2.

**V. 3.** *{I send thee}* I make you my legate and ambassador; mission, and ordination is requisite, Rom. 10:15. False prophets run before they be sent, Jer. 14:14. and ch. 23:21. and ch. 29:9. and it is Christ's prerogative to send prophets and ministers, Mat. 28:18, 19. Rom. 1:5. 1 Cor. 4:12. 2 Cor. 3:6. Ephes. 4:11. Act. 20:28.

*{to the children of Israel}* Evil branches of a good stock, Rom. 9:6. These were not praying and prevailing children, as their father was, Apoc. 2:9. Rom. 2:28, 29. Grace comes not by inheritance, Joh. 8:44.

*{to a rebellious}* Sin is *rebellion*, 1 Sam. 15:15. and chap. 15:23. and Act. 5:39. Luk. 19:14. let us do, as it is, Josh. 1:18. And herein is God's mercy seen, in sending to such a rebellious people, 2 Chr. 36:15, 16. Jer. 25:3. and chap. 35:15.

*{nation}* Heb. *nations.* The prophet prophesied against sundry *Heathen Nations:* yet here are meant the children of Israel; as is expressed: called *Nations* or *Gentiles* here, because they sinned as the *Heathen Nations* did: and so in reproach for their sins, they are called elsewhere by reproachful names, Esay 1:10. and 57:3. Jer. 7:27–35.

*{they and their fathers}* Judg. 2:19. Jer. 7:20. Authority and example of fathers pleaded, Joh. 4:20. Jer. 44:17. but is no sound rule of worship, or life, Ezek. 20:18, 19. 1 Pet. 1:18.

*{against me}* John 22:16. Esay 1:2.

*{even unto this very day}* Esay 48:8. Jer. 3:25. & chap. 32:31. Ezek. 20:31. affliction and captivity changed them not. Esay 1:5.

**V. 4.** *{Impudent}* Heb. *hard of face;* that is to say, such as are not ashamed of anything. A great progress, and height in sin, Jer. 6:15. and 8:12. & 3:3. and c. 3. Zeph. 3:5. Zech. 7:12. Esay 48:4. Phil. 3:19. Psal. 52:1. *Repentance* recalls *shame*, Rom. 6:21.

*{stiffhearted}* Act. 7:51. when judgments, mercies, miracles, will not move and mollify them, Exod. 32:9. Deut. 9:6. Esay 48:4.

*{thus saith}* The minister is God's mouth, Jer. 15:19. and 1:9.

**V. 5.** *{Whether}* Ver. 7. Chap. 3:11, 27. Jer. 26:2, 3. Matth. 21:37. God's primary intention in sending his prophets, is for the good of his people. In a second place it is for to leave the wicked inexcusable, and to justify his proceeding towards them. See Esay 55:11. 2 Cor. 2:15, 16. Joh. 9:39. That place of Esay, chap. 6:9, 10. is six times alleged and repeated in the New Testament.

*{or whether they will forbear}* Namely, to hear and to obey you from me: yet must the prophet do his duty, and refer the issue and success to God, Esay 49:4, 5. yea, he must deal roundly, as God himself here does, and put such vile sinners to it. See Mat. 10:14. Mar. 6:11.

*{for they are a rebellious house}* So charged with *rebellion* against God and his commandments very often; *obstinate sinners*, with an high hand; whose hearts were fully set in them to do evil, Eccl. 8:11. evil things, as they could, Jer. 3:5. even with greediness, Eph. 4:19. mighty sinners, as those, Amos 5:12. sons of Belial, filled with maliciousness, madness, as Luk. 6:11. provoking most bitterly, Hos. 12:14. as those, Deut. 32:32. even the worse for the means; in doing, they will do their idol-service, Jer. 44:17. and thus most do, Apoc. 13:3. 1 Cor. 1:22. Esay 53:1. few hear the word obediently, Mat. 7:13, 14. And thus the Lord tells the prophet aforehand, what he was to expect, that he might not stumble at it, nor despond, when he should come to it, and find it so.

*{yet shall know, &c.}* So chap. 33:33. and so be convinced of their rebellion, and obstinacy, for not taking warning by him, and so left without excuse, Esay 5:3, 4. and so, it shall be easier for Sodom in the day of judgment, Matth. 12:24. what languages will their consciences speak in that day, or on their deathbeds? In the end you shall know it, Jer. 30:24. See Jer. 5:31. and 16:21. and 30:24. The prophets shall witness it against them, to their further condemnation, 2 Cor. 2:15, 16.

**V. 6.** *{Be not afraid}* Four times in this verse. Stand it out, show yourself a man, a man of courage, a man of God. So chap. 3:9, 10. Jer. 1:8, 17. He shows thereby our aptness to fear: and yet, that for no fears or afflictions, prophets should forbear to do their duties. See Psal. 27:1. and 23:4. and 56:3, 4. No cause for the godly to fear; but the wicked justly may. 1 Sam. 8:12. Prov. 28:1. Further helps against fear; specially in magistrates, and ministers. See Neh. 6:11. Hagg. 2:4. Nor faint and flag, God is with them, Joh. 1:9. Jer. 1:8. Psal. 118:6. 2 Tim. 1:7. else God will confound us Jer. 1:17. Luk. 12:4, 5. Esay 8:13. The just live by faith, and will not die by fear, 2 Chron. 32:7, 8. Heb. 11:27. Prov. 29:25. The more holiness, the less fear: even Herod feared John, and Saul feared David: let us not value our life too much, Act. 21:13. Apocal. 12:11.

*{briars}* *Pricking briar*, Eze. 28:24. Mic. 7:4. See Jotham's parable, Judg. 9:14, 15. 1 Sam. 24:13. as Josh. 23:13. Ungodly men are like thorns, Nah. 1:10. worthless, fit to be burnt, Dan. 11:21. Ps. 58:9. Esa. 10:17. Job 30:8. pricking, grieving things, Num. 33:55. Thus, in their words and calumniations, provocations, scoffs and scorns, reproachings, threatenings, sleightings. Thus, in their looks; of kin to that nation of fierce countenances, Deut. 28:50. Thus, in their catching scratching nature.

*{Scorpions}* A thorny weed growing like *scorpions*, whereof instruments are made to scourge malefactors, 1 Kings 12:11, 14. also, a venomous stinging creature, Deut. 8:15. The *scorpion* is cunning, vigilant, torments, and kills, Apoc. 9:3, 5, 10. Here is the nature of the wicked, Mic. 7:2. Amos 2:7. and here is the lot of the righteous to dwell among the wicked. Lot in Sodom. David in Meshech and Kedar, Psal. 120:5, 6. Cant. 2:2. a lily among thorns, Apoc. 2:13. we had need to walk warily, to keep ourselves from their scratchings, stingings: beware of men, Matth. 10:17. seek not acquaintance of them, Mic. 7:4. Do men gather grapes of thorns? Mat. 7:16. and, as here, so specially is it the lot of prophets oftentimes, and of God's *ministers*, 1 Cor. 4:13. and 15:32. Heb. 11:36, 37. Luk. 10:3. They must not think it strange: but prepare for it: when these *scorpions* sting us, we must look up to the *brazen serpent.*

**V. 7.** *{My words}* Not the words of any other, Jer. 23:30, 31, 32. Num. 22:58. Matth. 22:20. 1 Cor. 11:23.

*{whether, &c.}* See ver. 5.

*{most rebellious}* Heb. *rebellion,* in the abstract: to note the height of their sinning, see annotations on ver. 5.

**V. 8.** *{Be not thou rebellious,}* Amos 3:8. They shall come unto you, not you to them. God looks for more at a prophet's hand. We must not follow the example of wicked men; though the most and the greatest. We must not write after their copy, trace their steps. Examples prevail much, *Magnates, magnetes.* Peter among the Jews would Judaize: but it must not be so with us.

*{what I say}* Not what others say; be they what they will be.

*{open thy mouth}* Prepare and dispose yourself for it, Psal. 119:131. and 81:10.

*{and eat that I give thee}* Apoc. 10:9. As he enjoins him the duty of his prophetical function, so he gives him the means and furniture for it. He gives seed to his sowers. He furnishes them with abilities. If they want books, he will provide them, Exod. 4:11, 12. Mal. 10:19. And ministers must digest first, themselves, what they deliver to the people after.

**V. 9.** *{an hand}* See Da. 5:5, 24. God's creative and infinite power.

*{a roll of a book}* Namely, a book, or a volume, wrapped and rolled up, according to the ancient fashion, Isa. 8:1. Jer. 36:2. Psal. 40:8. This a fit symbol of the gift of prophecy, hereby, and herewith bestowed on him by that great prophet of his church.

**V. 10.** *{and he spread it before me}* Expounded, and revealed it, opened it, and made it known unto me, Luk. 24:27. Apoc. 1:1. and 5:5. and 3:7. Esay 22:22. Mat. 11:25, 27. unrolled it, Joh. 15:15.

*{within and without}* Heb. *in the face and in the back:* everywhere, on all sides, full; containing likely the sum and arguments, of almost all the prophecies following; the people's sins and punishments.

*{lamentations, &c.}* Chap. 27:32. and chap. 32:2, 16. dreadful, and woeful things hang over the head of a sinful nation: which ministers must not refuse to declare.

*{mourning}* Mourning songs, and elegies.

*{and woe}* Lam. 5:16. Matth. 23.

## CHAPTER 3

**Verse 1.** *{He said unto me}* Namely, he that had spoken to him in the precedent chapter; he which sat upon the throne and spread the book before him.

*{Eat that}* All this was done in vision: and it may serve to represent the deep impression of the word of God, in the heart of his *ministers*, who must apprehend it by faith, and digest it by meditation; for to utter it, not as a mere narration, but as a quick and lively doctrine.

*{that thou findest}* Not some part of it, Rom. 15:4. 2 Tim. 3:16.

*{Findest}* Namely that I give you, whatsoever it be, sweet or bitter, hard or soft, pleasing or displeasing: and it may serve to signify that *ministers* ought to follow, their vocation, simply and readily, without contradiction or doubt whatsoever. And the obedience of all unto Christ, must be absolute, in all and everything, without disputing the case, without consulting with flesh and blood. A kind of blind obedience is here best. So Genes. 22:3. Luke 5:5. Mark 1:18. Joh. 15:14.

*{Eat this roll}* Rev. 10:9. The meaning here is that expressed, ver. 10. 1 Tim. 4:13, 15.

*{and go}* Then are *ministers* fit to go and preach, and feed others, when they have first eaten the roll, and fed themselves, Matth. 5:19.

*{unto the house of Israel}* Them of the captivity in Babylon.

**Ver. 2.** *{So I opened my mouth, and he, &c.}* Man's endeavor, 2 Tim. 1:6. Esay 64:7. and God the chief agent, Jam. 1:17. Psal. 51:15. 1 Cor. 3:6. and 12:6. and 15:10. Joh. 15:5. Phil. 2:13.

**V. 3.** *{Cause thy belly to eat}* Concoct it, digest it, retain it, draw out all the virtue of it, live and act in the strength of it. And thus should we deal with the roll of God's book, and word, eat and take in all the truths of it; and not as weakly and sickly stomachs do, cast it up, and get no benefit by it; but eat it by faith, and digest it by meditation, turn it to our spiritual nutriment, feel the power and efficacy of it in our heart, strengthening the inner man, and act accordingly. Other books and rolls we may look into, but this we must eat.

*{and fill, &c.}* The inwards of the soul are to be filled and fed with the word and will of God, Psal. 1:2. and that plentifully, Col. 3:16. It is not enough then to hear, and know it.

*{Then did I eat it}* Jer. 15:16. Apoc. 10:10. that is, in this vision, I seemed to eat the roll, in my own imagination: and yet afterwards he seems, through human frailty, somewhat to decline his vocation, ver. 14, 15.

*{It was in my mouth}* God's word, and book of Scriptures, is sweeter than honey, Psal. 19:10. and 119:72, 103. Though sinful men; as sick men, have lost their taste. And the office of a prophet seemed at first pleasant to him, by reason of the excellency of the revelations, the communication with God; the profit that might thence come to the church, and the honor which God did then do him, by this seal of his grace; but the bitterness did follow afterwards, ver. 14. Apoc. 10:9. Jer. 15:16, 17, 18.

**V. 4.** *{with my words}* Chap. 2:7. not yours, not the words of others, 1 Cor. 11:23. 1 Joh. 1:3. Jer. 23:26, 28, 22.

**V. 5.** *{for thou}* That is, I will not employ you in preaching to strange and barbarous nations, as Jonah, and others; but you shall preach only to your own nation, your friends and familiars, which should be a pleasure and comfort unto you, and should strengthen you in zeal and virtue. Thus God appoints where, and to whom, Act. 16:28.

**V. 5.** *{Of a strange speech, and of a hard language}* Heb. *deep of lip, and heavy of language.* See Esay 33:19.

*{of a strange speech}* Heb. *deep of lips, and heavy of tongue.*

*{surely had I sent thee}* Or *if I had sent thee, &c.* Would they not have hearkened unto you? or albeit I had not sent you (but you had gone of your own accord) yet they would have hearkened unto you.

*{They would have hearkened unto thee}* Sooner than the house of Israel, they of the captivity, Matth. 11:21, 23. and chap. 12:41. the unsearchable depth of God's wisdom, and ways, and judgments! In denying the means of grace to those that would embrace them, or better use them, and in giving them to those that will refuse them.

**V. 7.** *{will not}* So Joh. 5:40. Luk. 19:14. and chap. 13:34. will not do what they are able to do: our will and willfulness charged with the fault, Hos. 13:9.

*{unto thee}* Jer. 7:27.

*{for they will not hearken unto me}* See 1 Sam. 8:7. Joh. 15:20. Those that do not hearken to the word of God in the Scriptures, do not hearken unto God.

*{impudent and hard-hearted}* Heb. *stiff of fore-head, and hard of heart.* No more moved or mollified, than a rock with the waves of the sea. See chap. 2:4. Esay 46:12. Psal. 95:8. Rom. 2:5. Zach. 7:12.

**V. 8.** *{Strong against}* God promises his assistance to his ministers, and that he will give them boldness and constancy, in their vocation, Isa. 50:7. Jer. 1:18. Mic. 3:8. Esay 50:7.

**V. 9.** *{And as an adamant}* Jer. 15:20.

*{fear them not}* Chap. 2:6. See annotations upon it.

**V. 10.** *{Moreover}* A summary repetition and injunction of his foresaid calling and function, in this, and the next verse.

*{All my words}* Not some of them, Psal. 119:6.

*{Receive in thine heart}* He shows what is meant by the eating of the book; which is, that the ministers of God may speak nothing, as of themselves, but that only which they have received of the Lord, and lodged in their heart: and in their heart likewise people must receive God's word, Deut. 6:6. Psal. 119:11. and Psal. 40:8. as Prov. 6:20, 21, 22. Luk. 2:19. and chap. 8:15.

**V. 11.** *{To them of the Captivity,}* That were carried captive with Jechonias. These were not so bad as those yet remaining in Jerusalem and Judea, under Zedekiah. Jer. 24:5, 8. yet they were bad enough in their present affliction, ver. 6. of this chapter; and happily grudged at their present state and condition, that yet remained in the land of Jewry. And therefore far sorer judgments are denounced in this prophecy against Jerusalem and the inhabitants of Jewry, than against these mentioned in the text.

*{unto thy people}* As Deut. 9:12. as if God some way cast them off: and in the same dialect he speaks, *Your new moons, &c.* Esay 1:14. Mal. 2:3.

*{whether they will hear},* Chap. 2:5.

**V. 12.** *{The Spirit}* Not the wind, or the vigor of his own *spirit*, as some would have it; but God's *Spirit*, chap. 1:12, 20. called also the hand of the Lord, chap. 1:3. and in the 14. and 22. verses of this chapter, that *Spirit*, Mat. 4:1.

*{Took me up}* Lifted him up higher, that he might at less distance, and more clearly view and behold the glory of the Lord, the glory of the Son of Man.

*{Of a great rushing,}* Act. 2:2.

*{Blessed}* Most likely, the word of that voice afore-mentioned: and the words not of thunder, as that supposed, Job 12:29. but of the *living wights* the angels. It is their work and office to praise God, Apoc. 5:10, 11. Esay 6:3. Luk. 2:13, 14. Job 38:4, 7. Psal. 148:2.

*{from his place}* Sitting on his heavenly throne of glory, chap. 1:26. See Mic. 1:3. Some understand this of the Lord's removing from his temple, and forsaking it. And the voice or noise of a great rushing, to be like as when great princes do remove their dwellings. And histories do record the like to have fallen out, when the Romans afterwards burnt the temple, Josephus 6:7. chap. 12. of the War of the Jews. Apollonius 6:1. Corn. Tacitus 6:21. of his Annals: whence it may be the Gentiles had that opinion, *That their gods forsook their Cities, before they were taken and destroyed by enemies;* as by many instances of theirs might be showed. But this sense seems rather to be put upon this text, than naturally to arise out of it. The plainer sense is to take this for a doxology, or praising of God; yea even when he is in a way of judgment, he is to be blessed and glorified.

**V. 13.** *{The noise of the wings, and of the wheels,}* The noise of the celestial and terrestrial creatures, in way of acclamation and applause at the glory of the Lord.

*{touched}* Heb. *kissed.*

*{one another}* Heb. *woman, to her sister:* noting the angels' harmony, and loving unity, in praising God, in executing their offices; they have like affections, as a woman to her sister.

**V. 14.** *{And took me away}* Ver. 12. after the former sight, and applause; took him away and corporally translated him from the place where he had seen the vision, to the chief residence of his countrymen, which were captives in Babylon, vers. 14, 15. A thing which did often befall the prophets, 1 Kings 18:12. 2 Kings 2:16. Act. 8:39, 40.

*{And I went in bitterness}* After I had well, thought upon the subject of my prophesies, and of my commission; finding them full of miseries for my nation, and of dangers and troubles for myself, Jer. 20:9.

*{heat}* Heb. *hot anger,* the prophet's weakness and infirmity; yea froward humor, and distemper, to be in a chafe and indignation of *spirit*, upon so divine a call to this office, and so gracious a promise of assistance in it: so it was with Jonas. The best have their failings. No perfection here. And the prophet here conceals not his own fault; as not being guided by his own *pen*, or *spirit*, in writing Scriptures, 1 Pet. 1:20, 21.

*{The hand of the LORD}* That is to say, the power of God's *Spirit* in me overpowered my reluctancy, overcame all these fleshly apprehensions, Isa. 8:11. and comforted me, Jer. 1:7, 9. and 39:3. Act. 4:20. God's *Spirit* offers not violence to the wills of men, yet works invincibly; he inclines them to yield *omnipotenti suavitate, & suavi omnipotentia.* Some here make the meaning to be, that he went in bitterness, and hot anger of *spirit*, because the hand of the Lord was strong upon him to force him, and overpower his will, to this bitter and woeful message and office.

**V. 15.** *{Then I came}* In that fourth month, chap. 1:vers. 1.

*{at Tel-abib}* Which was a place of Mesopotamia, where some, and happily the most of the captive Jews were placed.

*{that dwelt by the river of Chebar}* See chap. 1:1, 3. the ten tribes were of the river of Gozan, 2 Kings 18:11. And so this place here likely not far from the place of the vision, chap. 1:1, 3.

*{And I sat}* Sadly, no doubt, as Lam. 1:1. and 2:10. And concealing my calling, and idly forbearing and foregoing my function enjoined me.

*{astonished}* At the woefulness of the roll given me in charge; as the matter of my commission, and argument of my prophesies to my own countrymen.

*{seven days}* Mourning for his people in astonishment, as Job's three friends did, Job 2:13. There might be other ends and uses also of this strange behavior.

**V. 16.** *{At the end of seven days}* God bears with the weaknesses of his servants, and leaves them not long, though they be in distempers and so overpowers them: that their will and weakness shall not hinder the efficacy and execution of his decree.

**V. 17.** *{Son of Man}* Chap. 2:1.

*{I have made thee}* God makes him so. Heb. 5:4. and because of his former tergiversation, his vocation, and prophetical function, is here again renewed, and reinforced.

*{a watchman}* A prophet. This shows the church's danger, and the minister's duty: so chap. 33:2, 7. Esay 52:8. and 56:10. Jer. 6:17. Heb. 13:17. See Esay 21:6, 8. carefully to watch, and to warn, to foresee, and foretell dangers. This is the watchman's duty: as Gen. 31:40. not to be blind watchmen, dumb dogs: given to sleep and slumber, Esay 56:10.

*{unto the house of Israel}* Watchmen are not for themselves, but for the flock, 2 Cor. 12:14, 15. and 11:28. Too many do otherwise, Phil. 2:21.

*{hear}* They must hear before they speak, learn before they teach, Hab. 2:1.

*{warning}* Vers. 17, 18, 19, 20, 21. Chap. 33:1–10. Esay 58:1.

*{From me}* From my mouth. The watchman's word and warning to the people, must be from the Lord, and from him only: and the warning must be given, not in his own name, but in the name of Christ, by and with his authority, and so ought to be received, 2 Cor. 5:20. As Christ did come, and work in his Father's name, Joh. 5:43. and 10:25.

**V. 18.** *{when I say,}* When I shall have given you commission to say so to them from me, and this according to the warrant of my word, and dictate of my *Spirit.*

*{surely die}* In dying you shall die: the like phrase in the original, is 1 Sam. 26:25. and usually in the Hebrew tongue, die temporally, and eternally.

*{and thou givest him not warning, nor speakest to warn}* He must be solicitous, and frequent in warning, Act. 20:31. Phil. 3:18.

*{to save his life}* Manasseh that exceeding sinner did so, 2 Kings 21:11. 2 Chron. 33:12, 13. And this is the end and scope of the *ministry*, 1 Tim. 4:16. Jam. 5:20. 1 Cor. 3:9.

*{die in his iniquity}* Ignorance, or not warning is not sufficient plea for wicked men.

*{but his blood will I require}* As Gen. 9:5. Ezek. 33:6. 2 Sam. 4:11. Act. 20:26. 1 Cor. 9:16.

*{at thine hand}* So ver. 20. The Lord applies it to Ezekiel, that he may take to heart his duty, and his danger. These words seem not so much words, as thunderbolts: and what then will become of seducing *ministers?* 2 Pet. 2:1.

**V. 19.** *{Thou hast delivered thy soul}* Doing your duty, and what lies in you. So ver. 21. Act. 20:26. and 18:5, 6.

**V. 20.** *{when a righteous man}* Chap. 18:24. righteous seemingly, in their own eyes, or in the eyes of others: outwardly, though inwardly hypocrites, Luk. 18:9. Mat. 23:28. children of the kingdom eternally, Matth. 8:12. and 5:20. The truly righteous turn not thus, 1 Pet. 1:5. 2 Pet. 1:10, 11.

*{commit iniquity}* Joh. 8:34. 1 Joh. 3:8.

*{and I lay a stumbling block before him}* Do by my just and provident administration turn against him his own *iniquity;* whereby he shall fall as by a stumbling block: or lay that before him, which he will make a stumbling block to himself. God himself, and Christ is a stumbling block unto them, Esay 8:14. 1 Pet. 2:8. 1 Cor. 1:23. Psal. 94:23. and 64:8. Not infusing any corruption into him, but for his former sin, leaving him to himself, delivering him up to his own vile lusts, giving Satan leave to tempt and seduce him, and keeping back my assistance, and withdrawing my grace; and thus in justice, punishing one sin with the occasion or commission of another, Rom. 1:24, 26, 28. 2 Sam. 16:21, 22. 1 Kings 22:22. 2 Thess. 2:11. The sun is without fault that draws with his beams, as well stink out of a carcass, as a sweet smell out of a rose. The cause is from within; only the occasion is from without.

*{Righteousness which he hath done}* Heb. *Righteousnesses:* materially for the substance of the outward works; and so righteous in men's eyes, though not formally righteous, as not done in faith.

*{shall not be remembered}* Chap. 18:24. Nay more, it shall make against him, 2 Pet. 2:21.

**V. 22.** *{and the hand}* As chap. 1:3. and chap. 3:14.

*{There}* At Tel-abib, ver. 15. at the end of seven days, ver. 16.

*{Into the plain}* Or, *Valley by Tel-abib:* a place more free from concourse and disturbance, and more fit for divine contemplations and visions, which were there showed and vouchsafed to him: the more separate from the world, the more free communion with God, when the mind is serene and contemplative; then the Lord opened himself unto him.

**V. 23.** *{By the river of Chebar.}* The same vision, the second time; suiting to the argument of his prophesy.

*{and I fell on my face}* As chap. 1:28. It affected him the second time, no less than at the first: which further proves the majesty and terror of it; and man's weakness and sinfulness, Esay 6:5.

**V. 24.** *{Then the Spirit}* The *Spirit* acted in our Savior, Esay 61:1. In *Stephen*, Act. 5:10. in *Apostles*, Act. 1:4. in *Cornelius*, Act. 10:44. in all the godly, 1 Cor. 6:11.

*{and set me upon my feet}* As chap. 2:2. God soon visits, revives, strengthens, comforts with his Spirit those that are thus humbled.

*{Go shut thyself}* Spoken by way of irony, or rather a plain injunction to him, from the Lord, so to do. It may be there to receive further instructions from the Lord.

**V. 25.** *{They shall put bonds upon thee}* This not in vision only, and imagination; but really that you come not forth to prophesy wrath and woes against them: or possibly seeing his behavior, ver. 15. It may be out of such a conceit as that in Mar. 3:21. and so done in his own house by them of his own household, and his friends, for his credit's sake, as they thought. Some conceive, as if this should emblematically set forth their shutting up in a siege; and the bonds of their captivity, Jer. 40:1. However, bands, and bad usage, betide the *prophets* and *ministers* of God, Jer. 1:19. Act. 9:16. and 12:8. Matth. 24:9. Joh. 16:2, 4. 1 Kings 22:27. Jer. 37:15. and 32:2. and 38:6, 7. 2 Cor. 11:23, 24. 1 Kings 18:4.

**V. 26.** *{And I will make thy tongue}* Dumb, either not able to speak; as Luk. 1:20. or dumb, by the restraint of my prohibition. I will suffer it for a time. You shall be as a man tongueless, and wordless; as a man whose tongue cleaves, etc. so to revenge their rebellion and ingratitude. And heavy judgment of God upon a people, Amos 8:9, 10, 11. and 5:13. The contrary is promised as a blessing, chap. 24:27. Esay 30:20, 21.

*{a reprover}* Heb. *a man reproving.* This while he was dumb.

*{for they are a rebellious house}* Their rebellion then, and impudency, and hardness of heart, in sinning against God; and not the prophets' fear of them, was the cause of his silence, and silencing.

**V. 27.** *{But when I speak with thee}* When it shall seem good to me so to do, not unlikely but after other seven days, or very soon after compare, chap. 1:1, 2. and chap. 4:5. with chap. 8:1. And therefore this time is not to be prolonged to some number of years, as some do.

*{I will open thy mouth}* To speak my word unto them, and to pronounce my words against them for their sins. See ch. 24:7. & c. 33:22.

*{He that heareth}* You shall do your office and duty; do they what they will, chap. 2:5. and 3:11. Apoc. 22:11. God will try men, whether they be curable, or no.

## CHAPTER 4

**Verse 1.** *{Thou also}* The sum of this chapter is the siege of Jerusalem, and the famine in it; foretold and foreshowed in types here; about four years before the same began.

*{Son of man}* When you shall go forth among them, you shall thus do, and to this effect; speak to them from me; and you shall use no other kind of preaching or prophesying to them, during all this time, but this following, ver. 7. or thus shall you prophesy against it. Some take this to be done in the time of his silence, chap. 3:26.

*{Take thee a tile}* Here begins as it were the reading of that roll, chap. 2:10. a preaching of it, not to the ear, but to the eye in a more moving way.

*{Tile}* Some doubt, whether unburnt, or burnt in a kiln. Others say the Hebrew word will bear a table, or tablet, smooth, and square, and somewhat large, and so fitter for the prophet's pencil. See Esay 8:1.

*{Jerusalem}* They then in the captivity, had no cause to grudge themselves, and murmur, or to envy them and their estate that were in Jerusalem. Of this city, see Jer. 32:31, 32. and chap. 26:6. Zeph. 3:1–5.

**V. 2.** *{lay siege}* Draw a design of a very strict siege.

*{and build a Fort}* Forts, and fortifications, towers, trenches, engines, portray these things upon the tile round about the portraiture of the city; make a kind of map of all; as Joshua 18:4, 9. by pencil rather than engraving. See 2 Kings 25:1.

*{camp}* Of the Chaldean armies.

*{battering rams}* (Or, chief leaders:) namely, to batter the walls, according to the ancient manner.

**V. 3.** *{an iron pan}* Or, a flat plate; it may be, to show the hardness of the siege, through God's inexorable anger towards them; and that all endeavors and resistance that they of Jerusalem could make against the besieging Chaldeans, should be to no purpose: and that it should be impossible for any relief to come and deliver them.

*{for a wall}* In resemblance of the wall of the city; though most interpreters construe this wall of iron sundry other meanings.

*{set thy face against it}* As Lev. 17:10. Jer. 21:10.

*{and thou shalt lay siege against it}* You shall act the part of a chief commander of an army, which does straitly besiege a place, Jer. 1:10. Thus he writes as it were in hieroglyphics, and preaches in emblems; and this is not unusual with the prophets. So Esay 20:2. Jer. 13. and 16. & 19. & 27. chapters; and Zech. 11. and none more uses it than Ezekiel. Types and figures make truth more evident, and efficacious.

*{To the house of Israel}* To those now in captivity; and to those yet remaining in Judah, under the reign of Zedekiah, which would not be persuaded that Jerusalem should be besieged: and to those also of the ten tribes scattered among them.

**V. 4.** *{lie thou also.}* Not sleep; as the vulgar Latin has it. All this was done in a prophetical vision and ecstasy, say many; made known from God to the people. And many such things are in the prophets; as Hos. 1:2. and chap. 3:1. And otherwise as they conceive, we should here forge a monstrous thing, or a miracle: yet others take it not visionally, but literally; as that, chap. 12:3, 4, 5, 6. Esa. 20:2. as conceiving it of little, or no use to the people, if done in an intellectual vision only, and the prophet's bare imagination. And besides their qualifying the hardness of it by their expositions; they suppose that God who gave the command, did minister also extraordinary strength, and constancy for the performance of it. And they understand this lying, seconded by that, ver. 8. yet not to be so strict and absolute, but that he arose sometimes to dress his meat, and fetch his drink, ver. 9, 10, 11. and for other necessaries: but that his far greatest posture was lying; and his sleep constantly on his left side, during this number of days.

*{upon the left side}* Happily, because Samaria and the kingdom of Israel, the ten tribes, lay on the left hand in relation to Jerusalem, and the kingdom of Judah; as left hand, and right hand are taken in Scripture; supposing the posture of the face toward the east. So chap. 16:46. and 47:1, 2. Exod. 26:18. in the Hebrew.

*{and lay}* As a type of God, in long patience bearing their iniquity, and the burden of it, though troublesome to him; as it would be to a man to lie long upon one side: yet so besieging them, as it were, and provoking them to a yielding and surrendering by repentance. But now at last ready to punish them fully.

*{the iniquity}* Guilt, and punishment.

*{of the house of Israel}* Of the ten tribes.

*{according to the number}* As Numb. 14:34.

**V. 5.** *{three hundred and ninety days}* A year and about a month, and between chap. 1:1, 2. and chap. 8:1. are a year and two months. Some begin these years from the seven and twentieth of the reign of King Solomon, when he and all Israel with him, fell away from God to idolatry: and end them in the fifth year of Zedekiah's captivity, Jer. 52:30. when Nebuzaradan carried away captive seven hundred forty five persons of the Jews; having burnt the temple and city four years before, Jer. 52:12, 13, 30. And this seems best to suit with the truest chronology of the times of the reigns of the kings of Judah, the proof whereof, they expound and apply that chapter 5:3, 4. Others begin them from the fourth year of Rehoboam, when he and Judah forsook the law of the Lord, 2 Chron. 11:17. and 12:1, 2. Others more likely than so, from the division of the kingdoms in the first year of Rehoboam and Jeroboam: and both end them in the eleventh and last year of Zedekiah: and both labor to make their chronologies of the kings of Judah to agree with this number. However for the main matter, the difference in all is not great. And withal, it is not unlikely, that the siege of Jerusalem, by the Chaldeans, continued three hundred and ninety days; by comparing the beginning and end of the siege, 2 Kings 25:1, 2, 3, 4. and Jer. 39:1, 2. and 52:4, 5, 6, 7. and considering therein the time that the Chaldeans left the siege, upon the coming up of Pharaoh's army out of Egypt, Jer. 37:5, 7, 8, 11. which may be gathered to be about five months spent upon that expedition. But these rabbis of the Jews that would understand this siege of the siege of Jerusalem, by Vespasian and Titus; and assign the three hundred and ninety days to the time of that siege; and the forty days to the time between our Savior's death and that siege; do seem therein willfully to blind themselves. However we see here God's great patience, and his long suffering of sins and sinners: and yet at last he does visit and punish.

*{so shalt thou}* Numb. 14:34.

*{of the house of Israel}* Though the body of them were carried captive into Assyria by Shalmaneser long before, in the sixth year of Hezekiah; yet divers of them remained scattered in the land, 2 Chr. 30:6. & 31:1. & 34:6, 7, 9, 33. and many of them joined to Judah, soon upon, and shortly after the first rent and separation by Jeroboam, 2 Chr. 11:16, 17. and 15:9. and continued so.

**V. 6.** *{And when thou hast accomplished}* Art about to accomplish them, that is, after three hundred and fifty, when you are within forty of the last, as may appear by comparing the ninth verse with the fifth, and this; and is clearly evinced by chap. 1:ver. 1, 2. chap. 3:15, 24, 25, 26, 27. and chap. 8:1. compared together.

*{of the house of Judah}* Whose sins have been in part confused and mixed with the sins of the other ten tribes.

*{forty days}* Some take these years out of the first forty of the wicked reign of Manasseh. Others, begin these years from the eighteenth year of Josiah; when though the covenant with God was renewed, yet the people continued still in all their rebellions and provocations, Jer. 25:3, 4, 7. 2 Kings 23:26. and they end them in the fifth year after Zedekiah's captivity; in that last captivity by Nebuzaradan. Others, more likely, begin them from the thirteenth year of Josiah, and end them in the eleventh of Zedekiah, which make forty years, and are the bounds of Jeremy's prophesy, Jer. chap. 1:2, 3. This difference is not great.

*{each day for a year}* Heb. *a day for a year, a day for a year.*

**V. 7.** *{Set thy face}* Do the actions of a commander, who is very busy about some siege: harden your face, be stern, as one inexorable; showing, that such should be their besiegers, the Chaldeans.
