*{arm shall be uncovered}* Isa. 52:10. Stripped, and stretched out, ready for service, and active; not put up in his bosom. Thus the prophet personates the Chaldeans in the siege.

*{shalt prophesy against it}* By these signs, with words and expressions suitable joined thereunto.

**V. 8.** *{Lay bands}* By the authority of my word, and power of my Spirit; not as those bonds, chap. 3:25. showing thereby, the firmness of this divine decree.

*{From one side}* Heb. *from thy side to thy side.*

**V. 9.** *{Take thou}* A figure of the extreme dearth in Jerusalem during the siege, signified by the coarse miscellany bread, made of divers sorts of the worst grain, and by the small weight and quantity of it. See their complaints of this, Lam. 1:11, 19. and 2:11, 12, 19, 20. and 4:4, 9, 10. and 5:6, 9, 10.

*{and fitches}* Or, *spelt.*

*{bread thereof}* A type of the sore famine they should endure in the siege. Many sieges yield example of terrible famine; as Samaria, 2 Kings 6:25, 28. and others in human stories.

*{Three hundred and ninety days shalt thou eat it}* Therefore the forty days, ver. 6. are included in the three hundred and ninety. And he slept not all the time, as some Papists imagine.

**V. 10.** *{twenty shekels a day}* There are mentioned in Scripture shekels of gold, of silver, of brass, of iron. We read also of the shekel of the sanctuary, or the holy shekel. Hence some gather a common shekel, to be contradistinguished to this, and make it less by the half. This to be half an ounce, or two shillings sixpence; the common, a quarter, or one shilling threepence. And so the weight of twenty shekels here, to be five ounces, or at most ten ounces. Which yet is scant enough to preserve life. Some conceive the common and scattered shekel to be all one, consisting of twenty gerahs, Exod. 30:13. Lev. 27:25. Numb. 3:47. Ezek. 45:12. And the gerah, weighing sixteen barley corns.

**V. 11.** *{water by measure}* Whereof there was also great scarcity during the siege; the springs without the city being possessed by the enemies. See Esay 30:20. Lament. 4:4. and ch. 5:4.

*{the sixth part of an Hin}* About as much as twelve hens' eggshells will hold, that is, about a pint; or one of those measures, called logs, Lev. 14:10. whereof six went to the hin; and the hin containing the quantity of seventy two eggshells. See Exod. 29:40. and the annotations upon it.

**V. 12.** *{Dung that cometh out of man}* Signifying the great scarcity of wood, to bake their bread within the time of the siege; yea of the dung of cattle, for their cattle should be consumed in the siege and famine. And also that the Lord holding his people for profane and unclean, would make them in exile and captivity, unable to observe that ceremonial purity in their meats, which God had commanded. See v. 13. Hos. 9:3. Dan. 1:8. See Deut. 23:12, 13, 14.

*{in their sight}* It seems then this was not done in vision only, and the prophet's imaginations; but actually before the people's eyes.

**V. 13.** *{Defiled bread among the Gentiles}* Defiled; not because of the diversity of seeds, ver. 9. but because of the dressing.

*{Gentiles}* To show their extreme misery, not only in the siege; but in their captivity also and exile.

*{whither I will drive them}* Gentiles, here, then must not be understood of the Chaldeans, while they were besieging them; as some would have it.

**V. 14.** *{Ah Lord}* So chap. 20:49. Jer. 1:6. and 14:13. See Act. 10:14.

*{of that which dieth}* Exod. 22:31. Lev. 17:15. Deut. 22:8. Ezek. 44:31.

*{Abominable flesh}* Deut. 14:3. And the prophet counted this enjoined him, as abominable, if not more; and a defilement for him to touch it.

**V. 15.** *{Cow's dung}* Thus God condescends: and so, Amos 7:2, 3, 5, 6. And should not we condescend to one another? Rom. 15:1, 2, 3. This used, being dried by the poor, in some nations, for to dress their meat; and was not abominable, as man's dung, Deut. 23:22, 23, 24. And this being the thing here changed, and condescended unto, shows that the thing complained of, as a pollution, was not the mingled seeds, but man's dung. And this might be a sign of the mitigation of the threatening towards the elect; but rather a peculiar privilege to Ezekiel's person, in this present case. For the famine in the siege, endured, and did worse than this, chap. 5:10. Lam. 1:11. and 2:17, 12, 20. and 4:10. See the like, 2 Kings 6:28, 29. See Job 6:6, 7.

**V. 16.** *{I will break}* An Hebrew phrase, Lev. 26:26. Isa. 3:1. That is, I will send an extreme penury of bread, and of all things needful for the sustenance of man's life. The evil arrows of famine, chap. 5:16, 10. the deficiency of bread; and happily to the unsufficiency of it to nourish, Micah 6:14. Hag. 1:6. See the accomplishment of it, Jer. 37:21. 2 Kings 25:3. Lam. 4:3–10.

*{staff of bread}* Chap. 5:16. and 14:3. Psal. 104:15. Levit. 26:26. Psal. 105:16. the whole staff of bread: and Esay 3:1. the whole stay of bread, and the whole stay of water. See Esay 5:1, 4.

**V. 17.** *{And be astonied}* At their present astonishing misery; so contrary to their false hopes, conceived by their false prophets' prophecies.

*{and consume away}* Lev. 26:39.

## CHAPTER 5

**Verse 1.** *{And}* This chapter carries on the argument of the former. In it are types, setting forth the general destruction of the people of the Jews for their sins.

*{Take thee}* This seems to be commanded, during the former vision; or rather presently after the three hundred and ninety days were ended, vers. 2. compare together, chap. 1:1, 2. & 3:15, 23, 27. chap. 4:ver. 5. and chap. 8:1. And this to be really put in execution; to signify a total destruction of the nation, here understood by the hair of the head and beard; whereof one part was consumed in the siege, by famine and pestilence, as it were by fire; another was killed by the Chaldeans; a third was scattered over the world, and perished by God's vengeance; and a small remnant escaped, whereof part also perished by divers calamities. See vers. 12. Jer. 15:2.

*{a sharp knife}* A razor is the sharpest. Such was Nebuchadnezzar: as formerly the Assyrian, Esay 7:20.

*{and cause}* By himself, or by a barber, or any other.

*{upon thy head, and upon thy beard}* This in itself was held for a reproach and shame, and sign of greatest sorrow, Esay 15:2. Jer. 47:5. and 48:37. 2 Sam. 10:5. But the prophet undergoes it at God's command; even therein also to be a portentous sign of that shame and sorrow, that should befall the people.

*{upon thine head}* The head city Jerusalem, vers. 5. so Esay 7:7, 8, 9. See Esay 7:20. Deut. 18:13, 44.

*{balances to weigh}* The precise accurateness of God's dealing, in executing his judgments, Dan. 5:25, 27. 2 Sam. 8:1. They are not too light, or heavy; but just to a hair's weight: yet mixed with mercy to his, Jer. 46:28. Lam. 3:22. Ezra 9:13.

**V. 2.** *{with fire}* That is, famine, pestilence, and other means; which as a fire should consume them.

*{a third part}* Chap. 7:15.

*{of the City}* To wit of that city which he had portrayed upon the brick or tile, chap. 4:1. Or of Tel-abib; or some city, where the prophet dwelt in Chaldea; for he was not brought unto Jerusalem, not so much as in vision yet.

*{when the days}* Those three hundred and ninety days, chap. 4:ver. 5, 8, 9.

*{about it}* That is, about the city: those that did fight, or fled in the time of the siege; and of the entering in, or breaking up of the city; and those slain in Riblah, 2 Kings 25:18, 19, 20, 21.

*{Scatter in the wind}* Scatter them into other nations by flight or captivity.

*{a sword after them}* Vers. 12. Lev. 26:33. See this accomplished, Jer. 42:16, 17, 22. and 43:10, 11. and 44:27. Obad. vers. 14.

**V. 3.** *{a few in number}* Such as Nabuzaradan left of the poor of the land, to be vinedressers and husbandmen; and Gedaliah to be ruler over them, 2 Kings 25:12, 22.

*{skirts}* Heb. *wings.* Meaning that a very few should be left, which the Lord would preserve among all these storms; but not without troubles and trial. So Esay 1:9. and 6:10.

**V. 4.** *{cast them}* They which were with Gedaliah, soon by Ismael come to nothing, 2 Kings 25:25. but the hairs of the head of the godly are all numbered, Matth. 10:30.

*{Thereof shall a fire}* Out of that fire, to wit, by the death of Gedaliah, miseries shall arise to the rest of the Jews: which are set out by Jeremy 41, 42, 43, 44. chapters, and 52:vers. 30. They look too far, that extend this to the times of the miracles.

**V. 5.** *{This is Jerusalem}* That is to say. This figure represents Jerusalem, and shows how I will use her. And in a like sacramental phrase, *This is my body*, Matth. 16:26. So Gen. 41:26. 1 Cor. 10:4.

*{I have set}* Among many nations, as Prov. 30:19. I had sanctified it, and made choice of it for my abode, and enriched it with singular graces, Psal. 48. and 87:1, 2, 3. That she sat as a queen among the nations, Lam. 1:1. Yea, and placed it much about the middle, and center of the known world; and so be indeed, a lantern, and pattern, to them of piety and sanctity, Deut. 4:6, etc.

**V. 6.** *{And she}* The causes of God's judgments against them, ver. 6, 7. she that should have been a light of religion, and of life unto them, Deut. 4:6. and 32:6. Esay 1:2. Jerem. 2:12. Esay 5:4.

*{my judgments}* My word and law, into superstition, and wicked works, and wages, chap. 7:2. Jer. 2:11. Rom. 1:23, 25. Jude 4.

*{more than the nations}* More transgressed my holy judgments and statutes, given from heaven, than the nations and Gentiles have done theirs, by nature's light dictated unto them, 2 Kings 21:11. See the like, Ezech. chap. 16:47, 48, 51. They could not do worse than they did, Jer. 3:5.

*{round about her}* The Moabites, Ammonites, Philistines, etc.

*{refused}* As Hos. 4:6. despised; as a light thing.

**V. 7.** *{Because ye multiplied}* Because your idols are in greater number, and your superstitions more than among the professed idolaters. See chap. 16:25, 26, 28, 29. Isa. 65. Or, he condemns their ingratitude in respect of his benefits, Deut. 32:15. Hos. 13:6. Their sins caused God to come on to judgments; provokes him to do his strange work, Esay 28:21.

*{According to the judgments}* Namely by cleaving to your God, as every one of them has done to their god, Jer. 2:10, 11, 12. See Act. 19:34. Solomon's wives were constant to their gods; and drew him also to do as they did, Judg. 2:12. and 10:6. Or, by following your mere natural reason, as the best among other nations have done, Rom. 2:14. Thus God convicts them of their sins; to make way for a further conviction, namely of the equity and necessity of his judgments.

**V. 8.** *{Therefore}* God's judgments set down to the end of the chapter, generally and particularly.

*{I even I}* By his instruments, Jer. 25:9. and so he did at other times, by *Ashur*, Esay 10:5, 6, 7. by *Cyrus*, Esay 41:1–6. Amos 3:6. Micah 1:12. and 6:9.

*{Am against thee}* And then who, or what can be for us; and as true on the other side, Rom. 8:31. Ezek. 22:14.

*{judgments}* Alluding happily to judgments in the former verse; and implying here judgments of another kind.

*{in the sight of the Nations}* Not secretly, or privately; but openly: all should see his dealing with them, even those nations, in whose sight they had so sinned.

**V. 9.** *{which I have not done}* Not the like judgments upon the heathens, Lam. 1:6. Greater than the punishment of Sodom: that though sharp, yet short, and sudden, Lam. 4:9. Samaria's punishment, 2 Kings 6:28, 29. short of this, by considering the next words, ver. 10. and Samaria then was delivered. However, this phrase of speech serves only to express the exceeding height and weight of these judgments. A like phrase is that, 2 Kings 18:5. and chap. 23:25. compared together. The like may be the meaning of that phrase, Mat. 24:21.

*{because of all thine abominations}* Idolatry, incest, and sundry other sins are called abominations, 1 Kings 11:5, 7. Lev. 18:27. whereof they were guilty.

**V. 10.** *{Therefore the Fathers}* Chap. 4:9, etc. Levit. 26:29. Deut. 28:53. 2 Kings 6:29. Lament. 4:10. if the pitiful mothers, much more the fathers, Jerem. 16:3. and 22:8, 9. Esay 9:19, 20.

*{execute judgments}* In these times of siege Ezekiel foretold extreme sorrow should be in Israel, by plague, famine, and sword; fathers should eat their children, and children their fathers. Moses foresaw and foretold that, Deut. 28:53. Daniel foretold of a greater desolation, when Christ is utterly denied by them, Dan. 9:27.

**V. 11.** *{As I live}* The Lord's vehement asseveration, ch. 16:48. often used in Scripture: but most often in this prophet. It is an oath as the word is taken in its latitude, Heb. 6:13, 14, 17. Deut. 32:40.

*{my sanctuary}* Psal. 2:c. 2. my temple, with their idols, altars, Jer. 7:30. and 32:34. 2 Kings 16:10–16. and chap. 22:12. and with other defilements, Ezek. 44:7.

*{neither shall mine eyes spare}* Chap. 7:4, 9. and 8:18. Deut. 7:16. and 13:8. See 1 Sam. 24:10. God it is still that does it. 2 Chron. 12:7. Amos 4:6, 10. and 5:ver. 6. Esay 63:10. Now the eye is the index, the intelligencer of the mind, the casement it looks out at; the dispositions of the mind, are visible in the eye, as in a looking glass. The meaning here is, you shall have no sign of mercy, in mine eye, in all your miseries.

*{neither will I have any pity}* As 1 Sam. 15:3. as Amos 1:11. See Zach. 11:6. Jerem. 21:7. and 13:ver. 14. Lament. 2:2, 17, 21. Ezek. 8:18.

**V. 12.** *{A third part}* The explication of that vers. 2. no escaping from God's judgments; one or other will overtake them, 1 Kings 19:15, 16, 17. Amos 9:1, 5. and 2:14, 15, 16.

*{I will scatter}* Chap. 6:8. Lev. 26:33. into Babylon, Moab, Ammon, Egypt, Psal. 44:12. Zech. 7:14.

*{I will draw, &c.}* Ver. 2. Levit. 26:33. Jerem. 46:10. and 48:2.

**V. 13.** *{be accomplished}* Let out to the full, go on to the highest degree. He would stir up all his wrath, pour out his full vials.

*{my fury}* No affections or passions in God's immixed and immutable essence; but spoken after the manner of men, and for our capacities.

*{to rest upon them}* Rest and reside there; so as they should be a people under fury, a generation of his wrath, Jerem. 7:29. a people of his wrath, Esay 10:6. See Jerem. 7:20. or else, as some my fury shall rest and cease, being satisfied. See chap. 16:42.

*{be comforted}* That is, I will not be pacified, until I be revenged, Prov. 1:26. Isa. 1:24. and 30:32. God again speaks after the manner of men, and to our understanding. Thus are men eased, comforted upon revenge taken: *Minuit vindicta dolorem,* revenge is sweet.

*{in my zeal}* Fiery jealousy, and hot hatred against them for their sins. Like that, Proverb. 6:34. See Psal. 79:5. Esay 58:17. Ezek. 16:38. Deut. 29:20.

**V. 14.** *{waste}* Jerusalem, and all Jewry. Jerusalem was the praise of the earth, Psal. 122:13, 14. Esay 1:21. and 52:1. and 60:14. Lament. 2:15. Jewry the glory of all lands, Ezek. 20:6. the Lord's land, Hos. 9:3. Immanuel's land, Esay 8:8. See Deut. 11:10, 11, 12. Zach. 2:12. and 7:14. yet for their sins, God would lay it waste, Esay 7:24. and 64:10, 11. Psal. 107:34. See Neh. 2:17. Lament. 1:4. and 2:2, 3. & 5:18.

**V. 15.** *{a reproach}* As was threatened, Deut. 28:37. and executed, Ezek. 22:4. Jerem. 42:18. and 24:9. Lam. 5:1. and chap. 2:15, 16.

*{an instruction,}* *nocumenta, documenta;* *παθήματα, μαθήματα;* as to themselves, so to others, by their examples, Esay 26:8, 9.

*{an astonishment}* Through admiration, Deut. 29:22, 23, 24. Jer. 19:8. and 22:8.

*{in furious rebukes}* See the height of God's anger whereunto it was provoked by sin. These rebukes then were not to cure, but to destroy.

**V. 16.** *{when I shall send}* Deut. 32:23.

*{evil arrows}* Which were the grasshoppers, mildew, and whatsoever were the occasions of famine: or rather, the famine in the siege, brought upon them by the besiegers. This famine like arrows shot, pierced, and pinched their stomachs, and inwards, wounded them to the heart, slew them, and laid them heaps upon heaps, Deut. 32:23, 24.

*{staff of bread}* Chap. 4:16.

**V. 17.** *{Evil beasts}* As was threatened, Lev. 26:22. Deut. 32:24. 2 Kings 17:25. which use to multiply and increase upon vastations, and desolations of countries.

*{I the LORD}* Ver. 11. *as I live.* He subscribes it, as it were, with his hand; avows and asserts it for his word, and doom; and that it is not the false figment of any other. And then shall the Lord speak it, and not perform it, chap. 12:25. Jer. 1:12. Esay 14:27. whose word shall stand, Jer. 44:28. His words should not be as wind, but as thunderbolts: and they should be convinced of it by woeful experience. So ver. 13, 15.

## CHAPTER 6

**Verse 1.** *{And the word}* This chapter contains a more ample explication of the former types, and threatenings; prophesied and denounced soon after the 390 days, chap. 4:5. were past: as may be gathered by comparing together, chap. 1:1, 2. and chap. 8:1. Together with a promise of mercy inserted to a repenting remnant that shall be among the Gentiles.

**V. 2.** *{Set thy face}* By this sign show whereunto your prophesy looks and tends, chap. 35:2. and chap. 38:2.

*{Towards the mountains of Israel}* Chap. 36:1. Israel and Judah were both mountainous, Exod. 15:17. Deut. 11:11. 1 Kings 28:23. Psal. 87:1, 125:2. Esay 14:25. 2 Chron. 27:4. Jer. 32:34. Ezek. 38:21. Amos 3:9.

**V. 4.** *{your Images}* Or, *Sun-images;* and so ver. 6. See Lev. 26:3. 2 Kings 23:11. 2 Chron. 34:7.

**V. 5.** *{Lay the dead}* Heb. *give.*

*{Their Idols}* In contempt of their power, and force, which shall neither be able to deliver you, nor themselves, 2 Kings 23:20. See ver. 13. of this chapter.

**V. 6.** *{Your works may be abolished}* Your idols, altars, chapels, and all other superstitious inventions of man.

**V. 8.** *{Yet will I leave}* A promise to a remnant repenting, he shows that in all dangers God will preserve a few, which shall be as the seed of the church, and shall call upon his name.

**V. 9.** *{I am broken}* That is, my patience has been overcome. Or I have been grieved and afflicted; even as a husband would be at the evil carriage of his adulterous wife. See Psal. 78:40. Isa. 7:13. and 63:18.

*{Loath themselves}* They shall repent, 2 Cor. 7:11. Job 42:6.

**V. 11.** *{Smite}* A repetition, if not exaggeration of the fearful judgments, to the end of the chapter. These two signs are here of grief, showing thereby the woeful destruction to come; yet smiting the hands together, is sometimes a sign of anger; as Num. 24:10. sometimes of heartening, encouraging, and setting on; as Ezek. 21:17. and sometimes of joy; as Psal. 98:8. Ezek. 25:6.

**V. 13.** *{under every green tree}* Isa. 57:5. Hos. 4:13.

**V. 14.** *{More desolate than the wilderness}* Or, *desolate from the wildernesse.*

*{Diblath}* This place is not mentioned elsewhere: unless it be Diblathaim: of which Jer. 48:22. which was in the country of Moab, near unto that great and terrible desert, mentioned Deut. 8:15.

## CHAPTER 7

**Verse 1.** *{Moreover}* This chapter is a continuation and confirmation, if not an exaggeration, of the judgments denounced in the former chapter.

**V. 3.** *{Thine abominations}* I will punish you as you have deserved for your idolatries, and other crying crimes, ver. 8.

**V. 4.** *{And mine eye}* Chap. 5:11.

*{shall be in the midst of thee}* That is, they having been never wiped out, nor canceled by my pardon, nor by your repentance, I will make you endure open punishment for them which you shall not be able to avoid, ver. 9.

*{The LORD}* That smites, ver. 9.

**V. 5.** *{An only evil}* Chap. 5:9. as 1 Sam. 26:8.

**V. 6.** *{An end is come}* These reduplications following, show the certainty and celerity of the judgments.

*{it watcheth}* Heb. *Awaketh against thee.*

**V. 7.** *{The morning is come}* Ver. 10.

*{and not of the sounding of the mountains}* Not any vain echo of false fears or foolish joys.

**V. 8.** *{Shortly}* About four years after (as was formerly said) the siege began by the Chaldeans, chap. 1:2. and Jer. 52:4. compared together.

**V. 9.** *{I will recompense thee}* Heb. *upon thee.*

**V. 10.** *{Behold the day}* As if it were present before their eyes.

*{The rod hath blossomed}* A proverbial kind of speech, to signify, that the people's sins were sprung up to ripeness and maturity, to the filling up of the measure heap-full, which draws God's judgment upon them, Gen. 15:16.

**V. 11.** *{None of them shall remain}* They shall all perish, I will suffer them no longer.

*{wailing for them}* Chap. 24:16, 22, 23. Jer. 16:5, 6. Their own affliction shall be so great, that they shall have no regard to lament for others; yea scarce any left remaining, to wail for them.

**V. 12.** *{Let not the buyer rejoice}* For the present profit.

*{seller mourn}* For he shall lose nothing.

**V. 13.** *{For the seller}* By reason of the desolation of the land, all manner of right and title to the property of land, shall be lost by the buyers or sellers, and neither shall come again to his land; not the seller, though he had not sold it: no not in the year of Jubilee, though he live unto it, Lev. 25:13, 23.

*{Although they were yet alive}* Heb. *Though their life were yet among the living.*

*{shall any strengthen himself}* None shall be able by art or cunning, by good or by evil means, to avoid my sentences of death.

*{In the iniquity of his life}* Or, *whose life is in his iniquity.* Heb. *his iniquity.*

**V. 14.** *{They have blown the Trumpet}* Though they make great preparations, their hearts shall fail them at their need, and they shall not be able to do anything for to defend themselves.

**V. 15.** *{The sword}* Deut. 32:25. Lam. 1:20.

**V. 16.** *{On the mountains}* Scattered up and down to seek safety there, in the most remote and inaccessible dens and secret places of the *mountains.*

*{mourning}* For their miseries, and their sins, the cause of them.

**V. 17.** *{All hands shall be feeble}* Isa. 13:7. Jer. 6:24. all manner of strength to defend themselves, and to subsist shall fail.

*{shall be weak as water}* Heb. *go into water.*

**V. 18.** *{And baldness upon all their heads}* According to a lawful custom of plucking off, or shaving of one's hair, in token of great grief: though in some kind and way God had forbidden it, Lev. 19:27, 28. Deut. 14:1. See Isa. 3:24. and 22:12. Jer. 48:37. Ezek. 27:31. Amos 8:10. Mic. 1:16.

**V. 19.** *{They shall cast}* Taking care for nothing but to save their own persons; or they shall leave their wealth to their enemies, thinking to ransom their lives therewith; but it shall all be but in vain. See ver. 12. and Isa. 13:17.

*{removed}* As the things were which were separated from them, by reason of their uncleanness by the law: and this because they should not find any help in them, as their trust had been; as the words following do show.

*{Their silver}* Prov. 11:4. Zeph. 1:18. Eccl. 5:8.

*{because it is a stumbling block}* Their iniquity has made a stumbling block to themselves.

**V. 20.** *{As for the beauty}* The ark, and sanctuary or temple, the signs of God's presence, and means of his worship,

*{therefore have I set it far from them}* Or, *made it unto them an unclean thing*, 1, 2. I decree to separate and put away from them, as an unclean thing by the law is, and used to be cast away, Esay 30:22.

**V. 21.** *{Strangers}* That is, of the Babylonians.

*{pollute it}* Ver. 22. by entering into it, spoiling it with the blood (likely) of slain persons in it: and by all manner of means profaning, sacking, and ruining it.

**V. 22.** *{My face}* Though they call and cry upon me in that time of their calamity, that I would pity and spare them and mine own temple.

*{My secret place}* Even the most holy place, whereinto none might enter but the high priest, and where God by the signs of his presence did dwell.

*{Robbers}* Or, *burglers.*

*{enter into it}* Namely, the *Sanctum Sanctorum,* or secret place.

*{defile it}* Shall sack and destroy it, as a profane and polluted place, without any respect at all had to it, 2 Kings 25:9. 2 Chron. 36:19. Jer. 52:13.

**V. 23.** *{Make a chain}* For a sign of the people's captivity. See Jer. 27:2.

*{bloody crimes}* That is, of sins that deserve death.

**V. 25.** *{Destruction}.* Heb. *Cutting off.*

**V. 26.** *{Mischief shall come}* Psal. 42:7. Jer. 4:20.

*{they seek a vision}* In the midst of these great troubles they shall require some counsel, direction, or comfort from the prophets: but not only prophesy, which is an extraordinary gift, shall fail them; but also the ordinary preaching of God's word, and all good advice and provision of human wisdom.

*{from the ancients}* Both in church and state.

*{but the law shall perish}* Psal. 74:9. Lament. 2:9.

**V. 27.** *{Hands of the people}* The people shall be dismayed, that they will be able to do nothing in their own defense.

*{According unto their deserts}* Heb. *with their judgments.*

## CHAPTER 8

**Verse 1.** *{And it came to pass}* This and the three chapters following, contain one vision. In this eighth chapter are showed divers ways the abominable idolatries of the Jews, yet remaining in Jerusalem, under the reign of King Zedekiah.

*{In the sixth year}* Fourteen months, that is one year, and two months, since that time mentioned in chap. 1:1, 2. and about a fortnight after the end of those three hundred and ninety days; chap. 4:5. as is most likely, upon consideration had of the words, chap. 3:15, 24, 25, 26, 27.

*{sixth month}* Which contains part of August, and part of September.

*{sixth day}* Towards the end of our August.

*{in mine house}* In Mesopotamia.

*{And the elders}* That were in captivity with me.

*{sat before me}* Likely come to ask counsel from God by me, chap. 33:31. and by God's providence, while they were there, this vision befell the prophet, which made for the greater notification, and credit of it.

*{hand of the LORD}* As chap. 1:3.

**V. 2.** *{And lo, a likeness}* Of a man, as appears by the words following.

*{As the appearance of fire}* As chap. 1:27.

*{of brightness}* Of the bright shining sun.

*{of amber}* Chap. 1:5.

**V. 3.** *{And he put forth}* That man in the former verse, (Dan. 5:5.) All this was done in a mental vision, and not really in a body.

*{to the door of the inner gate}* That is, to the door of the gate of the inner court. See chap. 10:3. or priests' court, namely of Solomon's temple; though some learned men would understand hereby the very ingress of the great court which was for the people.

*{that looketh toward the North}* This seems to be the north door and gate of the priests' court; for that court had four gates and doors: on the east, west, north, and south; that is, on each side one; and porters appointed to them; as appears, 1 Chr. 9:19, 22, 23, 24. and 1 Chron. 26. and they seem to have these names, viz. 1 Sur the east gate, 2 Kings 11:6. called also the gate of the foundation, 2 Chron. 23:5. from the foundation of it, because it had the greatest and lowest foundation (for the great court of the people joining to it, stood upon the declivity of an hill) called also the higher gate of the house of the Lord, 2 Kings 15:35. because it was the highest, and the greatest, reckoning from the foundation to the top. This was the chief gate. 2 The gate behind the guard which is the south gate, 2 Kings 11:6. which seems also to be called the gate Sippim, that is, of the doors, or thresholds, 2 Chron. 23:4. 3 Shallecheth, the west gate towards the king's house, 1 Chron. 26:16. 1 Kings 10:5, 12. 1 Chron. 26:16, 18. 2 Chron. 9:4, 11. 4 The north gate behind the brazen altar, as appears in these words, and in the word of the fifth verse following, likely so called from that time and fact mentioned, 2 Kings 16:14.

*{the seat of, &c.}* The image itself was at the north gate of the brazen altar, in the entry, ver. 5.

*{Image of jealousy}* It is very uncertain what image this was, but it is plain it was a very abominable one, (as the idol of Baal was) to which the people bare great devotion; built likely by Ahaz first after the example of Ahab, 2 Kings 16:3, 4. 2 Chron. 23:2. and after repaired by Manasseh, 2 Kings 20:3, 4, 5. and the wicked sons of good Josiah, and it is called the image of jealousy, because it was the principal object of the people's spiritual adultery. See Jer. 7:30. and 23:11. and 32:34, 35.

**V. 4.** *{saw in the plain}* Chap. 1:23. and 3:22, 23.

**V. 5.** *{Northward at the gate of the altar}* Namely, the brazen altar of burnt offerings; which being in the forefront, about the middle of the inner court, the north and south doors of this court, were by direct line opposite to the said altar, in equal distance. And therefore it is not known, why the northern gate should be so called peculiarly, unless this name was given it, when Ahaz removed the altar out of its place, and did set it near to this gate, 2 Kings 16:14.

**V. 6.** *{from my sanctuary}* For God will not be where idols are.

**V. 7.** *{To the doors of the court}* Which court is not named; and hereupon the opinions are divers. The tabernacle had but one court, and that court but one gate, Exod. 27. The temple of Solomon which is here spoken of, had but two courts, one for the priests and Levites which was the inner court, next to the temple, and compassing it, which was the inner court; and the other for the people, which was the great and utter court, compassing the court of the priests, 1 Kings 7:12. 2 Kings 21:5. and 23:12. 2 Chron. 4:9. We may not then understand this court, here, of a middle court, proper to the Levites, betwixt the court of the priests, and that of the people; for such a one Solomon's temple had not. That in Ezekiel's vision mentions such a middle court, chap. 40:28. to 44. But that was fourteen years after the city was smitten, and this temple destroyed, chap. 40:1. And it was in vision only; never in being: a type only of the church of Christ under the gospel. This court then here named, must needs be understood, to be, either that of the priests, or that of the people: but whether of the two, is not easily and certainly known. But it seems rather, by comparing what goes before and after, to be that of the priests; and the east door of it.

**7.** *{of the court}* Namely, the priests' court.

*{A hole}* To signify, as it seems, the Jews' carelessness, in preserving and repairing God's temple, and the chambers of it.

*{in the wall}* He seems to mean the wall, that was on the one side of this portal, where the doorkeepers' chambers were.

**V. 8.** *{a door}* It is likely that this was the chamber's ordinary door, but now was shut; and that the door, by which the idolaters came secretly into this chapel of idols and imagery, was on some other by-part of the chamber.

**V. 10.** *{Creeping things, and abominable beasts}* Kinds of idolatry, taken from the heathen neighboring nations, and specially from the Egyptians, whom now they courted for help, who worshipped dogs, cats, crocodiles, serpents, and other abominable beasts, forbidden in the law, Lev. 11.

**V. 11.** *{Seventy men of the ancients}* Not unlikely, but those of that number and order, appointed by God, Num. 11:16, etc. or at leastwise, such as should have kept all the rest in the fear, and true service of God, and yet were the ringleaders to all abominations; and by their example, pulled others from God.

*{A thick cloud}* It was in such abundance.

**V. 12.** *{do in the dark}* For besides their common idolatry, they had particular services, which they had in secret chambers. As the Egyptians had their dark devotions to Osiris; and afterwards, both Grecians and Romans, had the like blind, and close spiritual adulteries, and idolatries, in a secret and hidden way and manner, to some of their false gods and goddesses.

*{The LORD seeth us not}* Chap. 9:9. He takes no more care of us, he has forsaken both us and the land; wherefore we will fly to the gods of other nations, which do relieve them; and since he has renounced us, we will also renounce him. See 2 Chron. 28:23. Jer. 44:17.

**V. 13.** *{greater abominations}* Wicked idolaters proceed from evil to worse, 2 Tim. 3:13.

**V. 14.** *{To the doors of the gate of the LORD'S house, which was towards the North}* The north gate of the court of the priests.

*{women}* That ought not to have entered into that place; most likely the women priests, and unchaste worshippers of the Egyptian Isis.

*{weeping for Tammuz}* Called *Ammuz* of Herodotus and Plutarch; and seems to be the same called Osiris, or Apis, or Serapis, a king of Egypt, and the husband, brother, or son of Isis, queen of Egypt. Thus various are the heathen writers in their histories and narrations. This Osiris and Isis became so famous in Egypt, as they grew to be worshipped; and upon his loss, and death by Typhon, Isis ordained a yearly mourning for him, in their fourth month from him, called Tammuz. In which feast the women used very immodest rites and ceremonies. Thus out of heathen authors do Christian writers report. And this shameful, and whorish, and beastly idolatry, came in practice among the Jews, even in the house of the Lord. And to this do those places seem to relate, 1 Kings 14:24. and chap. 15:12. and chap. 22:46. and 2 Kings 23:7.

**V. 16.** *{The porch and the altar}* The porch of the temple, wherein were those two huge and high pillars, Jachin, and Boaz, 1 Kings 6:3. and chap. 7:15, etc. 2 Chron. 3:4, 15, 16, 17. in the east part of the temple; and the altar of brass for burnt offerings placed in the priests' court, between the east gate of the priests' court, and the porch of the temple: as the altar was in the court of the tabernacle, Exod. 3:18, 19, 20. and 38:1, etc. and 40:6, 7. 2 Chron. 4:1.

*{were about five and twenty men}* About, so that his number is somewhat uncertain. Not so as those, ch. 11:1. Neither does any reason enforce these to be the same with those. However prime men, likely they were, and against the law, came into this place, and exercised most abominable idolatry in it.

*{with their backs}* A sign that they had utterly renounced God and his service; and in that posture that they might worship the sun toward the east.

*{The Sun toward the East}* Deut. 4:19. 2 Kings 23:5, 11. Job 31:26. Jer. 44:17.

**V. 17.** *{Is it a light thing to the house of Judah, that they commit the abominations}* Or, *Is there any thing lighter then to commit?* or, *Is it lighter?* This question is a strong negation. See Gen. 18:17. The meaning is, that the Jews thought nothing lighter; although these abominations against the first table were the most grievous and fearfulest; yet there was no command so great, which the Jews thought not light of, to transgress it.

*{with violence}* By oppression of the innocent, and of the poor; which is done either in common, by unrighteous judgment; or in particular, by all kinds of injustice.

*{To provoke}* In profaning my temple.

*{They put the branch to}* זמורה, is a branch of a tree; so is the Hebrew word taken, Numb. 13:24. and chap. 15:2. and here is set forth a practice of heathenish idolaters, in worshipping the sun, which they call upon, because it quickens the fruits, and makes them ripe, in token whereof they kiss the branch, or the hand, as Job 31:26, 27. So did they to Bacchus, 2 Maccab. 6:7. See Jer. 44:17. Hos. 2:8. Others thus, they bring the stink to their noses; that is to say, they offend themselves by committing such abominations.

**V. 18.** *{Eye shall not spare}* Chap. 5:11. and 7:4.

*{Cry in my Ears with a loud voice}* Prov. 1:28. Isa. 1:15. Jer. 11:11. Mic. 3:4.

## CHAPTER 9

**Verse 1.** *{He cried}* In this chapter is figured out the slaughter to be made in Jerusalem, of the people that were therein, excepting the godly mourners that were marked out to be spared.

*{Cause them that have charge over the City}* The visitations of the city draw near; that is, the time to take vengeance: when the visitors are called for, the visitation will be kept.

*{even every man}* In this vision, being a figure of what was to be done on earth, God speaks to his angels, executioners of his judgments: by which are meant the Chaldeans, instruments of the Jews' destruction. See Dan. 10:13, 20. and 11:1.

**V. 2.** *{six men}* Which were angels in the similitude of men; as Gen. 18:2. Act. 10. These six may happily have some relation to the six chief commanders under the king of Babylon, who did invade and take Jerusalem, Jer. 39:3. And likewise to be six in number, with a reference to the six gates of Jerusalem. The city being divided into three towns, each of them had two chief gates, to which the streets and quarters of the whole city did belong. In the city of David (wherein was the stronghold, or castle of Zion) were the port of David, or the fish gate; and the old port. In the town of Benjamin, the gate of Ephraim, and the corner gate, 2 Chron. 25:23. In that town or port that belonged to the lot of Judah, were the dung gate, and sheep gate.

*{Higher gate}* Likely it is, that every gate belonging to the courts of the temple, had a portal belonging to it; which was a great building that had two overtures, with a gallery or entry between; one of the doors was toward the outward court, the other towards the inner court: now the plain on the outside being lower, some conceive that that gate was likewise lower, and that the other of the inner court was higher; and they went up to it by steps. Thus it was in that typical vision of the temple, Ezek. 40:18, 19, 20. See 2 Kings 15:35. 2 Chron. 27:3.

*{which lieth toward the North}* Heb. *which is turned.* And from the north, by the Chaldeans, and their armies, this slaughter and destruction came, Jer. 1:13.

*{A slaughter weapon}* Heb. *a weapon of his breaking in pieces.*

*{and one man}* Many understand this of Christ our Savior, but it may seem rather to be meant of a created angel, (as the rest) appointed to this gracious office; and so here set forth not armed, as the rest, but in a priestly habit. See chap. 10:2.

*{among them}* Heb. *in the midst of them:* but none of the six, ver. 2. because those every of them had weapons, and were ordained to destroy the wicked; but this had a writer's inkhorn.

*{inkhorn}* To mark them in the forehead that mourned for the sin of the city, ver. 3. that they might be kept safe. That falls out in Jeremy, Baruc, Abedmelech, and such godly persons. So in the spiritual fall from the faith to idolatry, which the Apocalypse reveals, an angel comes from the east, and seals a great number, Apoc. 7.

*{by his side}* Heb. *upon his loyns.*

*{brazen Altar}* Namely, the altar of burnt offerings, 2 Chron. 4:1. Now these angels do here present themselves, as it were, to receive their commission from God coming out of his temple, ver. 3, 5.

**V. 3.** *{And the glory of the God}* Seen in the full vision and description of it by the river Chebar, chap. 1:3, 28. and again in the plain, chap. 3:23. and lastly, at the north door of the inner gate (of the Lord's house) in Jerusalem, chap. 8:4. visiting his house, and showing to Ezekiel the abominable idolatries of his people there.

*{was gone up}* Or, *went up;* that is, his glory more properly and peculiarly. So called, chap. 1:25, 26, 27, 28. was gone up from the cherubims and wheels, leaving them at the north door of the inner gate, chap. 8:4.

*{from the Cherub}* Cherubims: not those in *Sancto Sanctorum;* there was no mention of them before, nor any notification or specification made of them now; neither was there any visible sign of God's glory sitting upon the cherubims that were over the propitiatory in the most holy place, that might be said here to be gone up from thence. These therefore here are to be understood of the same four living creatures, wights and cherubims, which alone are mentioned and described, chap. 1:5, etc. and 8:4. and so all along after in the 10 chapter throughout, and chap. 1:22, 23. to the end of this heavenly vision, and were now (as has been said) at the north door of the inner gate, chap. 8:4. from whence it appears they removed to the right side of the house, chap. 10:3.

*{to the Threshold of the house}* Or, *Temple;* there to pronounce the sentence, for marking some to be spared, and for slaying others. We read not that the cherubims were there at the threshold. That place, chap. 10:4. makes not to the contrary; as it is well cleared by our translation.

*{And he called to the man}* Namely, the Son of God which sat upon the throne, chap. 1:26. called to this angel.

**V. 4.** *{A mark}* The Hebrew word is תו which signifies a mark; and is not the bare letter ת. All this happened in vision, for a figure of the distinction which God makes in his general judgments, between his elect believers and the wicked. See Exod. 12:7, 13. Apoc. 7:3. and 3:12. we read of antichrist's mark, Apoc. 13:16.

*{That sigh}* He shows what is the manner of God's children whom he marks to salvation; to wit, to mourn and cry out against the wickedness which they see committed against God:

**V. 5.** *{Mine hearing}* Heb. *Mine eares.*

**V. 6.** *{Slay utterly}* This executed, 2 Chron. 36:17.

*{The Mark}* Thus in all his plagues the Lord preserves his small number, which he marks, as Exod. 12:23. Rev. 7:3. But the chief mark is the Spirit of adoption, wherewith the heart is sealed up to life everlasting.

*{and begin at my Sanctuary}* 1 Pet. 4:18. begin at the very place where you are. My sanctuary shall be no sanctuary to privilege them from slaughter, 1 Kings 1:50, 51. and 2:28. because by their sins they had so profaned it, 2 Chron. 36:14.

*{The ancient men}* Chap. 8:11.

**V. 7.** *{defile the house}* Make a slaughter there, and bear no respect to the holy place; for according to the law a place was defiled by a dead carcass, or by blood spilt therein.

*{the Court}* Of the priests, and of the people.

**V. 8.** *{and I was left}* Rom. 11:3.

*{Ah, Lord GOD}* This declares, that the servants of God have a compassion, when they see his judgments executed.

**V. 9.** *{Then said}* Jer. 9:3, to 17.

*{Of Israel and Judah}* The conjunction copulative seems to be put by way of interpretation; so that the former word is expounded by the latter: or it may be said, that among the tribe of Judah, some of the ten tribes did remain.

*{full of blood}* Heb. *filled with;* that is, with all kind of wickedness; read Isa. 1:15.

*{perverseness}* Or, *wresting of judgment.*

*{The LORD hath forsaken}* Chap. 8:12.

**V. 10.** *{Mine eye shall not spare}* Chap. 5:11. and 7:4. and 8:18.

**V. 11.** *{The man clothed}* Ver. 2.

*{reported}* Heb. *returned the word.*

## CHAPTER 10

**Verse 1.** *{Then}* This chapter figures out again more fully God's scattering the fire of his wrath upon the city of Jerusalem; and also shows the withdrawing of his gracious and glorious presence from it.

*{in the firmament}* Chap. 1:22.

*{The head of the Cherubims}* Which in chap. 1:5. he called four living creatures; described there at large.

*{A Sapphire stone}* Chap. 1:26. Heb. *as a Saphire stone;* as the appearance of the likeness of a throne, appeared over them; and Christ in his glory upon it: as is perceived by the next verse. See chap. 1:26, 27.

**V. 2.** *{The man clothed}* Whom, chap. 9:2.

*{between the wheels}* Ver. 13.

*{under the Cherub}* Cherubims, which were not far off, on the right side of the house in the next verse.

*{fill thine hand}* Heb. *the hollow of thine hand.*

*{coals of fire}* Of this fire, mention is made, chap. 1:4, 5, 13, 27. A sign of God's majesty, in wrath and judgment who is a devouring fire, which devours the wicked and such as are his enemies, Psal. 18:8, 13. And here we see the same man or angel, formerly a minister of mercy, chap. 9:2. now in this place an executioner of God's fiery wrath and vengeance.

*{over the City}* This signified that the city should be burnt; which vision of Ezekiel took effect, almost five years after, chap. 8:1. and Jer. 52:4, 5, 6, 12, 13. compared together.

**V. 3.** *{Now the Cherubims}* With all the rest of the frame, the wheels.

*{stood on the right side of the house}* Being removed from the north door, of the inner gate where the glory of the Lord left them, chap. 8:3, 4. with chap. 9:3. The right side is the south; the Scripture supposing a man in posture with his face towards the east, as has been said: and so thereupon the sea, that is the Midland Sea, is called the west, Dan. 8:4.

*{when the man went in}* Namely under the cherubims for to take some of the fire coals.

*{Cloud}* That cloud, chap. 1:4. or cloudy darkness, in which was hidden this apparition of God's glory; or rather another cloud, being now, as at many other times, an usual token of the glorious presence of the divine majesty, Exod. 40:34. Num. 9:15. and 16:42. 1 Kings 8:10, 11.

*{inner Court}* Namely the priests' court, or the court of the temple.

**V. 4.** *{Glory of the LORD}* Chap. 1:25, 26, 27, 28. and chap. 9:3.

*{went up}* Heb. *was lifted up.*

*{Threshold of the house}* Chap. 9:3.

*{Court}* To wit, the priests' court.

**V. 5.** *{sound}* Chap. 1:24.

*{utter Court}* That is the people's court, which way the Lord was now going, as he departed from his temple.

*{voice}* Namely, as thunder, Psal. 29:3, 4, 5.

**V. 6.** *{and it came}* Returns to the fuller prosecution, and explication, of that which he began to speak of in the second verse.

**V. 7.** *{Stretched}* Heb. *sent forth.*

**V. 8.** *{a man's hand}* Chap. 1:8.

**V. 9.** *{And when I looked}* A repetition and description of the former vision in the first chapter, with some alterations made for the further clearing and confirmation of it.

*{beryl stone}* Chap. 1:16.

**V. 11.** *{when they went}* Chap. 1:12, 17.

*{head}* According to some he means the cherubim which stood by each wheel; but it is better to be understood of him that sat upon the throne, who guided the whole chariot by an universal and equal inspiration, chap. 1:12, 20, 21.

**V. 12.** *{And their whole body}* In this varying somewhat from the former vision, chap. 1:18. and being more glorious than it: setting forth more fully the all-seeing providence of God; clearly guiding and governing all things whatsoever.

*{wheels}* The outward circle and rings of them, chap. 1:18.

*{That they four had}* Chap. 1:18.

**V. 13.** *{it was cried unto them}* Or, they were called in my hearing, wheel, or galgal, or round world. What was commanded them is not here set down; but all this seems to signify nothing else, but that the wheels did absolutely obey the Son of God, as well as the cherubims, who had life in them, and that they received all their motion from him.

**V. 14.** *{And every one}* Chap. 1:10.

*{The first face was the face of a Cherub}* Seeing that Ezek. 1:10. among the four faces there is an ox's face, instead of which here is set down a cherub's face; some conceive that *cherub* signifies properly the figure of an ox, under which hieroglyphically was represented an angel; as indeed in the Syriac tongue, the word *Cherub*, is taken from a word which signifies drawing the plough, which is the ox his proper labor; and therefore they are called the *same faces*, ver. 22. Howbeit, *Cherub* is commonly taken for a beautiful child's face.

**V. 15.** *{The living Creature}* So ver. 17, 20. and so chap. 1:20. yet called also *living creatures*, chap. 1:13, 14, 15, 19. and four in number, chap. 1:5, 21, 22. This is the same living wight, with the difference aforenamed.

**V. 16.** *{And when the Cherubims went, the wheels}* There was one consent between the cherubs and the wheels, chap. 1:19, 20.

**V. 17.** *{Spirit of the living}* Or, *of life.*

**V. 18.** *{Then the glory of the LORD}* Chap. 10:4. and 9:3.

*{stood over the Cherubims}* Came from the threshold of the house, chap. 9:3. and 10:4. and stood over the cherubims, that stood on the right side of the house, ver. 3.

**V. 19.** *{at the door of the East gate of the LORD'S house}* Namely of the people's court, which was the first coming in of the temple: and so by degrees the Lord and his glory departed more and more from his temple. See chap. 11:22, 23.

**V. 20.** *{And I knew that they were the Cherubims}* The same, though with these small variations.

**V. 22.** *{same faces}* Ver. 14.

## CHAPTER 11

**Verse 1.** *{Moreover}* This chapter which ends the vision, shows God's fierce wrath pursuing the remnant of the wicked desperate idolaters, that should escape out of the destruction of Jerusalem; the promises of God's mercy to the godly penitents among those that were already in captivity in Babylon: and the glory of God finally departing, leaving, and forsaking the temple and city of Jerusalem.

*{The Spirit lift me up}* In vision I thought I was carried up into the air from the priests' court.

*{to the East gate}* Of the court of the people, that chap. 10:19. where the glory of the Lord now was.

*{five and twenty men}* These seem not to be the same that were spoken of, chap. 8:16. But these seem rather to be the senate of Jerusalem, whereof the two here named, were chief. And hither the princes and magistrates did often use to resort, for judgment: as Jer. 26:10. These twenty five, being after that charge given, chap. 9:1, 2, 5, 6, 7. seem to be freed from that judgment in the city; and are sentenced to another judgment afterwards, ver. 7, 8, 9, 10, 11: save that Pelatiah, one of the chief among them, was stricken with present sudden death, ver. 13. for a sign and token of God's wrath against them.

**V. 3.** *{It is not near}* Or, *it is not for us to build houses near.* There is no likelihood that these prophets' predictions, concerning the taking and desolation of Jerusalem, should happen so soon; wherefore without taking any more care about it, let us take our ease; the storm is fallen, and has spent its rage upon the Jews which are already gone into captivity with Jehoiakim. See Ezek. 12:22, 27. Amos 6:3. 2 Pet. 3:4. or rather, if we should build houses near the walls of Jerusalem, the Chaldeans would come, and make use of them against us, and thereby the rather take the city; and so indeed it should become the cauldron, and we the flesh in it. They likely have herein an eye to Jeremy's prophesy, chap. 1:13. though betwixt fear, and scorn.

**V. 4.** *{Prophesy against them}* Namely, in vision, using such gestures, and doings, as if you were in Jerusalem speaking to this people.

**V. 5.** *{And the Spirit}* Chap. 1:3. and 3:14.

*{O house of Israel,}* And chiefly those, chap. 25:1, 2.

**V. 6.** *{ye have multiplied}* This may as well be referred to the murders and violences committed by the inhabitants of the city, as to the occasion which they had given, through their perfidiousness to the king of Babylon, and their obstinacy in not yielding to him according to God's command, to cause a very great number of the people to die by famine and pestilence; and so they made up the flesh for the cauldron.

**V. 7.** *{your slain}* Chap. 24:3. to the 14. Mic. 3:3.

*{they are the flesh}* They indeed shall remain there: but you the chief and heads shall be carried out to the slain, Jer. 39:6. & 52:10, 24, 25, 26, 27.

**V. 8.** *{ye have feared the sword}* Isa. 66:4. Jer. 42:16. Prov. 10:24.

**V. 9.** *{hands of strangers}* That is, of the Chaldeans.

*{among you}* Or, *upon,* or *against you.*

**V. 10.** *{In the border of Israel}* That is in Riblah, 2 Kings 25:6, 21. which city was placed in the land of Hamath, 2 Kings 23:33. and Jer. 39:5. on the northern border of Israel. Num. 34:8. Josh. 13:5. 1 Kings 8:65. See the fulfilling of this prophesy, Jer. 52:10, 24–27.

**V. 11.** *{Shall not be your Cauldron}* That is to say, you shall not remain in it, nor die in it; but in the border of Israel.

**V. 12.** *{for ye have not}* Or, *which have not walked.*

**V. 13.** *{when I prophesied}* The prophet saw this sudden death in vision, as an execution of the foresaid threatenings; whereupon he feared that God would presently destroy everything, and make a full end.

*{fell I down}* Chap. 9:8.

**V. 15.** *{Thy brethren}* They in the captivity in Babylon, are they that are thus wronged and reproached: they that remain still at Jerusalem, thus deem and doom you, as cast off and forsaken of God, who forsook his land and worship, and have nothing now to do with the Lord: and that they remaining in Jerusalem, are alone God's people, and heirs of all the privileges of grace.

**V. 16.** *{Yet I will be to them}* That is, though they want that in Jerusalem, yet they shall have from me really, and in spiritual virtue, that which was figured by the material sanctuary in Jerusalem, namely, the presence of my grace and favor; my protection, comfort, and conduct of my Spirit, etc.

*{a little Sanctuary}* Heb. *little* is after *Sanctuary:* and it may be read, yet will I be to them a sanctuary for a little, (to wit time) in the countries where they are, or shall come to; namely for those 70 years in Babylon, Jer. 25:11. and 29:10. Or, a little sanctuary imports no little blessing of God's favor to them, and protection over them, in the countries where they shall be scattered.

**V. 17.** *{I will even gather you}* Under the corporal return from the captivity, is also understood the spiritual gathering together of the church under the Messias.

**V. 19.** *{I will give them one heart}* Chap. 36:26, 27. Jer. 24:7. and 31:33. and 32:39.

*{a new Spirit}* Psal. 51:12. Eph. 4:23. enlightening the mind, which before was darkness in spiritual things, neither seeing nor able to perceive them; and changing the will which before was enmity against God his grace, neither subject to it, nor could be subject. So God challenges the whole work of man's first conversion to himself.

*{stony heart}* Zach. 7:12, nether millstone meaning the heart whereinto nothing can enter and regenerate them; giving them a will pliable to my word and Spirit, sensible of my motions, and of my acts of mildness, or severity; soft and ready to receive my grace, 2 Cor. 3:3.

**V. 20.** *{will be their God}* Chap. 14:11. and 37:27.

**V. 21.** *{whose heart walketh}* Who cannot turn by any good reason, or sound judgment, from their foolish inclination to idolatry.

*{I will recompense}* Chap. 9:10.

**V. 22.** *{Then did the Cherubims lift up their wings}* And the whole heavenly vision of the glory of the Lord.

**V. 23.** *{from the midst of the City}* Whither it was formerly come from the door of the east gate of the Lord's house, or of the utter court of the Lord's house, chap. 10:19.

*{and stood}* This is God's last step at his departure; by which is signified, that God would yet make one trial more, to see if the people would call him back by invocation, and lively repentance: or, that God being gone out of the city, stood there to be present at, and see the ruin of it. See the promise of his returning upon the selfsame hill, Zach. 14:4.

*{the mountain}* Which is the mount of Olives, 2 Sam. 15:30.

**V. 24.** *{by the Spirit of God}* Not corporally, nor in a dream, and natural imagination, but in a divine and supernatural rapture, caused by the Holy Ghost.

*{went up from me}* As Gen. 17:22.

**V. 25.** *{unto them of the Captivity}* To those elders, that were in some kind, witnesses of this rapture and ecstasy, chap. 8:1. and to the rest of the captivity.

## CHAPTER 12

**Verse 1.** *{The word}* This chapter shadows out the flight, deportation, captivity, and calamity of King Zedekiah, and of the Jews yet remaining in Judea; and avouches the truth and speedy execution of it, against their proverbial scoff, and flout scorn to the contrary. This prophesy, and the rest to the 20 chapter, came to Ezekiel, and were uttered by him, within the space of eleven months and few days, in part of the sixth and seventh year of Jehoiachin's captivity, and Zedekiah's reign, and within three years before the siege began, chap. 8:1. and 20:1. and 24:1. and Jer. 52:4. compared together.

**V. 2.** *{have eyes to see}* Isa. 6:9. and 42:18, 19, 20. Which is taught, and illuminated in the knowledge of my will, but make no use of this gift; but does out of mere malice withstand it, and endeavors, and strives as much as it can to put it out; wherefore the sin is so much the more aggravated, Joh. 9:41. Ephes. 4:18.

**V. 3.** *{prepare thee}* This is a sign or type, whose interpretation is set down, vers. 10, 11.

*{stuff}* Or, *instruments;* as your hat, cloak, shoes, staff, household stuff, etc. Mat. 10:9, 10.

*{It may be}* While there is any hope left, trial shall be made even this way; to convert them, or else convince them the more.

*{They be a rebellious house}* Because they are not touched with your plain ordinary preaching, therefore add this express sign thereunto.

**V. 4.** *{Bring forth thy stuff}* Take all these things which you have prepared, to have them all ready at your need.

*{by day}* That they may be eyewitnesses of it,

*{at even in their sight}* As the fittest time for stealing away by flight, 2 Kings 25:4. Jer. 39:4. and 52:7.

**V. 5.** *{Dig}* The more lively to express their fearful flight.

**V. 6.** *{in the twilight}* Vers. 12. By night, Zedekiah fled through the king's garden, and through the gate, between the two walls; and he went through the fields: the Chaldeans took him at Jericho, brought him to Riblah, condemned him, slew his children before his eyes, and brought him to Babel; but he never saw Babel, being made blind before.

*{Cover thy face}* Vers. 12.

*{a sign unto the house of Israel}* Chap. 4:3. Psal. 71:7. Thus the wheels of God's government appear full of eyes, that so accordingly ruled Zedekiah's case.

**Verse 8.** *{And in the morning}* After he had done that vers. 7.

**V. 9.** *{What doest thou}* Do they not deride your doings? Not ask the meaning in showing this type and figure? Are they so stupid, not so much as to enquire? The false prophets, it seems, had strangely bewitched them, chap. 13. and Jer. 18:1, 2, 3, 4, 10, 11. and chap. 29:8, 9, 15, 21. to the end of the chapter.

**V. 10.** *{This burden}* A prophetical sign of threatening, and calamity, Isa. 13:1. Jerem. 38:23.

*{Prince in Jerusalem}* Namely, Zedekiah.

**Vers. 11.** *{I am your sign}* Esay 7:3, 16. and 8:3, 4, 18. and 20:3.

*{they shall remove}* They in Jerusalem, and in the land of Judea.

*{captivity}* That is, into the Babylonish captivity. See the accomplishment of this, 2 Kings 25.

**V. 12.** *{and the Prince}* Ver. 4, 5, 6. to wit, Zedekiah with his courtiers, shall flee in the nighttime; he in the habit of a poor traveler; his face covered, either that he might not be known; or by reason of sorrow and grief. See 2 Kings 25:4. Jer. 39:4.

**V. 13.** *{My net also will I spread}* When the king shall think to escape by flying, I will take him in my net; as chap. 17:20. (and 32:3.) by sending after him the Chaldean soldiers which shall pursue and catch him, 2 Kings 25:5.

*{yet shall he not see it}* For his eyes were put out at Riblah, 2 Kings 25:7.

*{die there}* In Babylon.

**V. 14.** *{Draw out the sword after them}* Chap. 5:2, 12.

**V. 15.** *{And they shall know}* Even experience shall teach them, the schoolmistress of fools; when they shall see me to be the God of truth, in the executing my judgments upon them, which I now threaten against them: that I do not threaten in vain, but say the word, and perform it; as it is, chap. 12:25, 28.

**V. 16.** *{A few men}* Heb. *men of number;* that is, which may easily be numbered; or few in number; as Gen. 34:30.

*{declare all their abominations}* That is, confessing their sins and idolatries, give testimony for God's judgments against them.

**V. 18.** *{Eat thy bread with quaking}* Another prophetical sign or type: that is in the taking of your food, seem to be an affrighted and perplexed person, chap. 4:16.

**V. 19.** *{people of the land}* Namely, the common sort of the people of Judah, of the city and land.

*{They shall eat their bread with carefulness}* During the distress of the siege; by the means of which, and after which, all the country shall be wasted, according to my decree.

*{all that is therein}* Heb. *the fulnesse thereof.*

**V. 22.** *{the days are prolonged}* Of God's judgments, foretold by the prophets, they shall either be prolonged, or disannulled and brought to nothing. Because they did not immediately see the prophesies accomplished, they contemned them, as they should never be fulfilled; see vers. 27. chapter 11:3. Amos 6:3. 2 Pet. 3:4.

**V. 23.** *{effect of every vision}* That is, the things foretold, and signified by the visions.

**V. 24.** *{There shall be no more}* I will confound and disannul all false prophesies, made according to everyone's fancy, and for to gain favor; by the accomplishment of my true prophesies.

*{vain visions}* Chap. 13:23.

**V. 25.** *{in your days}* Within six years, all these judgments threatened, were executed and accomplished.

**V. 27.** *{for many days to come}* Vers. 22. that is, it shall not come to pass in our days, and therefore we care not for it. Thus the wicked ever abuse God's patience and benignity.

## CHAPTER 13

**Verse 1.** *{And the word}* In this chapter, Ezekiel, from God, prophesies against the false prophets, and prophetesses.

*{of the LORD}* Chap. 1:3. This command from the Lord, which hereafter follows.

*{came unto me}* The manner how, not particularly set down.

**V. 2.** *{out of their own hearts}* And not by God's commission and direction, ver. 3. Jer. 14:14. and 23:9, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17, 21, 22, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30, 31, 32.

**V. 3.** *{follow their own spirit}* Heb. *walk after.* That do meddle with prophesying after their own proper motion, being driven thereunto by their own carnal affections; and declare nothing but their own imaginations, and inventions, fancies and dreams.

**V. 4.** *{Foxes}* Which spoil the Lord's vineyard; as foxes do, Cant. 2:15.

*{deserts}* Heb. *in dry places.* Where no poultry, or other birds are to feed upon; whereupon, being hunger-starved, they gape after prey; and seize upon the means of food, ver. 19.

**V. 5.** *{Ye have not gone up}* Manners of speech taken from sieges, in which the duty of captains are to repair and fortify the place, and then stand to defend it, if there be any breach made. The true strength of the church, is faith, obedience, piety, etc. The defense, is, invocation, intercession, repentance, and zeal to repress the wicked, etc. Psal. 106:23, 30. Chap. 22:30. The false prophets are here reproved, for failing in both these duties.

*{day of the LORD}* Of his judgments.

**V. 6.** *{The word}* Which they falsely feigned, Jer. 28:2, 3, 4, 10, 11.

**V. 9.** *{And mine hand}* See Jer. 20:3, 4. and 28:16. and 29:22, 22, 31, 32.

*{They shall not be}* I will root them out of the world; so that they shall not only be debarred from having any voice or place, in public assemblies, Jer. 26:7. but they shall not so much as be members of the people.

*{Assembly of my people}* Or, *secret,* or, *counsel.*

*{they shall not be written}* Not written in the registers among the number of them, that shall return out of the captivity; nor shall enter and repossess the land, Ezr. 2. and chap. 8. Nehem. 3. and chap. 7. and 10. and 11. and 12.

*{And ye shall know}* See chap. 12:15.

**V. 10.** *{Peace}* They did not as was requisite, ver. 5. See ver. 16. Jer. 6:14. and 8:11. and 7:4, 10.

*{built up a wall}* Whereas the true prophets prophesied the destruction of the city to bring the people to repentance; the false prophets spoke the contrary, and flattered them in their vanities, (Jer. 28:8, 9.) so that what our false prophet said, (which is here called the building of the wall) another false prophet would affirm, though he had neither occasion, nor good ground to bear him out in it.

*{daubed it with untempered morter}* See ver. 15. Chap. 22:18.

**V. 11.** *{an overflowing shower}* Hereby are meant God's extreme judgments.

**V. 12.** *{shall it not be said unto you?}* You will bear the just reproofs of your flattering predictions.

**V. 14.** *{in the midst thereof}* You shall be enfolded in the ruins of it.

*{and ye shall know}* Ver. 9. So ver. 21. and 23.

**V. 15.** *{untempered morter}* Whereby is meant whatsoever man of himself sets forth vainly, under the color and authority of God's word.

*{The wall is no more}* I will presently destroy both the wall and the daubers thereof.

**V. 17.** *{Against the daughters}* Women that were false prophetesses; that look so much upon them contrary to the modesty of their sex, and to the truth of God. Indeed God raised up *Miriam*, Exod. 15. *Debora*, Judg. 4:5. *Hulda*, 2 Kings 22. 2 Chron. 32. *Annah*, Luk. 2:36. *Philip's* daughters, Act. 21:9. and the Devil would never be behind to raise up his like instruments; as these here; as Noadiah in the days of Nehemiah, Neh. 6:14. Jezebel in the church of Thyatira, Apoc. 2. women laden with sins, led away with divers lusts, 2 Tim. 3:6.

*{their own heart}* See ver. 6.

**V. 18.** *{That sew pillows}* They did use these signs and ceremonies after the manner of the prophets: and it should seem that the pillows were a sign of peace and ease for the one; and the veils, of mourning and calamity for the other; according to the customs of covering their faces in like cases, 2 Sam. 15:30. and 19:4. Esth. 6:12. and 7:8. Job 9:24. And to this, that which is spoken, ver. 19. and 22. seems to have relation.

*{To all}* Indifferently to whom they please, without regarding either God's will, or the worth, or the unworthiness of men.

*{armholes}* Or, *elbows.*

*{and make kerchiefs}* Head-tires, veils, bound to the heads, and to cover the faces.

*{every stature}* Namely, of all conditions and qualities of men, great and small, young and old, to fit all sorts.

*{hunt souls}* To ensnare them in error; and consequently in perdition.

**V. 19.** *{pollute me}* Making me the author and maintainer of your lies, and deceits; without any respect to my most holy name.

*{handfuls of barley}* For any light reward, Mic. 3:5.

*{slay the souls}* Denouncing death and unhappy chances to good men, and raising persecution against them among the people; and contrariwise promising life and prosperity to wicked men, and so some way defending them against the justice of men.

**V. 20.** *{Behold, I am against your pillows}* I will quickly cause you and your false ceremonies to perish.

*{To make them fly}* Or, *into gardens,* or, *groves,* for spiritual and corporal idolatries.

**V. 22.** *{heart of the righteous sad}* By threatening them that were godly, and upholding the wicked.

*{strengthened the hands of the wicked}* Jer. 23:14.

*{by promising him life}* Or, *that I should save his life.* Heb. *by quickening him.*

**V. 23.** *{See no more vanity}* I will root you out, that you may no more seduce my people, with your deceits, chap. 12:24.

## CHAPTER 14

**Verse 1.** *{Then came}* This chapter shows the idolatry and impiety of the Jews; and denounces God's judgments against them and their false prophets; and that by an irrevocable decree.

*{came}* To enquire of the Lord, ver. 7. Chap. 20:1.

*{Certain of the elders of Israel}* Namely, of the heads of the people that were in Babylon.

*{sat before me}* In Babylon. Therefore these seem to be elders that were already captives in Babylon. Indeed some learned men take these to be certain elders, not yet in captivity, but sent as ambassadors from King Zedekiah to Nebuchadnezzar, or prime men in the ambassadors' company: and this the rather, because the matter and contents of this chapter seem best to agree to the Jews in Jewry, under the reign of Zedekiah, not yet captivated. But that ambassage being in the fourth year of Zedekiah; and this being two years after, as appears by Jer. 28:1. and 29:2. and 51:49. and Ezek. 8:1. and 20:1. compared together, makes that opinion the less probable. However this their gesture and demeanor shows the hypocrisy of the idolaters, who will dissemble to hear the prophets of God, though in their hearts they follow nothing less than their admonitions, and also how by one means or other, God does discover them.

**V. 3.** *{Idols}* The word signifies dung: such are idols, base things, or dunghill deities: and as dung offends a man's eyes and nose, so do idols offend God; yet idolaters will make their hearts temples for them.

*{and put the stumbling block}* They are not only idolaters in heart, but also worship their filthy idols openly; seeking out objects and baits fitting to nourish and kindle that wicked inclination to idolatry, and catching occasion to fulfill it. See Zeph. 1:3. or they themselves by their sins will be the causers of their own ruin, chap. 18:30.

*{should I be enquired of at all by them?}* Being such, how dare they once appear before me, to enquire counsel of me? Is it not a mere hypocrisy and scoff in them, to come to me to ask for help and counsel, in their distress, seeing their hearts are separate from me, and they being the only causers of the evils which they suffer? Should I answer them when they seek me?

**V. 4.** *{Prophet}* To enquire of things which the Lord has appointed to come to pass.

*{according to the multitude of his idols}* Not answering him to what he asks; but to speak only as his abomination has deserved: his idols shall not shut up or close my mouth, but they shall rather open it against his idolatries, ver. 5. and to denounce the sentence of punishment against him for it.

**V. 5.** *{That I may take}* That is, my answers shall be like so many snares, or things, to their consciences convinced by my word, will they, nill they; and thereby will I keep them, as it were, shut up in expectation and fear of my unavoidable judgments: or, I will give them over to the delusion of their own hearts, as their idols lead them, 2 Thess. 2:11, 12.

**V. 7.** *{stranger that sojourneth}* Strangers and sojourners not to be tolerated in their idolatry, Exod. 20:10.

*{separateth himself}* Like unto a harlot that separates herself from her husband. See Hos. 4:14, 40. and 9:10.

*{by myself}* Not according to his design that asks the question, nor merely and solely according to the prophet's pleasure: but I will take the matter upon me myself, and I will answer according to my most holy truth and justice; as in the verse following.

**V. 8.** *{Set my face}* Chap. 15:7. Lev. 17:10.

*{a sign and a proverb}* Deut. 28:37. Chap. 5:15. namely, for a spectacle of my judgments, of whom everybody shall speak.

*{Cut him off}* Some for example.

*{ye shall know}* See chap. 12:15.

**V. 9.** *{If the Prophet be deceived}* God, for man's ingratitude, raises up false prophets, to seduce them that delight in lies, rather than in the truth of God; and thus without sin, in justice he punishes sin by sin, 1 Kings 22:20, 22. and destroys as well those prophets, as that people, ver. 10. See Job 12:16. Jer. 4:10.

*{when he hath spoken a thing}* Or, *word,* to please them; *placentia.*

*{I the LORD have deceived}* 1 Kings 22:vers. 23. 2 Thess. 2:11, 12. Rom. 1:24. He deceives him by suffering him to be deceived by their rewards; as judges sometimes are by bribes; and birds, and fishes by baits: by withdrawing also his *spirit*, and restraining grace from him; and by ordering wisely and justly such occasions, and occurrences as work upon his corruption.

*{I will stretch forth mine hand}* Though he does but what I by my secret providence, have suffered him to do, yet will I punish him, because he sins against my law, which is the rule of human actions.

**V. 10.** *{And they shall bear}* Both the false prophet whom God makes use of in seducing of others; and the seduced; though they would plead for themselves, and lay the fault upon their seducers; yet God will punish both.

**V. 11.** *{Israel may go no more astray}* Thus God's judgments against the wicked, are admonitions to the godly to cleave unto the Lord; and not to defile themselves with the like abomination.

**V. 12.** *{Land sinneth}* The meaning is, when I have once resolved to punish a land, I will not forbear to do it for any manner of intercession: all intercession were but lost labor, the final decree being once past, Jer. 7:16. and 11:14. and 14:11.

*{Then will I stretch}* Or, *and I stretch.*

*{and will break}* Or, *and break.*

*{staff of bread}* Chap. 4:16. and 5:16. Lev. 26:26. Esay 3:1.

*{and will send}* Or, *and send.*

*{and will cut}* Or, *and cut.*

**V. 14.** *{Though these three men}* A case supposed; as Jer. 5:1. and 15:1. to show, his sentence here to be irrevocable, his decree unalterable (as those of the Medes and Persians; himself inflexible, inexorable.

*{Noah, Daniel, and Job}* Jer. 15:1. *Moses* and *Samuel* are named in the like case. *Daniel* a man here of this singular note and eminency, though living now among them in the captivity, and being carried away young, about 14 years at the most before this time, 2 Kings 24:1. Jer. 25:1. Ezek. 20:1. Dan. 1:1. and 2:1. compared together. See Ezek. 28:3.

*{were in it}* Living, supposing the other two, were living as well as *Daniel* now was.

*{their own souls}* Neither sons nor daughters, ver. 16, 18, 20.

*{by their righteousness}* Though in other cases, the effectual fervent prayer of one righteous man avails much, Jam. 5:16. And the sentence against Sodom was revocable, if ten righteous persons could have been found in it, Gen. 18:32. and Jerusalem itself, in some part of Josiah's time, would have been spared and pardoned, if a just man could have been found in it, Jer. 5:1. But here now the decree was past, an end was come, chap. 7:2, 4, 5, 6, 10. However no place here for the prayers of the dead: nor for the pretended works of supererogation, which the Papists imagine: the meaning in this place is, that a very few (which he calls a remnant, ver. 22.) should escape these plagues, whom God has sanctified and made righteous: so that this righteousness is a sign that they are the church of God, whom he would preserve for his own sake.

**V. 19.** *{In blood}* The pestilence by corruption of the blood, is death's bloody and speedy executioner.

**V. 21.** *{How much more}* That is, I do allege all these examples of particular scourges, to conclude, that I shall much less be entreated, when I shall resolve to join them all together, for a deluge of evils, which are brought to the height.

*{four sore judgments}* Chap. 5:17.

**V. 22.** *{A remnant}* Chap. 5:3.

*{come forth unto you}* Within a very short time they shall be brought hither to Babylon into captivity, like as you are.

*{comforted concerning the evil}* When you shall know their grievous sins, you will have cause to acknowledge God's justice, and strengthen yourselves against the scandal of this destruction, and give glory to God; and likewise be comforted in your captivity, knowing these far greater evils and miseries which they in Jerusalem and Judea had suffered and endured.

**V. 23.** *{when ye shall see their ways, and their doings}* The just rewards of their evil works; as the words following do show.

## CHAPTER 15

**Verse 1.** *{And the word}* This short chapter again sets forth God's heavy judgment, against Jerusalem and the land of Judea; in a type, and the exposition of it.

**V. 2.** *{what is the Vine tree more}* As if he should say, what is there in the Jews at Jerusalem, and in the land of Judea: yet under the reign of King Zedekiah, why they should so vaunt, and soothe up themselves? or why I should so spare them?

**V. 3.** *{shall wood}* The wood of the vine, which is the thing here brought in comparison, is of less use than other wood; not good to make so much as a pin of it; nor for any other use, but fit for the fire.

**V. 4.** *{It is meet for any work?}* Much less is it meet when it is half burnt: so it is expounded in the next verse, Joh. 15:6.

**V. 6.** *{As the Vine tree}* The explication of the former type.

**V. 7.** *{And I will set my face}* See chap. 14:8. Levit. 17:10.

*{and another fire}* They shall go out of one fiery judgment into another: as 1 Kings 19:17. Amos 5:19. Jer. 48:43, 44.

**V. 8.** *{Committed a trespass}* Heb. *trespassed a trespasse,* chap. 14:13.

## CHAPTER 16

**Verse 1.** *{Again}* In this chapter the prophet sets down the history of God's dealing with the church of the Jews from first to last, under a continued allegory of a wretched maid, miserable in her birth and infancy, unpitied and cast away; till God took care of her life and preservation, caused her to grow up to comeliness and beauty, and by covenant took her unto himself: after which he bestowed all manner of rich and precious blessings upon her: till she became renowned through his comeliness which he had put upon her. But she then shamefully abused all his blessings, and played the whore in all abominable manners: whereupon he pours out his wrath upon her even by her idolatrous lovers: and recounting her sins above Samaria's, and Sodom's, he awards her a condign punishment, and yet adds, in the end of all, evangelical promises of repentance, grace and mercy in after times.

**V. 2.** *{Cause Jerusalem to know}* Namely, the remnant of the Jews which are in Jerusalem, which represent the whole body of the nation and state, as being the seat of the church and head of the kingdom.

**V. 3.** *{Thy birth}* Heb. *Cutting out;* or, *habitation.* Though you be of Abraham's race, yet you are so degenerate, that you deserve rather to be called Amorites and Hittites which were two accursed nations, ver. 45. Gen. 26:34, 35. and 27:46. See Isa. 1:10. Joh. 8:33, 37, 40, 44. Rom. 2:28. and 9:7, 8.

**V. 4.** *{Thy nativity in the day thou wast born}* Hos. 2:3. An allegory, continued in all this chapter; wherein the people's estate, both spiritual and temporal, is represented by a wretched maid; after fostered, married, exalted, and enriched through mere grace. If their *nativity* be referred to the time when it came to some form and perfection of a nation; and was brought from Egypt, the multitude of Israel, then forsook not the idols of Egypt, Ezek. 20:8. But more likely it is to be referred to the calling of Abram their father out of Ur of the Chaldees, where, he likely, and his ancestors sure, were idolaters, Josh. 24:2, 14, 15.

*{thy navel was not cut}* Terms taken from that which used to be done to little children newly born, to show, that the people had no human help or assistance, but were utterly forsaken in their misery.

*{thou wast not salted at all}* Salt being used about these little creatures, to bathe and rub them, to dry up, cleanse and strengthen the body of the child.

**V. 6.** *{Polluted in thine own blood}* Heb. or, *troden under foot.* Being thus in the filthiness, and forsaken of all men, I took you, and gave you life; whereby is meant, that before God wash his church, and give it life, there is nothing but filthiness and death.

*{yea I said}* God often repeated his promise to Abraham, Gen. 12. and 13. and 15. and 17. and 18. and 22. chapters.

**V. 7.** *{I have caused thee to multiply}* Heb. *Made thee a million.*

*{To multiply}* According to my promise, Gen. 12:2. and 13:16. and 15:5. and 17:2, 4. and 22:17. See the accomplishment of it, Exod. 1:7. Deut. 1:10.

*{excellent ornaments}* Heb. *ornament of ornaments.*

*{thy breasts are fashioned}* He continues the same figure of a maid, that is now come to age to have a husband: to signify the time appointed by God to set his people at liberty, and honor them with the title of his church, and to make his covenant with them in Horeb, Cant. 8:8. Hos. 2:15.

*{naked and bare}* Without any honor, or defense; as ver. 4, 5, 6. yea even in Egypt you were in misery and captivity.
