**V. 8.** *{was the time of love}* For me to marry you: that time foretold, Gen. 15:13. yet the true cause of this love is set down, Deut. 7:7, 8.

*{I spread my skirt over thee}* I took you to wife, Ruth 3:9. Gen. 20:16. This seems to be an ancient ceremony, wherein the husband, in sign of the right and property which he obtained in his wife, and for a pledge of his duty of protection and love, did when he married her, cover her with the skirt of his garment. Figuring here the righteousness, innocency, and merit of Christ, which hides all the blemishes of his church from his sight, and protects her; and hereby shows himself to be her head, Lord, and husband: which has at all times been the foundation and sum of the covenant of grace.

*{and covered thy nakedness}* These words, as blood, pollution, nakedness, and filthiness, are oftentimes repeated to beat down their pride, and to cause them to consider what they were before God received them to mercy, favored them, and covered their shame.

*{I sware unto thee}* I made a solemn covenant with you, that I would take you to be my people, Exod. 19. and 24 chapters. Jer. 2:2.

**V. 9.** *{Then washed I thee with water}* Corporally, I took away from you all signs and tokens of misery, and of your former oppression; and enriched you with my gifts and benefits: and spiritually, I purged you from your sins, which are the uncleanness of your soul, in which man is born; and endued you with the graces of my *spirit*, signified by the anointing.

*{blood}* Heb. *bloods.*

**V. 10.** *{Badgers' skin}* Which were some way neatly dressed to make handsome shoes.

**V. 11.** *{I decked}* I furnished you with all things necessary, and delightful; with grace, and glory.

**V. 12.** *{Thy forehead}* Heb. *thy nose.* See touching this kind of ornament, Gen. 24:47. Isa. 3:21.

*{Crown upon thine head}* Hereby he shows how he saved his church, enriched it, and gave it power and dominion to reign.

**V. 13.** *{Prosper into a Kingdom}* That is, did grow up so high, that you became a great and glorious kingdom.

**V. 14.** *{Thy renown}* 2 Sam. 8:13. 1 Kings 4:31.

*{Comeliness which I put upon thee}* He declares wherein the dignity of Jerusalem stood, to wit, in that the Lord gave them of his beauty and excellency.

**V. 15.** *{But thou didst trust}* Here comes in her lewdness; you are become presumptuous and bold, by reason of the gifts received from me, and would be no longer subject to me, nor contain yourself within the chastity of my service and obedience, but did choose to live a loose life.

*{playedst the harlot}* In the times of the judges and kings; but specially for these three hundred and ninety years, mentioned chap. 4:5. See chap. 2:2. Isa. 57:8. Jer. 2:20. and 3:26, 20. You have joined yourself also by unlawful covenants, and by imitation of idolatry to profane people, which you have drawn to you by the greatness of your state, and the preeminency which you had above other nations.

*{pouredst out}* You have indifferently, and impudently prostituted yourself. See chap. 16:36. Jer. 3:13.

*{his it was}* He might satisfy his lust as he would, for you gave him free liberty to do it.

**V. 16.** *{High places}* Namely, the altars, chapels, and temples of your idols, garnished with ornaments and rich tapestry, which I had bestowed upon you for your own use, Hos. 2:8.

**V. 17.** *{of men}* Heb. *of a male.* Namely of idols, which were as adulterers to the idolatrous souls; though there were many female idols also.

**V. 18.** *{Coveredst them}* Thus idols clothed, served, and worshipped.

**V. 19.** *{a sweet savour}* Heb. *a savour of rest:* burning those offerings, as it were, to please and appease the idols; as God had appointed they should do to him.

*{Thus it was}* All these things were notoriously known and verified; and they are not to be denied, nor excused.

**V. 20.** *{whom thou}* Who at their birth were mine, by virtue of my covenant, whereby the whole body of the nation was a wife to me, and the particular persons as children, wherefore you should have consecrated them to me.

*{And these hast thou sacrificed}* Chap. 20:26. 2 Kings 16:3. and 17:17. and 21:6. Psal. 106:37. Isa. 57:5.

*{to be devoured}* Heb. *to devour:* meaning by fire, read Lev. 18:21. 2 Kings 3:27. and 23:10. Jer. 7:31.

**V. 22.** *{in all thine abominations}* Your unbridled idolatry has been accompanied with an infamous ingratitude, and presumptuous confidence, that you could no more fall into your former misery.

**V. 24.** *{Thou hast also}* Like to an unchaste woman, who after she has used dishonesty privately with some particular men, does also prostitute herself publicly in a brothel house; whereby is meant idolatry which is commonly practiced, and allowed by public authority. See Esay 3:9.

*{eminent place}* Or, *brothel-house.*

*{and hast made thee an high place}* Isa. 57:5, 7. Jer. 2:20, 23. and 3:2, 6. and 13:27.

**V. 25.** *{Thou hast built}* Prepared all accommodations for your notorious and raging idolatry.

**V. 26.** *{with the Egyptians}* He notes the great impiety with this people, who first falling from God, to seek help from strange nations, (Esay 30. and 31. 2 Kings 24. & 25.) did also at length embrace their idolatry, thinking thereby to make their amity more strong. See chap. 8:10, 14. and 23:19.

*{great of flesh}* A figurative term taken from the shameless desires of lascivious unchaste women, chap. 23:20. to signify that the power and wealth of Egypt did entice the people to desire to adhere, and to be linked unto them.

**V. 27.** *{have diminished thine ordinary food}* I have weakened your estate, and taken away the abundance of my blessings from you.

*{the daughters of the Philistines}* 2 Chron. 28:18.

*{Are ashamed}* They detest and abhor your unconstant and wavering idolatry; they holding themselves constant to their ancient idolatry, which was first established. See Jer. 2:10, 11, 33.

**V. 28.** *{with the Assyrians}* Chap. 23:12. 2 Kings 16:7, 10. 2 Chron. 28:23. Jer. 2:18, 36.

**V. 29.** *{of Canaan unto Chaldea}* Chap. 23:14, 15, 16, 17. ever and anon since that time, 2 Kings 20:12. Esay 39.

**V. 30.** *{weak is thine heart}* Weakened and melted in spiritual lust; which works the same effect in the souls, as bodily lust does in the bodies; extinguishing all manner of true virtue in them, and effeminating them to a base and sensual esteem of God and his service, and weakening the true worship of him in spirit and truth.

*{an imperious whorish woman}* That is to say, licentious, unbridled, and incorrigible; that takes liberty to do what she pleases.

**V. 31.** *{Thou scornest hire}* As much as to say, you have not been sought after, nor solicited, nor rewarded, nor prayed; but you yourself have solicited and paid your adulterers, which in a woman, is the extreme of impudency. See 2 Kings 16:7, 8, 10. 2 Chron. 28:21, 22, 23. Isa. 30:6. Hos. 8:9.

**V. 34.** *{And in that thou givest}* Nobody has desired nor solicited you. So is the sin of the people aggravated, who without anybody's inducement of their own proper motion, were run into idolatry.

**V. 35.** *{O harlot hear}* The denunciation of judgment, and condign punishment upon her.

**V. 36.** *{Thy filthiness}* Heb. *Thy poyson,* Jer. 2:23, 24. The meaning is, you have sinned without shame, and I will punish you with infamy and disgrace, even by them with whom you have sinned.

**V. 37.** *{All thy Lovers}* The Babylonians, and other nations in their armies, whom you took to be your lovers, they shall come and destroy you, chap. 23:9, 22, 23, 24, 28.

*{and all them}* Assyrians, Egyptians, etc.

*{hated}* Chap. 23:18.

*{and will discover}* Chap. 23:29. Jer. 13:22, 26. Lam. 1:8. Hos. 2:10.

**V. 38.** *{as, &c.}* Heb. *with judgments of.*

*{break wedlock}* I will judge you to death as adulteresses, Lev. 20:10. Deut. 22:22. and murderers, Gen. 9:6. Exod. 21:12. deserve.

*{shed blood}* That do murder the children which they do bring forth, ver. 36.

**V. 39.** *{thine eminent place}* Namely, the city Jerusalem itself, or the whole state where idolatry had been publicly practiced, and without control, ver. 24, 31.

*{Thy fair jewels}* Heb. *instruments of thine ornaments*, ver. 10, etc.

**V. 40.** *{stone thee}* He has relation to the punishment appointed for adulteresses by the law, Deut. 22:24.

**V. 41.** *{they shall burn}* 2 Kings 25:9. 2 Chron. 36:19. Jer. 52:13.

*{in the sight of many women}* Namely, of many kind of people, and nations, whereof the Chaldean army shall be composed; of all the nations round about, that you shall be an example, or rather a mockingstock unto them, Psal. 137:7. Lam. 1:7, 21. Ezek. 25:3, 6, 8. and 26:2.

**V. 42.** *{my jealousy}* When it is fully satisfied with revenge upon you.

**V. 43.** *{Thou hast not remembered}* Ver. 22. your first condition, and my first dealing with you, ver. 4, etc.

*{fretted me}* Thus God takes on for idolatry.

**V. 44.** *{Proverb}* Taunting proverb.

*{so is her daughter}* As were the Canaanites, and the Hittites, (who were taxed with serving of devils, and sacrificing their children to them, Levit. 18:21, 24. Deut. 18:9, 10.) and others your predecessors; so are you their successors, you do not degenerate from them.

**V. 45.** *{thy mother's daughter}* She an Hittite, in the end of this verse.

*{her husband}* Namely, God the creator of all, and lawgiver to all, whose knowledge and service these nations utterly forsook, to worship devils; and so did she.

*{thy sisters}* That is, of Samaria and Sodom. You, and Samaria, (that is, the ten tribes) and Sodom, are like one another in sinning, as if you were one and the selfsame mother's daughters, Deut. 32:32. Isa. 1:10. Jer. 3:8.

*{your mother was an Hittite}* Vers. 3.

**V. 46.** *{Thine elder sister}* Having more tribes, and a larger country.

*{at thy left hand}* That is northward: for the Hebrews call the forepart the east, and the hinder part the west, the left side the north, and the right side the south: supposing the posture of a man with his face toward the east, as has been said, Exod. 26:18. Southward, or on the right hand.

*{thy younger sister}* Heb. *lesser then thou.*

*{at thy right hand}* Southward, Exod. 26:18.

**V. 47.** *{their abominations}* You have not been content with imitating and equaling them, but have gone beyond them; as is further avowed in the verses following.

*{as if that were a very little thing}* Or, *that was loathed as a small thing.*

**V. 48.** *{As I live}* God confirms it by an oath.

*{Sodom thy sister}* Mat. 10:15. and 11:24.

*{hath not done}* Sodom wanted all spiritual means of grace, Matth. 11:23.

**V. 49.** *{Pride}* Sodom's sins here recorded are only against the second table. Out of that plenty, Gen. 13:10. and after that Abram had rescued them from their enemies, Gen. 14. she had gotten none but temporal excellencies from me, and not the spiritual ones of my word and covenant, as you had: and besides, her greatest sin was but against the second table, and not against the first, as yours is in violating my service.

*{strengthen the hand}* See their sins against the two angels, and Lot, Gen. 19.

**V. 50.** *{before me}* Gen. 13:13.

*{I took them away}* Both Sodom, yea and Samaria, Gen. 19:24. 2 Kings 17.

*{as I saw}* Or, *after I had seen;* to wit, their abominations, Gen. 18:21.

**V. 51.** *{Neither hath Samaria}* Which worshipped the calves in Dan and Bethel.

*{half of thy sins}* Because you received more favors at my hands, the true priesthood being in Judah, and had been longer suffered, exhorted, and corrected; and have seen my judgments upon the other: wherefore your ingratitude, rebellion, and obstinacy, cannot be equalized, chap. 23:11. Jer. 3:6, 7, 8.

*{hast justified thy sisters}* Made their sins to seem but little ones, and excusable, by the excess of yours; and them innocent in comparison of you, Jer. 3:11.

**V. 52.** *{judged thy sisters}* Condemned them without any pity as wicked, and not deserving any mercy.

*{bear thine own shame}* Mat. 10:15. and 11:24. Lam. 4:6.

**V. 53.** *{when I shall bring}* That is, as I will never reestablish those cities and nations into their former estate; so the Jewish nation, whatsoever their false prophets say, yet shall never be perfectly restored after the ruin which shall come upon them by the Chaldeans; in respect of the glory of the temple, and the state of the kingdom, and other advantages. But as concerning the spiritual good, the people had always a remainder of grace, and hope of restorement to salvation, vers. 60, etc. which the other nations had not, Isa. 1:9.

*{the captivity}* This word is taken for all kinds of extreme misery, Job 42:10.

*{the captivity of thy Captives}* Namely, free them from servitude, and other continued and successive calamities.

**V. 54.** *{A Comfort}* Chap. 14:22. A common kind of speech; as if those other nations had any way been eased, by seeing the Jews as much, or more punished than they were, Lam. 4:6.

**V. 55.** *{Then thou}* As vers. 53.

**V. 56.** *{mentioned}* Heb. *for a report,* or, *hearing.*

*{by thy mouth}* You would not call her punishment to mind, when you were aloft, to learn by her example to fear my judgments.

*{pride}* Heb. *prides,* or, *excellencies.*

**V. 57.** *{discovered}* Till you were brought under by the Syrians and Philistines, 2 Chron. 28:18, 19. Or thus, you were not convinced in your conscience, of your obstinacy and wickedness, when your sister Samaria was reproachfully handled by the Syrians and Philistines, which should have been a lesson unto you.

*{Syria}* Heb. *Aram.*

*{about her}* Which joined with the Syrians, or compassed about Jerusalem.

*{despise thee}* Would have spoiled you, but for my preserving of you, Esay 7. and 8. chapters.

**V. 59.** *{the oath}* Joined with an execration, or curse, in case of failing. By which oath you had bound yourself to me, as a people to their king, and a wife to her husband; as vers. 8.

**V. 60.** *{Nevertheless I will remember my Covenant}* I will pity you, and so stand to my covenant of mercy and love; though you have deserved the contrary, Gen. 8:1. and 9:15, 16. Exod. 6:5.

*{I will establish}* Jer. 31:31, 32. Hos. 2:19.

*{an everlasting covenant}* Namely, a spiritual covenant, made with the true Israel in spirit.

**V. 61.** *{Then thou shalt remember}* Chap. 20:43. You shall be touched with true compunction and repentance.

*{when thou shalt receive}* Namely, when I shall convert both Israel, and the Gentiles, and make them members of the church, (and perform that great promise, Gen. 9:27.) whose body was represented by the old Jewish nation, which was also first employed in the preaching of the gospel.

*{elder and thy younger}* Samaria, Sodom; Jew, and Gentile.

*{for daughters}* Gal. 4:26. See Esay 54. and 55. chapters.

*{but not by thy Covenant}* Not by virtue of the old, external, and outward covenant, in which the natural Jews had only part, or such as joined themselves to their religion and ceremonies; but by virtue of the new, spiritual, and eternal one, grounded upon the Messias, and upon his righteousness, redemption and Spirit, which is made with the whole Israel, according to faith, Jer. 31:31, 32, 33, 34. 2 Cor. 3:3. Gal. 4:24, 25. Heb. 8:8.

**V. 63.** *{That thou mayst remember}* These things are here written and set down, to that end, that after your reestablishment, especially in the time of the gospel, you may give God all the glory for your deliverance, by a sincere acknowledgment and confession of your sins, approving of, and adoring in silence those judgments, which had been formerly inflicted upon you, Job 5:16. Rom. 3:19.

## CHAPTER 17

**Verse 1.** *{And the word of the LORD}* The prophet under the parable of two eagles and a vine, sets forth the taking of Jeconiah from the kingdom, and the placing of Zedekiah in his room, by the king of Babylon: Zedekiah's rebellion by relying upon the king of Egypt: God's severe threatening of him, and his adherents for the same. And lastly, concludes with evangelical promises of Christ, and of the largeness and benefits of his kingdom.

**V. 2.** *{Put forth a riddle}* Or, parable; or a figurative speech, representing one thing by another, with a convenient correspondency.

*{the house of Israel}* Principally is here meant the kingdom of Judah.

**V. 3.** *{A great Eagle}* That is, Nebuchadnezzar, Jer. 48:40. and 49:22. who has great power, riches, and many countries under him, shall come to Jerusalem, and take away Jeconiah the king, as ver. 12.

*{divers Colours}* Heb. *embroidering.* Hereby is meant Nebuchadnezzar's empire over many nations of divers customs, and languages; or his army composed of several sorts of people.

*{unto Lebanon}* To Judea, which is oftentimes likened to a wood of excellent trees.

*{highest branch}* Namely, Jehoiachin, led into captivity, 2 Kings 24:12.

**V. 4.** *{He cropped off}* Hereby is signified the captivity of the chief of the kingdom, led away with Jehoiachin, ver. 12.

*{merchants}* Or, *traffick.* He means Babylon, famous for merchandise and traffic.

**V. 5.** *{of the seed}* Seed royal; namely, Zedekiah, the uncle of Jeconiah, who was of the blood royal. See annotations upon vers. 13.

*{planted it in a fruitful field}* Heb. *put it in a field of seed;* to wit, in the kingdom of Judea, not yet made desolate, where Zedekiah was made king instead of Jeconiah, 2 Kings 24:17. Jer. 37:1. and might have maintained himself, and grown prosperously.

*{and set it as a willow tree}* Or, *with most curious circumspection;* that is, with all cautions and conditions, oath and pledges, to make firm the covenant between him, that is, Nebuchadnezzar, and Zedekiah. See ver. 13, 14.

**V. 6.** *{And it grew}* Zedekiah prospered while he was faithful to the Chaldeans.

*{of low stature}* Signifying that he was but a vassal, not sovereign, nor absolute; that he might not have power to rebel against Babylon; as ver. 14.

*{whose branches}* Being a dependant, and subject to Nebuchadnezzar.

*{the roots}* That is, he stayed in his own country, and was not transported into any other place; and the state remained in its ancient form and seat: only the king's power was diminished, and subjected to the king of Babylon.

*{brought forth branches}* Zedekiah begat and brought up children, Jer. 52:10.

**V. 7.** *{another great Eagle}* Namely, the king of Egypt, who was also a mighty king, with whom Zedekiah joined himself against Nebuchadnezzar, ver. 15, 17. 2 Kings 24:20. Jer. 52:3.

*{This Vine}* King Zedekiah.

*{her roots}* By secret embassies, ver. 15.

*{water it}* That is, uphold and relieve him with his forces, against the Chaldeans. See Jer. 37:7.

*{the furrows}* He alludes to the channels and pipes wherewith the Egyptians conveyed the waters of Nilus to their land. See Deut. 11:10.

**V. 8.** *{it was planted}* There was no cause for Zedekiah to rebel, and fly to Egypt for succor; for he prospered well under the Babylonian; as ver. 6. Or, though it were very likely that Zedekiah and Judah, by this league with Egypt, might have maintained and bettered himself, and rid himself from subjection to Nebuchadnezzar; thinking to be moistened, by the waters of Nilus; (thence to get chariots and horses to keep them from Babylon) yet in vain did they resist: one set up a conqueror for their sins, when they daily more and more provoked God against them.

*{soil}* Heb. *field.*

**V. 9.** *{Shall he not pull}* Shall not Nebuchadnezzar the great eagle destroy it? ver. 3.

*{without great power}* He shall not need a great army to subdue King Zedekiah, for God will deliver him into his hand for violating his oath. The King Nebuchadnezzar himself was not there with all his army; but his princes took Jerusalem and brought Zedekiah the king to Riblah, Jer. 37:7, 8, 9, 10. and 52:7, 8, 9.

**V. 10.** *{East-wind}* East wind is hurtful to vines, Jonah 4:8. By this tempestuous and scorching wind, is meant God's judgments executed by the means of the Chaldeans, chap. 19:12. Hos. 13:15.

*{furrows}* Notwithstanding Egypt's assistance and relief.

**V. 12.** *{The King thereof}* That is, Jehoiachin, ver. 2, 3. 2 Kings 24:12, 15. who was called Jeconiah, 1 Chr. 3:16. and Coniah, Jer. 22:24. and 37:1.

**V. 13.** *{of the king's seed}* To wit, Mattaniah, whose name was changed to Zedekiah, 2 Kings 24:17. who was son to King Josiah, 1 Chron. 3:15. yet he is called son to Jeconiah, 1 Chron. 3:16. not by nature, for he was uncle; but son, by succession in the kingdom: also Zedekiah is called Jeconiah's brother, 2 Chron. 36:10. by general term of kindred.

*{taken an oath of him}* Heb. *brought him to an oath,* ver. 16. 2 Chr. 36:13. for his subjection and obedience.

*{the mighty of the Land}* That is, he has carried them away with him, to weaken the kingdom, and for to have hostages by him.

**V. 14.** *{But that by keeping of his Covenant, it might stand}* Heb. *to keep his Covenant, to stand to it.*

**V. 16.** *{But he rebelled}* 2 Kings 24:20.

*{horses}* Isa. 31:1, 3.

**V. 16.** *{in the midst of Babylon he shall die}* Jer. 32:5. and 34:3. Chap. 12:13.

**V. 17.** *{Make for him}* He shall do Zedekiah no good, who was straitly besieged by Nebuchadnezzar, Jer. 37:5, 7, 8.

*{by casting up mounts}* Chap. 4:2. Jer. 52:4.

**V. 18.** *{He had given his hand}* Because he took the name of God in vain, and broke his oath, which he had confirmed by giving his hand, therefore the prophet declares, that God would not suffer such perjury and infidelity to escape punishment. See Jer. 50:15. Job 17:3.

**V. 19.** *{Mine oath}* Namely, the punishment for breaking the oath he had made in my name. God calls it his oath, though made to an heathen king. See Josh. 9:19. 2 Sam. 21:1.

**V. 20.** *{I will spread my net upon him}* Chap. 12:13. and 32:3.

**V. 21.** *{and all his fugitives}* Chap. 12:14. which fled with him, Jer. 39:4.

*{They that remain}* As we see in Gedaliah, Johanan, and those that fled into Egypt, Jer. 41, 42, 43, 44. chapters.

**V. 22.** *{I will also take}* Promises of restoring and enlarging of the church in Christ. This begun to be put in execution in Zerubbabel, who was of the blood royal, and brought the people out of Babylon; but the perfect accomplishment is in Christ, the everlasting King, and Son of David, Isa. 11:1.

*{a tender one}* Hereby are meant Christ's weak beginnings in his human nature, who was descended from the ancient stock of the kings of Judah.

*{And will plant it upon a high mountain}* That is, God would set this king upon his *holy hill of Sion*, Psal. 2:6. and over his church.

**V. 23.** *{of the height of Israel}* Namely, in my church which spiritually is higher than any worldly height, chap. 20:40. Isa. 2:2, 3. Mic. 4:1.

*{and under it}* The Israelites, and all the tribes, and spiritually the elect of all countries, both Jews and Gentiles, shall come under the Messias, to shelter themselves from all evils, Isa. 66:23.

**V. 24.** *{All the trees}* All the great ones of the world shall know, that I have plucked down the proud enemies; and set up my church, which was low and contemned. So it came to pass by God's ordering, that Babylon was overthrown by Cyrus; and the spiritual kingdom of the Devil by Christ; that the Jewish people were returned from captivity, and the elect from their spiritual bondage into the liberty of the sons of God.

## CHAPTER 18

**Verse 1.** *{And the word}* The prophet sharply reproves, and strongly refutes, a wicked proverb used in Israel, impeaching the justice of God, in his judgments: and seriously calls them to repentance; with assurance of life and safety thereupon.

**V. 2.** *{The fathers have eaten}* That is, have sinned, and the children have suffered for it; as the sins of Manasseh are remembered upon Judah; and the sins of Jeroboam upon the ten tribes. See Lam. 5:7. See more, Jer. 31:29.

**V. 3.** *{any more}* Since you make my patience an argument whereupon to tax me my judgments, I will hereafter bring them presently upon him that sins; and lay open your iniquities, like unto the iniquities of your forefathers, for which I have heretofore punished them.

**V. 4.** *{All souls are mine}* I am equally God, and judge of all, not accepting of persons; and if I do delay my general punishments, it is of my superabundant goodness; and if the children do bear the iniquities of their fathers, it is to terrify the fathers the more from sinning; and yet done according to justice: the children imitating their fathers in sinning, and having sufficient and abundant cause in themselves for the punishment; else in no wise should they die. But seeing such ill use is made of my delaying, and putting off my judgments, as I have formerly done in your fathers' times, I shall hereafter less use it, and make speedy demonstration of my justice, by my deeds; to clear it the more from these wicked aspersions.

**V. 5.** *{That which is lawful and right}* Heb. *Judgment and Justice.*

**V. 6.** *{hath not eaten}* Namely, of the idols' sacrifices, whose service was done upon hills and mountains, Ezek. 22:9. See Deut. 32:38. Psal. 16:4. and 106:28. 1 Cor. 8:1, 4, 7, 10, 13. and 10:19, 20, 21.

*{lift up his eyes}* To pray to them, or make worship to them, Psal. 123:1, 2. but does detest them rather.

*{defiled his neighbour's wife}* Lev. 18:20. and 20:10. by adultery.

*{To a menstruous woman}* Lev. 18:19. and 20:18.

**V. 7.** *{And hath not oppressed any}* Exod. 22:21. Lev. 19:15. and 25:14, 17.

*{pledge}* Exod. 22:26. Deut. 24:12.

*{hath spoiled}* Lev. 19:13.

*{hath given his bread to the hungry}* Deut. 15:7. Isa. 58:7. Mat. 25:35.

**V. 8.** *{upon usury}* Exod. 22:25. Lev. 25:36, 37. Deut. 23:19, 20. Psal. 15:5. usury, and increase: the first word in Hebrew properly signifies biting, from the effect, because it does bite or eat out a man's estate; the latter word signifies multiplication, or increase; in Greek it is τόκος, Mat. 25:27. Luk. 19:23. a birth, because thereby money begets money, as Arist. shows, in Polit. book 1. chap. 7. Thus money was not to be lent to the poor, Exod. 22:25. Levit. 25:35, 36, 37.

**V. 10.** *{That doth the like to any one of these}* Or, *that doth to his brother besides any of these;* or, *any one of these;* in ver. 9:

**V. 11.** *{and that doth not any of those}* Or, *though he hath not done any of these things;* has not done all the good deeds set down before, ver. 7, 8, 9.

*{even}* Or, *either.*

*{and defiled}* Or, *defiled.*

**V. 12.** *{hath oppressed}* Or, *or hath oppressed.* And so in the clauses following, as this and in the next verse.

*{all these abominations}* Or, *any.*

*{he shall surely die}* He shows how the son is punished for his father's fault; that is, if he be wicked as his father was, and does not repent, he shall be punished as his father was; or else not.

*{his blood}* Or, *his bloods.* He shall suffer capital punishment for his own sin, and he shall be the cause of his own death, Lev. 20:9. Act. 18:6.

**V. 16.** *{Hath not withholden the pledge}* Heb. *hath not pledged the pledge,* or, *taken to pledge.*

**V. 17.** *{Hath taken off}* That is, keeps himself from wronging or oppressing, though he might have some reason to do it.

**V. 19.** *{Yet say ye, why? doth not the son}* He shows that those profane men contending with the prophets did seek still to urge and press on that proverb; and happily to catch them, and make them confess, that either God's judgments were not just, in punishing the child for the fathers' sins, or at least that the prophets' intimations and expositions, were contrary to God's law, Exod. 20:5. Deut. 5:9. Thus the wicked persist in their blasphemies, and will not be beaten off from them.

*{where the son}* God again avows his just dealing in his judgments against their blasphemy, as if he should say, the meaning of my law is, that I will punish the sins of the fathers upon the children, in case that they follow their fathers' examples, which I do often suffer to fall out so, through my secret judgment; and if through my special grace I do sanctify them, so that they do not follow their fathers' steps, they shall also be exempted from their fathers' punishment.

**V. 20.** *{The son shall not bear}* Deut. 24:16. 2 Kings 14:16. 2 Chr. 15:4. Jer. 31:29. Namely, the innocent son which is by my Spirit purged from his father's wickedness: yet this is no general rule, in respect of the body and bodily good, in which God has, and may, visit and chastise the child for his father's sins, by temporal chastisement. But this must be principally be understood of the everlasting punishment of soul and body.

*{the righteousness}* That is, everyone shall receive either a reward for his good works, or punishment for his evil works.

**V. 21.** *{But if the wicked}* Chap. 33:12.

*{my statutes}* He joins the observation of the commandment with repentance: for none can repent indeed, except he labor to keep the law.

**V. 22.** *{He shall live}* He shall be delivered from the plagues and punishments of the wicked, and shall attain to everlasting life; to which the true way and direction, is the pure conversion of a sinner, and perseverance.

**V. 23.** *{Have I any pleasure at all}* Ver. 32. Chap. 33:11. He speaks this to commend God's mercy to poor sinners; who rather is ready to pardon, than to punish, as his long suffering declares.

**V. 24.** *{But when the righteous}* Chap. 3:20. and 33:12, 13.

*{his righteousness that he hath done}* That is, the false opinion that the hypocrites have of their righteousness, shall not move God to spare them. See ver. 22.

**V. 25.** *{Yet ye say}* Or, in Heb. *and will ye say?* Will you yet dare to tax, either mine actions with injustice, or my words and my law with contradiction?

*{The way of the LORD is not equal}* Chap.33.20. in punishing the father with the children.

*{are not your ways unequal?}* All the injustice is in you who follow your fathers' ways, and not in me: my law agrees well with this doctrine, but your understanding is perverted.

**Verse 26.** *{When a righteous man}* This is the rule of my sovereign justice, that the death of every one shall be the punishment of his own proper sin: as the order of my mercy is, to give a sinner hope that he shall be restored by repentance. Both the one and the other is well enough known unto you, but that in this contestation, you fight against your own consciences.

**Verse 29.** *{Yet saith}* The wicked, desperate sinners, will never make an end of caviling and sinning.

**Verse 30.** *{Every one according to his ways}* And not for the sins of their fathers, as you impute it to me.

*{ruin}* Heb. *a stumbling block*; to cause the ruin, Jer.5.25.

**Verse 31.** *{Make you a new heart}* Chap.11.19. and 36.26. Jerem.32.39. Eph.4.22,23,24. 2 Cor.5.17. Give way to the Spirit of grace, to whom it properly belongs to regenerate a man, who cannot do it of himself, Jerem.13.23. Ezek.11.19. and 36.26.

**Verse 32.** *{I have no pleasure in}* Chap.33.11. 2 Pet.3.9. to the great comfort of poor penitent sinners.

## CHAPTER 19

**Verse 1.** *{Moreover}* This chapter contains a lamentation for Jehoahaz, and Jehojakim, kings of Judah, under the parable of a lioness and her whelps; and for the whole kingdom and state of Judah under the parable of a vine.

*{for the Princes of Israel}* Namely for Jehoahaz, and Jehojakim; which indeed were rather princes than kings, as being under the kings either of Egypt, or Babylon. And under the king of Babylon were also Jehojakim, and Zedekiah, the two last kings of Judah.

**Verse 2.** *{What is thy mother}* Namely, the mother of the kings, or the royal stock, or the nation of the Jews?

*{she lay down}* Securely in former ages.

*{she nourished}* Herself brought up, and made her kings; but not so now.

**Verse 3.** *{One of her whelps}* Namely Jehoahaz, who was made king three months in Jerusalem. 2 King.23.30,31.

*{it devoured men}* Turned his regality and sovereignty, to inhuman cruelty, 2 King.23.32.

**Verse 4.** *{He was taken in their pit}* A term taken from the manner of hunting of lions. Pharaoh Necho carried him to Egypt, and put him in chains, 2 King.23.33. Of him Jeremiah told he should return no more to his native country, but die in Egypt, Jer.22.10,11,12. So it fell out, 2 King.23.34.

*{Egypt}* See 2 King.23.34. Jerem.22.11,12. the places afore cited.

**Verse 5.** *{When she saw that she had waited}* Namely, Jerusalem, when she saw that there was no hope that Jehoahaz should be restored.

*{another of her whelps}* Namely, Jehojakim, appointed to be king, by the king of Egypt, 2 King.23.34.

**Verse 6.** *{He went up and down}* This haply may relate to his various estate, between the king of Egypt, and the king of Babylon.

*{devoured men}* As ver.3. See Jer.26.20,21,22,23.

**Verse 7.** *{And he knew}* That is, Jehojakim visited and spoiled the palaces of those whom he did destroy, or in hostile manner slew with the sword, ver.6.

*{their desolate palaces}* Or, *their widows*.

*{he laid waste}* 2 King.23.35,37. and 24.3,4. 2 Chron.36.8. Jer.22.17,18.

*{noise of his roaring}* By his cruel and tyrannical commands.

**Verse 8.** *{The nations set against him}* Nebuchadnezzar with his great army, which was gathered of divers nations, 2 King.24.2.

*{and spread their net}* 2 Chron.36.6. Jer.22.18.

**Verse 9.** *{Into holds}* Where it seems he died, being upon the way to Babylon; and his body was afterward thrown out upon a dunghill. See 2 Chron.36.6. Jer.22.18,19.

**Verse 10.** *{Thy mother is like a vine}* After your tyranny was ceased, which was signified by the lion's roaring, Jerusalem seemed to flourish again, especially under Zedekiah, who had many sons fitting to succeed him in his kingdom, and to prop him up; which is signified by the following terms: or else, this verse, and the next, may relate to the flourishing state of the kingdom of Judah, in former times and ages, for the majesty of their kings, largeness of dominion, multitude and wealth of subjects.

**Verse 12.** *{But she was plucked up}* In such manner, and by such means, as were showed in the former part of this chapter.

*{East-wind}* Hos.13.15. meaning that the Chaldeans, which were east from them, should destroy them, as the east wind does the fruit of the vine. See Chap.17.10.

*{her strong rods were broken}* Namely, all the young men, and the royal issue. See 2 King.25.7.

**Verse 13.** *{She is planted in the wilderness}* The whole body of the nation, is partly already, and the rest shall be carried away into Babylon within a short while, to live there in extreme misery.

**Verse 14.** *{Out of a rod of her branches}* Namely, out of Jehojakim, or rather Zedekiah, who through his perfidiousness, and rebellion against Nebuchadnezzar, shall be the cause of the last desolation, 2 Chron.36.13. So that none of Judah's kings were strong, nor after should be, till Christ came, to whom the kingdom did belong.

*{And shall be for a lamentation}* Not only at the present time, but in after ages likewise: like that of Jeremiah, in his book of Lamentations.

## CHAPTER 20

**Verse 1.** *{And it came to pass}* In this chapter the Prophet reprehends certain elders of Israel, for their vile and base hypocrisy, pretending to ask counsel of God by him, and yet continuing in the abominations of their forefathers. Whereupon he recounts their rebellions and idolatries, against God, notwithstanding his marvelous, and merciful good dealings with them: and all this, in Egypt, in the wilderness, and in the land of Canaan. He denounces therefore grievous judgments against them, with promises of comfort to a repenting remnant. And lastly, in the end of this chapter under the type of a forest, he denounces extreme desolation unto Jerusalem and Judah.

*{In the seventh year}* Of Jechoniah's captivity, or Zedekiah's reign: that is two years, one month, and five days, since the beginning of Ezekiel's prophecies, Chap.1.1,2. And two years and five months before the siege began, Jerem.52.4. and two years, ten months, and nine and twenty days, before the city was taken, Jer.52.5,6.

*{certain of the elders}* See Chap.8.1. and 14.1.

*{came}* In dissimulation and hypocrisy, ver.31.

**Verse 3.** *{Are ye come to inquire of me}* In that true manner as I have appointed you, to desire my favor, by the way of faith and repentance? with a mind willing to learn, and obey my word? or rather with prejudicate opinions, and hearts settled upon your own ways, whatsoever you shall hear from my Prophet? Jer.42. Do you not come to tempt me, having no other aim, but to get something out of my Prophet, as may be pleasing to you? Chap.14.3.

*{I will not be inquired of by you}* Or, *I will not answer you*, or *you shall not find me*.

**Verse 4.** *{Wilt thou judge them?}* Chap.22.2. and 23.36. or, *shouldest thou plead and intercede with me for them?* Jer.14.11. They should rather be roughly handled with checks, and comminations, Chap.14.3.

*{Cause them to know}* This declares his goodness and justice, which calls sinners to repentance, before he condemns them, though here he does it with a kind of indignation.

*{The abominations of their fathers}* Imitated, and increased by their children; who would not amend by the examples, nor the punishment of their forefathers, ver.30.

**Verse 5.** *{In the day when I}* When as my people being in Egypt, I declared by effects, Exod.3, &c. that I had chosen them to be mine, Deut.10.15. according to my promises made to their forefathers, Gen.15.14.

*{Lifted up mine hand}* Or, *sware*. And so ver.6, &c. and ver.23. did show my sovereign power for their deliverance, to the destruction of their enemies. See Exod.14.8.

*{known unto them}* Exod.3.8. and 4.31.

**Verse 6.** *{I spied for them}* That is, appointed it for them by my decree, as the most excellent above all other countries: and for the conquest of which, I had prepared all things in my secret counsel; as into which I had marched before mine ark, to give them a secure entrance into it. See Numb.10.33.

*{glory of all lands}* Or, *flower*. A title of the land of Israel, or Canaan, ver.15. Dan.8.9. and 11.16. Psal.48.2. So, by its natural qualities, and by God's singular blessing upon it, and being made a type of the heavenly Canaan.

**Verse 7.** *{Said unto them}* These things are not specified in so many words in Exodus, but may be gathered thence. Exod.3.12. and 5.1. and are revealed by the Spirit to the Prophet, and do agree with what is written, Josh.5.9.

*{abominations of his eyes}* That is, the idols to the spiritual desiring of which, man's heart is induced by the eyes; delighted with the matter, or the form of them: and generally by all the outward senses, seeing they could not apprehend any deity in them by the Spirit. See Numb.15.39. and to which they lifted up their eyes, Chap.18.6.

*{defile not yourselves}* God had forbidden them to make mention of the idols, Exod.23.13. Deut.12.3. Psal.16.4. Hos.2.17.

**Verse 8.** *{And would not}* Exod.5.21. and 6.9. and Chap.23.

*{their eyes}* Which thing declares the wickedness of man's heart, which judges God's service by their eyes and outward senses.

*{Then I said}* Or, *Though I said*, Exod.5.3.

**Verse 9.** *{But I wrought for my name's sake}* Ver.14. and 22. I proceeded on in my delivering of them. God has ever this respect to his glory, that he would not have his name evil spoken of among the Gentiles, for the punishment that his people deserved; in confidence whereof the godly ever prayed; as Exod.32.12. Num.14.13, &c. Deut.9.28. Josh.7.9. 1 Sam.12.22.

**Verse 10.** *{Caused them to go forth}* Exod.13.18.

**Verse 11.** *{Shewed them}* Heb. *made them to know*.

*{which if a man do}* Lev.18.5. Deut.30.15. Rom.10.5. Gal.3.12. So he shall be preserved from all dangers, and shall, at the last, obtain everlasting life; the way to which, and the beginning and pledge of which is, new obedience: though it be no way a meritorious cause of it.

**Verse 12.** *{Sabbaths}* Exod.16.23, &c. and 20.8. and 33.13, &c. and 35.2. Deut.5.12.

**Verse 14.** *{Heathen}* Who might thereby take an occasion to blaspheme my name, and to accuse me of lack of ability; or else, that I had sought a means to destroy them more commodiously.

**Verse 15.** *{Given them}* That is, had promised and resolved to give them, if they had not made themselves utterly unworthy of it. Numb.14.29. and 26.65. 1 Cor.10.5,6.

**Verse 16.** *{Sabbaths}* That is, my true religion (the public worship being on the Sabbath) which I had commanded them: and gave themselves to serve me according to their own fantasies. See Isa.56.4.

**Verse 18.** *{Children}* Whom I spared. Numb.14.31.

*{Not in the statutes of your fathers}* Whereby the Holy Ghost confutes them that say, they will follow the religion and example of their fathers, and not measure their doings by God's Word, whether they be approvable thereby or no. See Jer.44.17.

**Verse 20.** *{A sign between me and you}* A sacrament of an interchangeable agreement; namely, that I shall sanctify you by my Spirit, causing you to cease from your evil works; and you likewise shall be conformable to the working of my grace.

**Verse 21.** *{The children}* Vers.18.

*{I would no more}* Numb.21.1. and 25.9. Psal.106.26, &c.

**Verse 22.** *{Nevertheless I withdrew}* Psal.78.38.

**Verse 23.** *{I lifted up mine hand}* Though I did forbear them for that time; yet I swore to them, that if they continued in their sins, after I had performed my promises unto them, by bringing them into the promised land, I would drive and scatter them out of it. See V.5.

*{and disperse them}* After they should be come into the land. See Levit.26.33. Jer.15.7.

**Verse 24.** *{They had not}* The children in the wilderness had not executed my judgments; nor their posterity after they were come into the land of Canaan.

*{Their fathers Idols}* Meaning, that they set their delight upon them.

**Verse 25.** *{I gave them also statutes that were not good, and judgments whereby they should not live}* I gave them in the wilderness decrees and ordinances, that were not good for them, but hurtful; and judgments that did sentence them to death; as that to hang up the heads and chief of the people before the sun; and for the judges of Israel to slay every one his men that were joined to Baal-Peor, Num.25.4,5. and that judgment for the death of Korah, Dathan, & Abiram, and the 250 Princes; and the 14700 murmurers, Num.16. And as that sentence and judgment was of the fiery serpents, Num.21. Besides that the Lord by his just judgment gave them up to themselves, that they should follow their own lusts, because they would not obey his laws; as ver.39. Psal.81.12. Rom.1.21,24.

**Verse 26.** *{And I polluted them}* So deemed them, used them, and dealt with them, and their gifts, as polluted things, cast them from me as unclean things.

*{to pass through the fire}* Ver.31. Chap.16.21. Though Moses express not this particular, yet it is most likely it was done then when Israel joined himself to Baal-Peor, Num.25.3. See 2 King.17.17. and 21.6. 2 Chron.28.3. and 33.6.

*{That openeth the womb}* That is, the firstborn, which ought to be sanctified to the Lord, Exod.13.2.

**Verse 27.** *{Yet in this}* Seeing that I give you the commission to lay all their fathers' sins open before them, ver.4. add this to all the rest, this their trespass in the land of Canaan.

*{committed a trespass}* Heb. *trespassed a trespass*.

**Verse 28.** *{For when I had}* Not only in the wilderness, when I brought them out of Egypt, but since I placed them in this land: which declares how prompt man's heart is to idolatry; seeing that by no admonitions he can be drawn back.

*{Then they saw}* Isa.57.5,6.

*{the provocation of their offering}* That is, their offering which was the object and provocation of my wrath.

**Verse 29.** *{Then I said unto them}* Or, *I told them what the high place was*, or, *Bamah*: I did often admonish them by my Prophets, and reproved them for their idolatry. See Chap.23.43.

*{what is the high place?}* Do you not know sufficiently by the very name of high place, which at all times has been infamous, as a place of idolatry, and unlawful worship, that all that is done there is abominable? as the very name of a brothel is sufficient to make any honest woman to fly the conversation and neighborhood of it.

*{Bamah}* Which signifies an high place, so that for all that it was so odious to God, yet they continued it, and the name of it, declaring thereby that they vaunted themselves of their idolatry, and were not ashamed thereof.

**Verse 30.** *{Wherefore say}* Seeing that reproving of the fathers' sins, ought to serve for a correction to the children which follow them; which thing yet they neglect; therefore tell them that I reject all their false shows of piety, which they make in seeking after my word, ver.3.

**Verse 31.** *{For when ye offer}* Ver.26.

*{shall I be inquired of}* Ver.3. He shows that the ingratitude of the people deserved, that God should cut them off, and that they should not have the comfort of his Word; that he would not henceforth suffer them, in this base and treacherous way, of such gross hypocrisy, to have anything to do with him, or his Prophets; or so much as to pretend anything thereunto, ver.37.

**Verse 32.** *{And that which cometh}* Your projects and purposes shall not take place.

*{we will be as the Heathen}* Idolaters as they: and so we shall prosper as they do, Jer.44.17.

**Verse 33.** *{I will rule over you}* I will show myself a king, and exercise my power over you in a punishment, as over rebellious and disloyal subjects; seeing you have not accepted of my government in due obedience: and I will not suffer in you, that are bound unto me by duty and obedience: the excuses which I wink at in other nations, strangers to my Covenant. See Hos.9.1. Amos 3.2.

**Verse 34.** *{I will bring you out}* I will not let you live at ease in the idolatrous countries, where you had sheltered yourselves. See Jer.40.11,12. and 43.5. and 44.1,7,8,11, &c.

**Verse 35.** *{I will bring you into}* I will drive you into the most solitary and savage places of the world, for a fullness of misery, ver.38.

*{there will I plead with you}* Execute my revenge; and yet so, as follows, ver.37.

**Verse 37.** *{I will cause you to pass}* Even as a shepherd makes his sheep to pass one by one when they come out of the sheepcote and marks them distinctly with his rod, to distinguish them from other, Lev.27.32. So I will sever those that are rebellious from among you, to punish them; and show favor to the other, Ezek.34.17. Matth.25.32,33.

*{I will bring you into}* Will constrain you to acknowledge me for your God and King by those punishments, the threatening whereof I have joined to my Covenant: by which I will chain you up like wild beasts, and will tame your stubbornness.

**Verse 38.** *{I will purge out from among}* Signifying that he will not burn the corn with the chaff; but choose out the wicked to punish them, when he will spare his.

*{I will bring them forth}* As ver.35.

*{they shall not enter}* Jer.44.14.

**Verse 39.** *{Go ye}* I do cast you off quite, and give you over to all manner of wickedness: that my name which you make profession of worshiping, may be no more profaned by your hypocrisy, nor be exposed to the scorn of mine enemies.

*{with your gifts}* Offered indeed to the true God, but defiled with the idolatry which is joined to it. See Isa.1.13. and 61.8. or else really presented to idols.

**Verse 40.** *{for in mine holy mountain}* Isa.2.2,3. Ezek.17.23. Mic.4.1. I do cast all you apostates off, and will only hold the true Israel, which truly believes, and is truly converted, for to be my people, and will gather them into my Church, to be by them served in spirit and truth; whereof the ancient service was but only a figure; an evangelical prophecy, whereof the return from Babylon was only an essay.

*{accept them}* An ordinary term for lawful sacrifices which were duly performed.

*{require your offerings}* Shunning them no more, as I had done the offerings of idolaters, and hypocrites, vers.39. Isa.1.13.

**Verse 41.** *{Sweet savour}* Heb. *savor of rest*.

*{sanctified in you}* I will cause myself to be reverenced, praised, & blessed for your sakes; and the sacred and inviolable truth of my promises shall be acknowledged by all men.

**Verse 43.** *{and there shall ye remember}* Chap.16.61.

*{loathe yourselves}* Chap.6.9. Your own consciences shall convict you after that you have felt my mercies.

**Verse 46.** *{Toward the South}* That is, toward Judea, which lay most southward from Chaldea, where the Prophet was, Chap.21.2.

*{and drop thy word}* That is, speak, and preach like a Prophet. A term which seems to be taken from Deut.32.2. See Amos 7.16. Mic.2.6,11.

*{forest of the South field}* City of Jerusalem, and land of Israel, Chap.21.2. And in such a sense is the similitude of forest often used, Esay 9.18. and 10.18. and 32.19. Jer.21.14. and 46.23.

**Verse 47.** *{kindle a fire in thee}* The Chaldean war, who is a fierce enemy.

*{green tree in thee}* All manner of people indifferently, both young and old, weak, and strong, poor, and rich, good and bad, Chap.21.3,4. Many righteous men being also entangled in these temporal judgments. See Luke 23.31.

*{All faces}* Those that are not consumed therewith, shall be brought unto extreme misery. See Lam.4.8. and 5.10.

*{from the South to the North}* From one end of the country to the other.

**Verse 48.** *{that I the LORD have kindled it}* That it is not man's work, but God's work; and so by man not to be mastered.

**Verse 49.** *{They say of me}* These profane people do scoff at my prophecies, which you do dictate to me, in allegorical and enigmatical terms, as if they were dreams and imaginations.

## CHAPTER 21

**Verse 1.** *{And the Word of the LORD}* Ezekiel again does prophesy and foretell against Jerusalem, and the land of Israel, the sword of the Chaldean: is commanded to sigh at the grievousness of it. And upon further declaration of the sharp terrors of it, he is commanded to smite upon his thigh for sorrow. After, by command, he smites his hands together; and God himself does so, to hearten on the Chaldean to slaughter. He foretells the manner of the king of Babylon's consulting about his coming against Jerusalem, the overthrow of it, and of the king thereof. And lastly, he prophesies the destruction of the Ammonites.

**Verse 2.** *{set thy face toward Jerusalem}* Seeing it is so, prophesy the same things in plain and proper terms.

*{the holy places}* Namely, against the Temple, and the buildings adjoining to it; which were not to be spared in this general desolation. See Jer.7.4. Ezek.9.6.

**Verse 3.** *{my sword}* The Chaldeans.

*{the righteous and the wicked}* Chap.20.47. Righteous, here may signify those less wicked than the others: for all were naught, Chap.22.18. Jer.3.8.

**Verse 5.** *{it shall not return}* Until it has performed all its execution, Chap.20.47.

**Verse 6.** *{the breaking of my loins}* Doing like a woman that travails, bowing and wringing herself. See Esay 16.11. and 21.3. Jer.4.19.

**Verse 7.** *{for the tidings}* Because of the great noise of the coming of the army of the Chaldeans, which I see approaching to Jerusalem to destroy it, as I have foretold.

*{shall be weak as water}* Heb. *shall go into water*.

**Verse 9.** *{a sword, a sword is sharpened}* Deut.32.41. My judgment is ready to be put in execution; the instruments fully prepared.

**Verse 10.** *{that it may glitter}* That it may terrify, and affright with the brightness of it, Job 20.25.

*{should we then make mirth}* Can one do anything but lament, as I do?

*{it contemneth the rod of my son, as every tree}* Or, *it is the rod of my son, it despiseth every tree*, Heb. *the rod of my son contemning every tree.* That is, the rod appointed by me to chastise my people, which is dear to me as a son, is now become a sword; and so being used against such a wicked people, it contemns all resistance. Or thus, this sword contemns, despises, slights, as a small matter, my other rods wherewith I chastised my children, or any rod that could be made of any tree or wood: this being a sword for surpassing them. And should we then make mirth? The meaning then is to this effect, You Nation, hardened against all fatherly punishments, as an evil child is against a rod, you shall now be punished by the sword. In the Hebrew text, there is an allusion, between a rod or staff, and tribe: The meaning whereof, is, Whilst your afflictions are moderate, as from rod to rod, you do contemn them; but this shall be as from rod to sword, which you shall not be able to endure, nor withstand.

**Verse 11.** *{The hand of the slayer}* To the king of Babylon, who is the executioner of God's judgments.

**Verse 12.** *{terrors by reason of the sword}* Or, *they are thrust down to the sword with my people*.

*{smite therefore upon thy thigh}* Jerem.31.19. Devise all the actions of a grieved and moaning person.

**Verse 13.** *{because it is a trial, and what if the sword contemn even the rod}* Or, when the trial has been, what then? Shall they not also belong to the despising rod? I am almost forced to come to the extremity of punishment; for all chastisements, to try to bring them to repentance, would be vain, Isa.1.5. Jer.6.28,29,30. Or, as if God had said; because I have tried Judah with two captivities, and they are not bettered, therefore they are worthy to be further punished with famine, pestilence, sword, and the burning of Temple and city; and Nebuchadnezzar, set up a conqueror by me for their sins, shall be the rod they shall not resist.

**Verse 14.** *{smite thine hands together}* Heb. *hand to hand*. Either in token of extreme sorrow, Ezek.6.11. or, in an holy spleen and rage against them for their sins, in that manner to animate the Chaldean to take revenge upon them. See vers.17.

*{the third time}* The Chaldeans' forces came three times against Jerusalem after this prophecy; first time, when the city was taken in the eleventh year of Zedekiah, fourth month, ninth day. 2. When Nebuzaradan came in the nineteenth year of Nebuchadnezzar, fifth month, seventh day. 3. When Nebuzaradan came in twenty third year of Nebuchadnezzar, when Gedaliah was slain, &c. 2 King.25.1,2,3,8. Jer.52.5,6,12,30.

*{the sword of the slain}* By which there shall be a great slaughter in Jerusalem, not only of mean people, but of princes and great ones also: and the king himself being overcome by this sword, shall die in captivity.

**Verse 15.** *{I have set the point of the sword against all their gates}* Or, *glittering* or, *fear*. I have caused their cities to be furiously besieged, and set upon.

*{wrapped up for the slaughter}* Or, *sharpened*.

**Verse 16.** *{Go thee one way or other}* Heb. *unite thy self*, &c. In which words, by apostrophe, God speaks to the sword; comparing it to a soldier, because he commands it to unite itself, to wit, with fellow soldiers, to invade, with joined forces, all parts of the land of Judah, and to oppose the Jews, whether they turn to the right hand or the left. Or, it is an expression of the permission which God gave the Chaldeans, to rout out at their pleasure.

*{or on the left}* Heb. *set thy self, take the left hand*.

**Verse 17.** *{I will also smite}* As I have commanded you Ezekiel, to smite your hands, vers.14. so will I likewise smite mine, in sign of joy that I have vented my wrath, and satisfied my justice. See Deuter.28.63. Isa.1.24. Ezek.5.13. and 16.42.

**Verse 19.** *{appoint thee two ways}* God commands the Prophet to draw out two ways upon a tile; as Chap.4.1,2. the one leading to Rabbah, the royal city of the Ammonites; the other to Jerusalem, ver.22. And because the king of Babel was purposed to go against both the Prophets choice of, or pointing the side to which the king should turn at the head of the way, (or crossing of the road,) shows his army should take the way to Jerusalem. The meaning then is, draw out in prophetic action, upon a tile, or upon the ground two ways, coming both from one plain place, to represent Nebuchadnezzar's deliberation, where he had gathered his army, whether he should go against Jerusalem, or against the land of the Ammonites.

*{Choose thou a place}* To make a mustering place to muster the army in.

*{the head of the way}* At the crossing of a roadway; such as those highways were which led to great cities.

*{to the city}* Jerusalem.

**Verse 20.** *{Rabbah}* The royal city of the Ammonites, 2 Sam.11.1. and 12.26. Jer.49.2.

*{Jerusalem the defenced}* The fortified Jerusalem.

**Verse 21.** *{for the king of Babylon}* Thus he shows the meaning of the type.

*{At the parting of the way}* Heb. *mother of the way*. To know whether he should go against the Ammonites, or them of Jerusalem.

*{to use divination}* According to the custom of the heathens in deliberation of any importance.

*{his arrows}* Or, *knives*; likely to slay the multitude of his sacrifices; as were usual in divinations, Num.23.1,2,14,29.

*{images}* Heb. *teraphim*.

*{he looked in the Liver}* This was a kind of divination, by the shape, color, posture, &c. of entrails of sacrificed beasts, and especially of the liver.

**Verse 22.** *{At his right hand}* They used lots, also in their divinations; as Esther 3.7. and 9.26,28,29,31,32. And the lot which had Jerusalem on it, came to the right hand; and likely the way, on the right hand, led to Jerusalem.

*{to appoint Captains}* The lots, and all his divinations, showed, that he was thoroughly to prepare all things needful for the strait siege and war against Jerusalem: wherein he should prosper and obtain a glorious victory.

*{to open the mouth in the slaughter}* That he should take it by assault, with shouts and cries, used in such actions, and with a general slaughter of the inhabitants of Jerusalem.

**Verse 23.** *{And it shall be to them as a false divination}* The Jews, who had oftentimes before broken, and renewed their covenants with the Chaldeans, without falling into final ruin, shall think that the business will pass so for this time also; and that Nebuchadnezzar's enterprise will come to nothing, and thereupon they will charge and scorn you as a false diviner, and as one that seeks to betray them into Nebuchadnezzar's hands; but care not for it: thereby they shall not hurt you, but themselves.

*{to them that have sworn oaths}* Or, *for the oaths made unto them*; and are so often forsworn and perjured: and mind still to go on in their perfidious treachery.

*{but he will call to remembrance}* Nebuchadnezzar will at this time pay them for their former treacheries: And this very act of theirs against you, shall conduce to the same end, Exod.32.34.

**Verse 24.** *{Ye have made your iniquity to be remembered}* Through your wickedness and impiety which you make open profession of; even by this your scorning of my Prophet, and slighting of your oaths, you give cause, first to me, whose name has been used in your oaths, and next the king of Babylon, to punish you at once for your perjuries, and reiterated rebellions.

*{ye shall be taken with the hand}* Of the Babylonian, ver.9.

**Verse 25.** *{wicked Prince of Israel}* Namely, Zedekiah; who practiced with the Egyptians to make himself high, and able to resist the Babylonians. He speaks to him in God's name, and sentences him by his authority; and so omitting any honorable compellation, he slights him, as his sin deserved. So, 1 Sam.13.13. 1 King.18.18. 2 King.3.13,14.

*{whose day is come}* To wit, of your just punishment.

**Verse 26.** *{remove the Diadem}* The royal ornaments.

*{this shall not be the same}* The kingdom of Judah shall no more be that triumphant kingdom, which it was at other times.

*{exalt him that is low}* This seems to relate to Jehoiachin, now a prisoner; and afterward set at liberty, and exalted, 2 King.24.12. and 25.27,28,30. Jer.52.31.

*{abase him that is high}* Zedekiah. See his abasement, 2 King.25.7.

**Verse 27.** *{I will overturn, overturn, overturn it}* Heb. *perverted, perverted, perverted will I make it*.

*{until he come}* The kingdom of Judah shall never be restored to its former luster, (for after the return it was but very mean, and full of troubles and miseries, as the books of Ezra, Nehemiah, and Esther, and the 11. chapter of Daniel, and the history of the Maccabees declare) until the Messiah come, to whom by right it belongs, as David's successor according to the flesh, and established by God to be king over his Church, who shall alter it into a spiritual and everlasting kingdom. See Genes.49.10. Luke 1.32,33. To this place of Ezekiel, Nathanael seems to have respect, Joh.1.49. acknowledging him come, to whom the kingdom did belong.

**Verse 28.** *{Thus saith the Lord GOD concerning the Ammonites}* This prophecy against the Ammonites is inserted here, by occasion of those words, ver.20.21. Their fuller history, and God's judgments against them, are set down, Chap.25.1–7. Though this expedition of Nebuchadnezzar proved, not to fall out against them; yet shortly after he should destroy them: as is most likely, in the three and twenty year of his reign, when Ishmael sent by Baalis king of the Ammonites had slain Gedaliah, and the Chaldeans that were with Gedaliah at Mizpah; and after returned to the Ammonites again, Jerem.40.14. and 41.2,3,10,15. and 52.30. Of God's judgments against the Ammonites, see more, Jer.49.1–7. Amos 1.13,14,15. Zeph.2.7,8.

*{concerning their reproach}* Namely, the wrong and outrages offered unto my people, and their insulting over them, Chap.25.3,6. Zeph.2.8,9.

**Verse 29.** *{Whilst they see vanity}* Whilst your diviners and soothsayers do foretell you all manner of happiness and security; persuading you that you shall trample upon the Jews' necks, which are slain for their sins; and that no harm shall come to you.

*{the necks of them that are slain}* Insulting over the dead carcasses of the Jews.

*{their iniquity}* Ver.25.

**Verse 30.** *{Shall I cause it to return?}* Or, *cause it to return*; insult over them no more.

*{In the place}* O you Ammonites, I will not scatter you into strange lands, as I have done my people, but will destroy you in your own land,

**Verse 31.** *{I will blow against thee}* I will light and kindle it, as Chap.20.47.

*{brutish}* Or, *burning men*; the Chaldeans burning in wrath against you.

**Verse 32.** *{thy blood shall be in the midst of the land}* All your country shall be overflown with blood.

## CHAPTER 22

**Verse 1.** *{Moreover}* This chapter contains a catalogue of sins in Jerusalem, and of God's judgments following thereupon: And that in Jerusalem he will melt them, as dross in a furnace. After more particularly, the Prophet from God, charges upon her the sins of her prophets; of her priests; of her princes; of her people: And not a man seeking to stop God's anger against them.

**Verse 2.** *{wilt thou judge}* Or, *plead for*. Chap.20.4. and 23.36. are you ready to execute your charge which I commit unto you against Jerusalem, that murders the Prophets, and them that are godly?

*{bloody City}* Heb. *City of bloods*, Chap.14.6,9. Nah.3.1. Abak.2.12.

*{yea, thou shalt shew her}* Or, *wilt thou show her*. Heb. *make her know*.

**Verse 3.** *{That her time may come}* The time of her destruction.

*{against her self}* To her own undoing.

**Verse 4.** *{Thou hast shed}* 2 King.21.16. and 24.4. Jer.26.21,22,23.

*{reproach}* As was threatened in the Law, Deut.28.37.

**Verse 5.** *{infamous, and much vexed}* Heb. *polluted of name, much in vexation*: whose very name all men hate; that shall be famous for the greatest desolation that ever befell any city.

**Verse 6.** *{power}* Or, *arm*; lending also the arm of their authority unto others, for the shedding of blood.

**Verse 7.** *{In thee}* Hereby he means, that there was no kind of wickedness which was not committed in Jerusalem, and therefore the plagues of God should speedily come upon her.

**Verse 9.** *{men that carry tales}* Heb. *men of slanders*, Exod.23.1. Lev.18.16.

*{they eat upon the mountains},* Chap.18.6.

**Verse 10.** *{discovered}* Lev.18.8. and 20.11.

*{set apart}* Lev.18.19. and 20.18. and 15.25.

**Verse 11.** *{Committed abomination with his neighbour's wife}* Lev.18.20. Jer.5.8.

*{another hath}* Or, *every one hath*.

*{lewdly}* Or, *by lewdness*.

*{humbled his sister}* Lev.18.9. and 20.17.

**Verse 12.** *{Have they taken gifts}* Against God's laws. Exod.23.8. Deut.16.19. and 27.25.

*{Thou hast greedily gained}* Against God's laws, Exod.22.25. Lev.25.36. Deut.23.19.

**Verse 13.** *{I have smitten mine hand}* Chap.21.14,17. in token of my wrath and vengeance. A sign, either of great joy; or of extreme sorrow and anger: as in this place.

**Verse 14.** *{Can thine heart endure}* Will you have courage and strength enough to withstand my judgments? or be able to defend yourself? your heart, how hard soever, cannot bear it out.

**Verse 15.** *{I will scatter thee}* Deut.4.27. and 28.25,64.

*{and will consume}* Your filth of sin, and filthy sinners out of you; cause you to perish, that you may no more provoke me by your enormities, Chap.23.27,48. Or, I will take away the occasion of your wickedness.

**Verse 16.** *{And, &c.}* You shall be no more the inheritance of the Lord, but forsaken; or, Heb. and you shall be profaned, and defiled; that is to say, filled with blood, desolated, not regarded as a holy city; but like a wicked place, which God has cast off, and abandoned.

**Verse 18.** *{become dross}* Isa.1.22. Jer.6.29,30. it is quite degenerate, like to good silver turned to dross.

*{dross}* Heb. *drosses*.

**Verse 19.** *{midst of Jerusalem}* As in a furnace, Chap.24.10.

**Verse 24.** *{Say unto her}* Namely, to the Jewish nation, or to Jerusalem.

*{not cleansed}* The fire of my judgments has not cleansed you from your filthiness, and there is nothing sound, nor good in you; therefore I will lay a most extreme burden upon you, and use no clemency at all towards you, Chap.24.6. See Jer.4.11. & 6.28,29,30. and 24.8.

*{nor rained upon in the day of indignation}* Not eased or helped with any shower of rain, to quench and allay the flame and fire of my indignation.

**Verse 25.** *{There is a conspiracy}* All this may have relation to the persecutions, which the false prophets by plots and conspiracies, used against good men. See Jer.20.2. and 26.8,20. and 29.25,26. Lam.4.13.

*{They have devoured souls}* Chap.13.18. Mat.23.14.

**Verse 26.** *{Have violated my Law}* Heb. *offered violence to*: have openly and impudently gone and done against it, Zeph.3. verse 4.

*{They have put no difference}* See Chap.44.23. Jer.50.19.

*{have hid their eyes}* They have winked at the violation of my Sabbaths; or they themselves have framed pretenses whereby they might falsely violate it.

*{profaned}* Continued, and dishonored without any reverence to my sacred majesty.

**Verse 27.** *{Her Princes}* Mic.3.11. Zeph.3.3.

**Verse 28.** *{And her Prophets}* Chap.13.4, &c. They which should have reproved them, flattered them in their vices, and covered their doings with lies.

**Verse 29.** *{Oppression}* Or, *deceit*.

*{wrongfully}* Heb. *without right*.

**Verse 30.** *{And I sought for a man}* Jer.5.1. Esay 59.16. and 63.5.

*{that should make up the hedge}* Chap.13.5. which should show himself zealous in my cause, by resisting vice; and also pray unto me to withhold my plagues, Psal.106.23.

**Verse 31.** *{Their own way}* Their evil deeds have I punished; as Chap.16.43.

## CHAPTER 23

**Verse 1.** *{The word of the LORD}* This chapter under the names of Aholah, and Aholibah, two sisters, sets down the spiritual whoredoms of Samaria, and Jerusalem. How Aholah played the harlot with the Assyrian, and formerly with the Egyptian; and was therefore delivered into the hands of the Assyrian, to execute judgments upon her. How Aholibah, notwithstanding played the whore more than her sister, and doted upon the Assyrian, and the Chaldean, and not forgetting the Egyptian; and therefore is she also delivered up into the hands of her lovers, the Chaldean and others with them, for sorer judgments. And afterwards it joins them both together, in sins, and in punishments.

**Verse 2.** *{Two women}* Meaning Israel and Judah, divided into two kingdoms; both came out of one family. See Jer.3.7,8,10. Ezek.16.44,45.

**Verse 3.** *{And they committed whoredoms}* Levit.17.7. Josh.24.14. Ezek.20.8. spiritually, by idolatry.

*{There were their breasts pressed}* A figurative description of the people's first going astray after idols.

**Verse 4.** *{Aholah the elder}* These seem names framed and fitted to the present purpose in hand. Aholah signifies a mansion, or dwelling in herself, meaning Samaria, which was the head city of the ten tribes, Isa.7.9. who having forsaken the Temple of God, had established a worship of God according to their own minds, 1 King.12.16,18,31. and Aholibah signifies my mansion in her, whereby is meant Jerusalem, where God's Temple was, in which the pure worship of God remained, 2 Chron.13.10,11.

*{and they were mine}* Chap.16.8,20.

**Verse 5.** *{when she was mine}* Heb. *under me*: which may be understood thus, being my wife, subject to me. Some render it, in my stead, or, instead of me.

*{her lovers}* An ordinary title for profane nations, with which God's people joined in idolatry and impiety.

*{on the Assyrians her neighbours}* 2 King.15.19. after the Assyrians had conquered Syria, which bordered upon the ten tribes.

**Verse 6.** *{Clothed with blue}* A magnificent, gentle, rich, and powerful nation.

**Verse 7.** *{Committed her whoredoms with them}* Heb. *bestowed her whoredoms upon them*.

*{the chosen men of Assyria}* Heb. *the choice of the children of Ashur*.

**Verse 8.** *{From Egypt}* Vers.3. He seems to have relation to the golden calves set up by Jeroboam, in imitation of the calf Apis, dedicated to the Egyptians' chiefest idol Serapis. See Exod.32.4. This may also be understood of the unlawful covenants made with Egypt, 2 King.17.4.

*{they lay with her}* The Holy Ghost uses these terms, which seem strange to chaste ears, to cause this wicked vice of idolatry so to be abhorred, that scarce any should abide to hear the name thereof mentioned, Psal.16.4.

**Verse 9.** *{I have delivered her}* 2 King.17.3,4,5, &c.

*{into the hand of the Assyrians}* 2 King.17.23.

*{upon whom she doted}* And fell from them in Hoshea his time, 2 King.17.4.

**Verse 10.** *{These discovered her nakedness}* Chap.16.37. that is, they have shamefully punished her: a term taken from the disgraces which are done to poor women taken in the wars, Isa.20.4. and 47.3.

*{and slew her}* Namely, the body of the nation, taking away their kingdom: whereof see 2 King.17.4,5,6. and 18.11.

*{famous}* Heb. *a name*: that is a noted and memorable example of God's judgments.

**Verse 11.** *{Her sister Aholibah}* Jer.3.8. she was more corrupt in her inordinate love.

*{than she}* Heb. *she corrupted her inordinate love more than she*, Chap.16.47. Jer.3.8,11.

*{more than her sister in her whoredoms}* Heb. *more than the whoredoms of her sister*.

**Verse 12.** *{upon the Assyrians}* Chap.16.28. 2 King.16.7.

*{her neighbours}* Ver.3,5,6.

**Verse 14.** *{Increased}* Not satisfied and content with the Assyrians.

*{men portrayed upon the wall}* A description of unbridled lust; like a woman that should fall in love with strangers that are absent, only by seeing their pictures, and should send to seek after them into far countries.

**Verse 16.** *{And as soon as she saw them with her eyes}* Heb. *at the sight of her eyes*.

*{sent messengers}* Likely in Hezekiah's time. And partly thereupon that judgment seems threatened, 2 King.20.17,18. Esay 39.6,7. and afterwards in the times of Manasseh, and the succeeding kings.

**Verse 17.** *{Babylonians}* Heb. *Children of Babel*.

*{her mind was alienated from them}* Heb. *loosed*, or, *disjoined*. So Jer.6.8. and often here in this chapter. The meaning is the Jews had broken the league which they had made with the Chaldeans, to join with the Egyptians, their enemies and emulators, 2 King.24.20.

**Verse 20.** *{Their paramours}* Heb. *concubines*. By the Egyptians *concubines*, or *paramours* are to be understood the neighboring nations, adhering to them, and imitating their idolatry. And so likewise did *Aholibah*.

*{whose flesh is as the flesh of asses}* Chap.16.26. These are terms taken from the shameless lusts of unchaste women, and do show the strength and riches of Egypt, which moved the Jews to make a league with them.

**Verse 21.** *{Thou calledst to remembrance}* Repeating your first lewdness, ver.3. which yet was then more excusable, than now it is: because when you were in your spiritual childhood, you had but small knowledge and experience of my pure worship; which you cannot now pretend, being fully instructed in it: as a young maid, if she were seduced, might partly be excused for her simplicity, which a married woman which is of full age cannot plead.

**Verse 22.** *{Behold, I will raise}* Chap.16.37.

**Verse 23.** *{Pekod, and Shoa}* Names of some people of Asia, which were subjects to Nebuchadnezzar, of which his army was also composed, Jer.50.21.

**Verse 24.** *{With chariots, wagons, and wheels}* Such were the ancient manner of carriages and weapons in war.

*{judge thee according to their judgments}* They shall indeed by the secret guidance of my Spirit, execute my judgments; but I will leave the manner of the execution of them to their discretions. And men usually do it with less mercy, and pity, than God does, 2 Sam.24.14. Zach.1.15.

**Verse 25.** *{They shall take away thy nose}* A kind of speech taken from the ignominious punishments used in some countries against adulterous women; so to deform the beauties they so abused: or, from the cruelties of enemies enraged against prisoners, before they kill them.

**Verse 26.** *{They shall strip thee}* Chap.16.39.

*{fair jewels}* Heb. *instruments of thy decking*.

**Verse 27.** *{Thus will I make thy lewdness to cease}* Ver.48. Ch.22.15.

*{from the land of Egypt}* I will hinder you from going any more into Egypt to commit fornication. Or, I will cause your fornication which you have brought out of the land of Egypt to cease.

**Verse 28.** *{Whom thou hatest}* Ver.17.22. Chap.16.37.

**Verse 29.** *{All thy labour}* All your treasures and riches which you have gotten by labor.

*{shall be discovered}* Even as a malefactor's misdeeds are discovered, when he is once sentenced to death. Or, all the world shall see your shameful forsaking of God to serve idols.

**Verse 31.** *{I will give her cup into thine hand}* I will execute the same judgments and vengeance against you. See Psal.75.8. Jer.25.15. Mat.20.23.

**Verse 32.** *{Suck it out}* Psal.75.8. Isa.51.17.

*{break the shards thereof}* You shall grow mad therewith like a drunken man, who after he is overcome with wine will break the cups and pots, and will tear his flesh with his nails, and with his teeth, Jer.25.16.

**Verse 35.** *{And cast me behind thy back}* 1 King.14.9. Neh.9.26.

**Verse 36.** *{Judge Aholah}* Chap.20.4. and 22.2. or, *plead for*.

**Verse 37.** *{And blood is in}* Chap.16.38,20.

**Verse 38.** *{They have defiled my sanctuary in the same day}* Having done so wickedly, yet they come into my sanctuary the same day, making a show of serving me at the same time, ver.39. which is most odious to me, Chap.20.29. Esay 66.3.

**Verse 39.** *{Lo, thus have they done in the midst of my house}* Have committed these abominations in my Temple, Chap.8.3,6. 2 King.21.4, &c.

**Verse 40.** *{That ye have sent for men}* A figurative description of the profane entertainment given to the Chaldeans, Egyptians, and others sent for by the Jews, to treat of unlawful covenants, taken from a whore's preparation, when she entertains her expected ruffian.

*{to come}* Heb. *coming*.

*{wash thyself}* He directs his speech to either of them two; namely, Aholah, or Aholibah.

**Verse 41.** *{Stately bed}* Heb. *honorable*. According to the manner of many ancient nations, and also of the Jews, who at their most solemn feasts, used to sit or lie upon beds, Esth.1.6. and 7.8. Joh.13.25.

*{whereupon thou hast set}* Prov.7.17.

*{mine incense and mine oil}* Which you had received of me, together with other blessings, and with which they did rub and anoint themselves at banquets and public feasts: as if he would say, she had employed God's gifts in gaining the favor and friendship of profane nations, Isa.57.9.

**Verse 42.** *{with her}* Heb. *in her*: Namely, Jerusalem and Samaria, upon these occasions have been full of strangers, walking, and taking their pleasures, like fornicators in a brothel house.

*{with the men}* Heb. *to the men*; that is, besides those great and noble nations, the Jews have also desired the friendship and relief of the Arabians, Ethiopians, and other base nations, who thinking themselves much honored by such a request, have carried them presents, which the nobler nations have not done, but have received presents of the Jews, Chap.16.33,34.

*{of the common sort}* Heb. *of the multitude of men*.

*{Sabeans}* Or, *drunkards*.

**Verse 43.** *{Then said I}* Admonishing her.

*{unto her that was}* To either of those nations, which were grown old in their idolatry.

*{they now commit}* Words of disdain; as much as to say, Now they will satisfy their unbridled lust, having all their fornicators at their command; sometime with the one, and sometime with the other; yet their lovers will not be true and trusty to them, but ready to forsake them, and fall to others, as the times, and their best advantage shall require.

*{whoredoms with her}* Heb. *her whoredoms*.

**Verse 45.** *{Righteous men}* Namely, the Assyrians and Chaldeans executioners of God's justice, who shall have a just cause, according to men's opinions, to revenge themselves of the Jews and Israelites' perjuries and rebellions. Or, your lewdness is such, that all just men shall judge you worthy of all that shall befall you.

*{judge them after}* Chap.16.38.

**Verse 46.** *{I will bring up}* Chap.16.40. the Chaldeans.

*{to be removed and spoiled}* Heb. *for a removing and spoil*.

**Verse 47.** *{stone them}* Deut.22.24.

**Verse 48.** *{Thus will I cause}* Vers.27. Chap.22.15.

*{all women may be taught}* Meaning all other cities and countries; especially those where God has planted his Church.

**Verse 49.** *{Your lewdness upon you}* Namely, the just punishment for it.

## CHAPTER 24

**Verse 1.** *{Again}* This chapter contains the last denunciation of judgment against the Jews, before the final ruin and destruction of that kingdom: uttered in Babylon, the very day that Nebuchadnezzar began his last siege against Jerusalem; declaring then, and there, the very day of the siege. And prophesying the utter destruction of the city, under the parable of a boiling pot, whose scum is in it. And under the sign of the Prophet's not mourning for the death of his wife, showing the woeful calamity of the Jews, to be indeed, either beyond all sorrow; or to be such, and so sudden, as they should not dare, or else not have time to bemoan it; shutting up this prophecy (and so confirming it) till the time that the news should be brought to Ezekiel of the fulfilling of it.
