*{in the ninth year}* Of the second captivity, viz. since Jeconiah, Mordecai, Esth.2.5,6. and Ezekiel, Chap.40.1. were carried captives, and Zedekiah made king: the tenth month, and the tenth day of the month, Ezekiel is told in Mesopotamia, that Nebuchadnezzar, that very day, in Judea laid siege to Jerusalem, 2 King.25.1. Jer.39.1. and 52.4.

*{in the tenth month}* Called Tebeth, which contains part of December, and part of January.

**Verse 2.** *{write thee}* That so it may be certainly remembered and known.

*{this same day}* Jer.52.4.

**Verse 3.** *{Set on a pot}* That is, set a pot on the fire. See such a similitude, Jer.1.13. Ezek.11.3,7. The pot is Jerusalem; the flesh and fat pieces, are the chief, the richest, and the noblest that are in her; the fire are God's judgments, by which he would have humbled, and mollified his people's hearts, to bring them to repentance; but that having taken no effect, by reason of their obstinate rebellion, he would convert those judgments, into a total consumption.

**Verse 4.** *{every good piece}* The great ones.

**Verse 5.** *{burn}* Or, *heap*.

*{bones under it}* Meaning, it seems, the bones of the innocents whom they had slain; who were the cause of kindling God's wrath against them.

*{seethe the bones of it therein}* It seems that the bones were put into the bottom of the pot, vers.4,10. and not, as others have understood it, without the pot to kindle the fire. By the bones, may be meant the great ones and governors, which bear up the body of the state: Or rather, the mean and vulgar sort of lesser esteem; as opposed to the former.

**Verse 6.** *{wherefore}* To make the accomplishment answerable to the figure.

*{woe to the bloody City}* Chap.22.2. Nah.3.1. 2 King.21.16.

*{to the pot whose scum}* Namely, to Jerusalem, that has not been cleansed from her open and notorious sins, by my punishments, Chap.22.44. See Jer.4.11. and 6.29.

*{let no lot}* Let all the people, without any difference be burnt in it, or driven out of it, and carried away; let none be pardoned: though in wars, oftentimes, they cast lots to slay some, and to save some.

**Verse 7.** *{her blood is in}* She is full of misdeeds, especially of murders and oppressions.

*{she set it upon}* This city showed her cruelty to all the world, and was not ashamed thereof, neither hid it; contrary to what God had commanded, namely, that the very blood of beasts should be covered over with dust, Levit.17.13. Deut.12.16,24. and 15.23.

**Verse 8.** *{set her blood upon the top of a rock}* I will punish her openly; and the signs and monuments thereof shall remain forever.

**Verse 9.** *{woe to the bloody city}* Vers.6.

*{I will even make the pile}* That is what I have commanded you to do for a sign, that I will do in act, making ready the Chaldeans' great fire, Jer.1.13.

**Verse 10.** *{kindle the fire}* God would do it, that so the city should be utterly destroyed; and that he would give the enemies an appetite thereunto.

**Verse 11.** *{set it empty}* To signify, that Jerusalem, after the inhabitants were gone out of it, should be burnt by the Chaldeans, as it were, to cleanse it perfectly from all its excrements, Chap.16.36,37.

**Verse 12.** *{she hath wearied}* Instead of the only means of deliverance, which was repentance and conversion, she has with great care and toil, sought out other worldly and unprofitable means, that will prove false and unfaithful to her, and in the end deceive her.

**Verse 13.** *{lewdness}* Your wicked obstinacy, has been the only cause that you have not been cleansed.

*{I have purged thee}* I labored, by sending my Prophets to call you to repentance; but you would not, 2 Chron.36.15. Jer.18.11,12. and Chap.25.3, &c. and 35.15.

*{till I have caused}* Chap.16.42.

**Verse 14.** *{I will not go back}* Esay 47.3,11,12,13. Nothing shall hinder or alter me.

*{shall they judge thee}* That is, the Babylonians. See Chap.23.45. and 16.38.

**Verse 16.** *{the desire of thine eyes}* Namely, your dear wife; as ver.18. Another type of their doleful and desperate condition.

*{with a stroke}* By a sudden death, proceeding from some supernatural and divine cause, for whom he is forbid to mourn; signifying that their sorrow for the losses of their dearest things, in their miserable calamities, should be greater, than open mourning might express. See vers.22. Jer.22.10.

*{run}* Heb. *go*.

**Verse 17.** *{Forbear to cry}* Heb. *be silent*.

*{bind the tire of thine head upon thee}* Use your ordinary attire, and show no sign at all of mourning; such as those were of going bareheaded, Lev.10.6. and 21.10. and barefooted, 2 Sam.15.30. Isa.47.2. Of covering the lips, Lev.13.45. Mic.3.7. and eating of food brought in, and given by neighbors and friends, Jerem.16.5,7.

*{lips}* Heb. *upper lip*. And so ver.22.

**Verse 18.** *{morning}* Morning the morning following.

**Verse 21.** *{I will profane my sanctuary}* The exposition of the type. Profane, by sending the Chaldeans to destroy it; as Chap.7.22.

*{the excellency of your strength}* Namely, the Temple, the Ark, and other signs of my presence, wherein consisted the glory, and the strength of your subsistence and defense.

*{the desire of your eyes}* Namely, your wives, vers.16.

*{that which your soul pitieth}* Heb. *the pity of your soul*. That which you are most jealous and tender of.

*{your daughters whom you have left}* In Judea: for the Prophet spoke to those which had been carried away prisoners with Jehoiachin.

**Verse 22.** *{You shall do as I have done}* You shall be forced to digest all these sorrows, not daring so much, as to seem to be grieved thereat, for fear of angering your enemies: or because, that in so great a desolation, every one shall think upon himself, without taking any care for others: Or, because that there will an express curse of God be perceived in it: Or, for that there will be more cause of weeping for these that are alive, and languish in sorrow, than for the dead, See Job 27.15. Psal.78.64. Jer.16.5. Amos 6.10. and Jer.22.10.

*{ye shall not cover}* Vers.17. Jer.16.6,7.

**Verse 23.** *{but you shall pine away for your iniquities}* Chap.33.10. Lev.26.39. In a desperate and stupid senselessness you shall stand astonished and waste away, Amos 6.10.

*{and mourn one towards another}* Bemoan yourselves one to another, for your miseries, and wonder at them; rather than knowing the plague of your sins in your heart, 1 King.8.29. mourn to God in true repentance.

**Verse 24.** *{a sign}* Chap.12.6. and 43. Esay 8.18.

**Verse 25.** *{In the day when I take}* In the time when I shall have fulfilled these prophecies, by the taking and ruin of Jerusalem, I will cause tidings thereof to be brought unto you, and then shall you put the people in mind of these things, for their instruction and conviction.

*{that whereupon they set their minds}* Heb. *the lifting up of their soul*; that is, that which they desire most; all those things which are most dear unto them.

**Verse 26.** *{He that escapeth in that day}* Of my visitation, and their destruction. See the accomplishment of this, Chap.33.21,22. The distance of time betwixt the taking of Jerusalem, and that tidings brought to Ezekiel, was one year, five months, and twenty six days. No doubt, but the Prophet heard of it long before that time. But at that time he received the most full and perfect relation, of all that Nebuzaradan also had done, since the taking of the city. And that was the time that it pleased the Lord to open his Prophet's mouth again.

**Verse 27.** *{In that day shall thy mouth be opened}* See Chap.3.26,27. and 33.22. not that he was miraculously struck dumb in the meantime; for we may not multiply miracles, without a just and sufficient warrant. But he became as dumb, for holding his peace; as if God had said, You have spoken enough already to this people for to warn them sufficiently; forbear that argument any more: let them alone, until that by the bringing to pass of these things you have already told them, you may thereupon take occasion to convince and waken them again. And therefore in the meantime, he prophesies only against the foreign nations, from this place to Chap.33. that is, from the time of beginning the siege, in the ninth year, tenth month, tenth day, till this news here spoken of was brought to the Prophet, in the twelfth year, tenth month, fifth day. And so in that meantime, he prophesies against Egypt, on the tenth year, tenth month, twelfth day, Chap.29.1. And again on the eleventh year, first month, seventh day, Chap.30.20. And again on the eleventh year, third month, first day, Chap.31.1.

## CHAPTER 25

**Verse 1.** *{The word of the LORD}* In this chapter, the Prophet denounces God's vengeance against the Ammonites, against the Moabites; against the Edomites: against the Philistines, for their insolency and insulting over the Jews in their calamity.

**Verse 2.** *{Set thy face}* Chap.20.46. and 6.2.

*{against the Ammonites}* Chap.21.28. Jer.49.1. Amos 1.13. Zeph.2.8.

**Verse 3.** *{Thou saidst, Aha}* ver.6. because you have injuriously scoffed and joyed at the miseries of my people, Chap.21.28. Zeph.2.8.

**Verse 4.** *{to the men of the East}* Heb. *children*, the Babylonians, ver.10. Jer.15.21.

*{palaces in thee}* Namely, their holds, fitted for war, and for defense of the country.

**Verse 5.** *{Rabbah}* Which was the chief city of the Ammonites, and full of conduits, 2 Sam.11.1. and 12.26,27.

**Verse 6.** *{because, &c.}* Their sin and punishment both again insisted upon, and foretold.

*{clapped thine hands}* Heb. *hand*. This here is a sign of joy, derision, and insulting.

*{feet}* Heb. *foot*.

*{heart}* Heb. *soul*.

**Verse 8.** *{because that Moab}* Jer.48.1, &c. Amos 2.1,2,3. See Esay 16.6, &c.

*{Seir}* This was Edom's, the Idumeans' country, Gen.36.8. Deut.2.1,4. bordering upon the Moabites: whereby these nations did oftentimes join against the Jews; and joined here in their derision.

*{house of Judah}* Though she boast of having alone the true God, yet he has not protected her, no more than the gods of other nations have protected them against the Babylonians: words of contempt and blasphemy against God himself, as 2 King.18.33,34,35.

**Verse 9.** *{I will open the side of Moab}* Heb. *shoulder of Moab*. I will cause the Chaldeans to break through that part of the country, where the strong and frontier cities are, which are here undernamed: for a difference between that, and the other part of the land, which lay open, and was not fortified, Jer.48.

*{from his Cities}* Har, and Haroher, seated upon the river Arnon, the border of Moab. See Num.21.28. Deut.2.9,36. Josh.12.2.

*{Bethjeshimoth}* Josh.13.20. and 12.3.

*{Baal-meon}* Numb.32.38.

*{Kiriathaim}* Numb.32.37. See Jer.48.1. and ver.23.

**Verse 10.** *{with the Ammonites}* Or, *against the children of Ammon*. As I have already adjudged the Ammonites, and by my sentence have given them up to the Babylonians, vers.4. So I do the Moabites.

*{and will give them}* Have given them by my prophetical sentence and doom already.

**Verse 12.** *{Because that Edom}* Jer.49.7,8. Ezek. [illegible reference]. Amos 1.11. Obad.1.1. Out of their ancient hatred, derived from Genes.27.43.

*{by taking vengeance}* Heb. *by revenging revengement*. See the like, ver.15. for the title of birthright, which Jacob did get away from Esau the father of the Edomites: Or of David's conquering Edom, 1 Chron.18.13. See Ezek.35.5. Amos 1.11.

*{and hath greatly}* Psal.137.7.

**Verse 13.** *{Teman}* Jer.49.7,8. the place, or country denominated from Teman, the grandchild of Esau, Gen.36.15.

*{They of Dedan shall fall by the sword}* Or, *they shall fall by the sword unto Dedan*, Jer.49.8. of the posterity of Abraham by Keturah, Gen.25.3.

**Verse 14.** *{by the hand of my people Israel}* We read nothing of this execution done by the Jews upon the Edomites, but only, 1 Mac.5.3. and 2 Mac.10.16,17. Some do understand this of that same hand which overthrew God's people Israel, the hand of the Babylonians. Some spiritually, of the Church's victory over her deadly enemies, as the Edomites were to the Jews; as Isa.11.14. Jer.49.2.

*{They shall know}* Namely, my people: or Edom: shall know by experience.

**Verse 15.** *{Because the Philistines}* See Jer.25.20. and 47.1,4,5.

*{have dealt by revenge}* See 2 Chron.28.18. Amos 1.6.

*{for the old hatred}* Or, *with perpetual hatred*.

**Verse 16.** *{Cherethims}* Which were a part of the Philistines, of whom mention is in the verse before. Confer with this 1 Sam.30.14,16. Zeph.2.5. Of the Cherethims David had also a guard, 2 Sam.8.18. which most likely were out of some colonies of the Israelites, that then were placed in their room.

*{Sea Coast}* Or, *Haven of the Sea*; upon which was the Philistines' land, and had many haven towns. Hezekiah had formerly spoiled them, 2 King.18.8. And after that Psammeticus, father of Pharaoh-Necho king of Egypt, as Herodotus relates in his Euterpe. But this last was the most grievous, by the Chaldeans. See Esay 14.29, &c. Jer.25.20. and Chap.47.

**Verse 17.** *{Vengeance}* Heb. *Vengeances*.

## CHAPTER 26

**Verse 1.** *{And it came to pass}* In this chapter the Prophet denounces God's fearful judgments against Tyrus, for insulting against Jerusalem: and to be executed by Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon; the destruction and desolation he makes: The isles tremble at it; for so woeful and irrevocable a downfall.

*{The eleventh year}* Of the captivity of Jeconiah, and the reign of Zedekiah.

*{month}* Some say it was the first, Ezek.40.1. But it rather seems to be the fifth; that is to say, the first after the taking of Jerusalem: which was the cause of the Tyrians insulting, which is here mentioned. See Jerem.52.6.

**Verse 2.** *{because that Tyrus hath said against Jerusalem, Aha,}* The sin of Tyrus, Amos 1.9. Zach.9.2,3. Thus Chap.25.3.

*{the gates of the people}* That is, the famous city Jerusalem, whereunto all people resorted, both for religion's sake, and for traffic. A manner of speech taken from that, that near the chief gates of the city, there were commonly great places for market meetings, and law pleas, &c.

*{she is turned unto me}* That is to say, I being the second city in Palestine for commodiousness and repute, shall obtain all those privileges which Jerusalem now has, for traffic, concourse of people, and for wealth.

*{be replenished}* My riches and fame shall increase. Thus they promise mountains of gold to themselves; and thus the wicked rejoice at their fall by whom they have any profit or advantage.

**Verse 3.** *{I am against thee}* God's judgment upon her for her sins. See Esay 23. Jer.25.22. and Chap.47.4. Amos 1.9. Zach.9.4.

*{Many Nations}* Or, *great Nations*; Namely, the Chaldeans' army, composed of divers mighty nations, ver.7.

**Verse 4.** *{scrape her dust from her}* That is, I will destroy it utterly. This did not befall Tyrus the first time the Chaldeans took it, Isa.23.15. But the ruin of it began then; and then from time to time it came to that degree at last as the Prophet speaks of, in Alexander the Great in his time.

*{the top of rock}* Ver.14. Chap.24.7,8.

**Verse 5.** *{The spreading of nets}* As who should say a waste and desolate place; as those shores are on which fishers do use to spread their nets.

*{the midst of the Sea}* Because that Tyrus was an island encompassed with the sea.

**Verse 6.** *{her daughters}* The towns that belonged unto her, as a mother city; or rather, her people, without sparing of either sex.

**Verse 7.** *{Nebuchadnezzar}* Shows now by whom this judgment shall be executed, Chap.28.7. and 30.11. and 32.12. This name Nebuchadnezzar, is no less than seven divers ways written in Scripture. There were divers of this name; insomuch, as some think this to be a common name to the kings of Babylon; as Pharaoh was to the kings of Egypt; Abimelech to the kings of the Philistines; Sylvius to the Latin kings: Caesar and Augustus to the Roman emperors; Arsacides to the Persians: and Cecropides to the Athenians. But this Nebuchadnezzar appears by the sacred history to be the same that had taken Jehojakim, Jehojacin, and Zedekiah, destroyed Jerusalem, burnt the Temple; and in after times, through God's just judgment, became mad for seven years, as a mad beast. We may not then take this Nebuchadnezzar to be him that is mentioned in the apocryphal story of Judith; and much less to be either Cyrus, or Alexander the Great; as some have weakly imagined.

*{a King of kings}* Namely, him that is possessor of this mighty monarchy, which calls itself the universal. See Jer.27.6,7. Dan.2.37. See the phrase, *King of kings*, Ezra 7.12. and such like is the phrase, *God of gods*, Deut.10.17.

*{from the North}* From Chaldea, which was partly northward from Tyre and Palestine.

**Verse 8.** *{cast a mount}* Or, *pour out the engine of shot*.

*{and lift up a buckler against thee}* That is, coming to the assault.

**Verse 10.** *{Chariots}* Chap.23.24.

*{as men enter into a City}* Heb. *according to the enterings of a City broken up*.

**Verse 11.** *{strong garrisons}* Or, *pillars of strength*: for Tyrus was built and fortified by much art, and labor of men, against the sea, and against all enemies.

**Verse 12.** *{Thy pleasant houses}* Heb. *houses of thy desire*.

*{in the midst of the water}* The sea round about you.

**Verse 13.** *{And I will cause the noise}* And so this expression used Esay 24.8. Jer.7.34. and 16.9. and 25.10. Hos.2.11.

*{thy songs to cease}* The Tyrians having at all times been much given to music, Chap.28.13. Esay 23.16.

**Verse 14.** *{Thou shalt be a place to spread nets upon}* I will make you so bare, that you shall have nothing to cover you, verse 4,5.

*{shalt be built no more}* At least, it shall be no building, in comparison of that in its former glory.

**Verse 15.** *{Shall not the Isles}* The horror and consternation of the isles and neighboring nations, being astonished at her fall, Chap.32.10.

**Verse 16.** *{clothe themselves with trembling}* At the hearing of her fall, and for fear of the Chaldeans.

**Verse 17.** *{A Lamentation for thee}* Rev.18.9.

*{of Seafaring men}* Heb. *of the Seas*. Meaning merchants, which by their traffic did enrich her wonderfully, and increase her power.

*{her inhabitants}* Those that were born and brought up there. See Esay 23.8.

*{on all that haunt it}* Haunt the sea; neighbors and strangers.

**Verse 18.** *{at thy departure}* Into exile, likely, and captivity.

**Verse 19.** *{the deep upon thee}* As ver.3.

**Verse 20.** *{thee down, with them that descend}* When I shall cause you utterly to perish as many nations and cities have done before, especially in the general deluge, to which he may seem to have some relation; having before likened the invasion of Tyrus to an overflowing.

*{with}* Or, *to*.

*{of old time}* Which were dead long ago.

*{I will set glory}* I will gloriously reestablish my people; and cause them to live and flourish again, for to be the honor of the world. See Chap.20.6. and 28.25,26.

*{Land of the living}* Psal.27.13. Esay 38.11. The meaning here is, in Judea, when it shall be restored. See Chap.28.25,26. Or else thus, By your destruction I will glorify myself among the living.

**Verse 21.** *{I will make thee a terror}* Heb. *terrors*, Chap.27.36. and 28.19. You shall be brought into such an extreme desolation, full of horror in yourself, and a subject of terror to others. Others render it, *I will bring thee to nothing and to perdition.*

*{shalt be no more}* Verse 14.

## CHAPTER 27

**Verse 1.** *{The word of the LORD}* In this chapter the Prophet sets down a lamentation for Tyrus; by setting forth her former glory, in her building, shipping, army, power, merchandisings, praise; and then opposing to it her present ruin; concluding with the extreme wailing and astonishment of the neighboring isles, nations, and kingdoms, at her fall.

**Verse 3.** *{The entry of the Sea}* Built there first by the Sidonians, as Justin relates, in a very commodious place to resort unto by sea, from divers places, and to carry merchandise from thence into the continent or firm land.

*{of perfect beauty}* Heb. *perfect of beauty*.

**Verse 4.** *{Thy borders}* You are encompassed round with the sea.

*{midst}* Heb. *heart*.

**Verse 5.** *{made}* Heb. *built*.

*{of Senir}* This mountain was Hermon, called by the Sidonians, Syrion; but the Amorites called it Senir, Deut.3.9. by Moses called, Sion, Deut.4.48.

**Verse 6.** *{the Company of the Ashurites}* Heb. *the daughter*.

*{Thy benches of Ivory}* Or, *They have made thy benches of Ivory well trodden.* Ivory wherewith they covered the walls and floors of chambers, and the decks of ships. Which shows their profuse riot, and excess of delicacy.

*{of Chittim}* Cilicia, rather than Italy. See Gen.10.4. Num.24.24. Esay 23.1. Jerem.2.10. 1 Macc.1.1. Cilicia joins to Syria. In Tharsus, the chief city thereof was Paul born, Act.21.39. and 22.3.

**Verse 7.** *{blue and purple}* Or, *purple and scarlet*.

*{of Elishah}* It is thought, that thereby are meant the Eolians, a people of Greece. Elishah is Javan's son, Japhet's grandchild, Gen.10.4.

*{which covered thee}* Namely, the poops of your ships; Or, which was your raiment.

**Verse 8.** *{Arvad}* It is thought to be the island, called Arado, and Ararado, near to Sidon, Gen.10.18.

*{that were in thee}* Other nations were your mariners; but your own Tyrians were your pilots, and chief in your navy.

**Verse 9.** *{of Gebal}* A prince in Phoenicia, and nigh to Sidon.

*{Calkers}* Or, *stoppers of chinks*. Heb. *strengtheners*, Meaning, the chief of this nation, which were cunning shipwrights, were employed in mending and making of shipping.

**Verse 10.** *{of Lud}* See Genes.10.13. inhabitants of part of Egypt.

*{Phut}* In Libya, Gen.10.6.

*{they hanged the shield}* According to the custom of garrison soldiers.

*{they set forth thy comeliness}* These soldiers being there continually, did much increase your splendor and majesty.

**Verse 11.** *{The men of Arvad with thine Army}* Those of Arvad which lived by the seacoast, ver.8. and your own soldiers, kept a continual watch about the city.

*{And the Gammadims were in thy towers}* Dwelling in a province of Phoenicia.

**Verse 12.** *{Tarshish}* Namely, the people that lived along the coast of the Mediterranean Sea, and especially the city of Tharsus, a Tyrian colony. Others will have it to be Carthage. Some

*{they traded in thy fairs}* That is, these things they gave for your commodities.

**Verse 13.** *{Javan}* That is, Greece, *Javan* was *Japhet's* son, Gen.10.2. of whom came the Greeks. See Dan.8.21. Joel 3.6.

*{Tubal}* The posterity of *Tubal Japhet's* son, Gen.10.2. inhabiting likely in a part of Cappadocia.

*{Meshech}* *Tubal* and *Meshech* are usually joined together. So Chap.32.26. and 38.2. and 39.1. being brethren, Gen.10.2.

*{persons of men}* Heb. *soul of man*: that is, souls of men, or, persons, by selling slaves. See Apoc.18.13.

*{Market}* Or, *Merchandise*.

**Verse 14.** *{Togarmah}* Chap.38. posterity of the grandchild of *Japhet*, Gen.10.3. thought to inhabit the lesser Armenia; some say, the lesser Asia.

*{horsemen}* Men which were expert in managing and taming of horses.

**Verse 15.** *{Dedan}* Ver.20. people of Arabia of Cham's posterity, by Cush, and Rhamah. Or of Abraham by Keturah, Gen.25.3. dwelling in some part of Syria, Gen.10.6,7.

*{they brought thee for a present}* To have the benefit of being free of your fairs and markets; or else to traffic therewith.

*{horns of ivory and ebony}* So the great teeth of the elephant, which on either side of his mouth stand out, are called; because by their crookedness they are like horns.

**Verse 16.** *{the wares of thy making}* Heb. *thy works*.

*{Agate}* Heb. *Chrysoprase*.

**Verse 17.** *{wheat}* By 1 King.5.9. Act.12.20. it appears, that Tyrus was furnished with corn out of the land of Israel.

*{Minnith}* The name of a town, upon the border of the Ammonites, on the south side of Canaan: whereof mention is Judg.11.33. where the best wheat grew.

*{Pannag}* Some will have it to be the name of a country; and the name of Phoenicia to be taken thence. Others translate it rosin, or balsam.

*{balm}* Or, *rosin*, or *turpentine*, wherein Gilead abounded, Gen.37.25. Jer.8.22. and 46.11.

**Verse 18.** *{Helbon}* A place of Syria, which is thought by some to be that which at this time is called Aleppo.

**Verse 19.** *{Dan}* Likely he means those which after the captivity of the ten tribes, came and inhabited the ancient country belonging to *Dan*, in Laish, a city near to Tyre, Judg.18.28,29.

*{Going to and fro}* Or, *Meuzal*: to seek what they may give for your commodities. The Greeks have been always great travelers: and the trade at Tyrus so great, that those countries which had nothing to exchange, went to other countries to get something, to trade with Tyre.

**Verse 20.** *{Dedan}* ver.15.

*{precious clothes for chariots}* Heb. *clothes of freedom*. It may be, they were such, as wherewith they covered their horses and chariots, to sit upon them.

**Verse 21.** *{Kedar}* Which came from Kedar the son of Ishmael Gen.25.13. inhabiting Arabia Petrea, which country abounded with cattle, Isa.60.7. See Psal.120.5.

*{they occupied with thee in lambs}* Heb. *they were the merchants of thy hand*. Or, *they occupied with thee at hand in lambs*, &c. for lambs and such like cattle could not be carried far, as other wares.

**Verse 22.** *{Shebah, &c.}* Names of Arabia Felix. See Gen.10.7.

**Verse 23.** *{Haran, &c.}* Gen.11.31,32. and 12.4,5. places of Mesopotamia.

*{Ashur}* *Northern Nations.*

*{Chilmad}* It is thought to be Media, or some part thereof.

**Verse 24.** *{All sorts}* Or, *excellent things*.

*{blue clothes}* Heb. *foldings*. Namely, silks and wools dyed in this noble color, inclining to a violet.

**Verse 25.** *{The ships of Tarshish did sing of thee in thy market;}* That is, they which were carried in the ships, did praise you for your wares. Others, read it according to the Hebrew, *The ships of Tarshish were thy chief, and thy caravans*: as those places which are haunted with wild beasts upon the land, are traveled through with great companies of travelers, and loaded cattle; so do the ships flock towards you in great numbers together.

*{very glorious}* Enriched in goods.

**Verse 26.** *{Thy rowers have brought thee into great waters}* The ruin of Tyre. You have been like unto a well furnished ship, brought into the main sea of greatness, riches, and power, by your governors, and there left to suffer shipwreck.

*{the East-wind}* Most tempestuous and dangerous in those countries, Psal.48.7. meaning Nebuchadnezzar with his army, which lay east from Tyre.

*{midst}* Heb. *heart*.

**Verse 27.** *{Thy riches and thy fairs}* Rev.18.9, &c.

*{thy calkers}* Heb. *those which mend thy breaches*: Which some take, for the breaches of walls, and buildings.

*{And in all}* Or, *even with all*.

*{shall fall into the midst of the seas}* Heb. *heart*. Shall be all infolded in your ruin. A continuation of the same similitude of a broken ship.

**Verse 28.** *{The suburbs shall shake, &c.}* Or, *waves*. Heb. *the boats*. The Hebrew word seems to signify a cockboat, which they throw out of the ship, it signifies also suburbs, which are as it were expelled the city. The meaning is, that the cities and colonies subject to Tyre, should be surprised with terror, at the fall and ruin of their chief city: and all other lesser states should be likewise so terrified.

**Verse 30.** *{Against thee},* or for, or over, ver.31. Rev.18.11,15–16.

*{dust upon their heads}* Which is a sign of great sorrow and mourning, Jer.6.26.

**Verse 33.** *{when thy wares went forth out of the seas}* You were the magazine, and staple of victual and merchandise, which you did transport by sea, and distribute them to divers nations.

**Verse 34.** *{Thou shalt be broken by the sea}* That is, by the overflowing of wars, ver.26. See Chap.26.19. Isa.8.7,8. and 28.2. and 59.19. Psal.124.

**Verse 36.** *{Hiss at thee}* In token of wonder. See 1 King.9.8.

*{a terror}* Heb. *terrors*: a subject of terror to all men, by reason of your terrible fall. Or, You that have been a terror, are brought to nothing, Chap.26.21.

*{never shalt be any more}* Heb. *shalt not be for ever*. See the annotations on Chap.26.4,14.

## CHAPTER 28

**Verse 1.** *{The word of the LORD}* In this chapter the Prophet sets down the blasphemous pride, and presumptuous confidence of the king of Tyrus: God's great judgments against him: and a lamentation which the Prophet is commanded to take up, at his fall. Afterwards he prophesies against Zidon; and concludes with a prophetical promise of the restoration of God's own people.

**Verse 2.** *{I am a God}* You are grown vain, and have exalted yourself, through your glory, wisdom, and happiness, as if you were no more a mortal man, but a petty god.

*{I sit in the seat of God}* I reign in a most strong place, full of glory and delights; which are the properties belonging to heaven, which is God's habitation.

*{midst of the seas}* Heb. *heart*, Chap.27.4.

*{yet thou art a man, and not God}* Isa.31.3.

*{though thou set thine heart, as the heart of God}* You did think yourself to be endowed with divine, more than human understanding, and wisdom; the Tyrians' wisdom being very famous, Zach.9.2.

**Verse 3.** *{Behold}* Zach.9.2.

*{wiser}* In your own conceit and opinion.

*{Daniel}* Who for the incomparable gifts of God's Holy Spirit, as well in prophecy, as in counsel and wisdom, Dan.1.17. and 4.9. was by a common proverb set as a pattern of wisdom in Babylon, where Ezekiel then prophesied. See Chap.14.14.

**Verse 4.** *{Riches}* Or, *power*.

**Verse 5.** *{By thy great wisdom}* Heb. *by thy greatness of thy wisdom*.

**Verse 7.** *{Strangers}* Those Chap.26.7.

*{the terrible}* Chap.30.11. and 26.7.

*{against the beauty}* By strength they will overcome all your counsels, and arts of prevention and defense. Or, they will kill you, not respecting your wisdom, which makes you so majestical and venerable.

**Verse 8.** *{The deaths}* Namely, of all kinds of violent death, in the general slaughter of your people. See Isa.14.19.

**Verse 9.** *{Slayeth}* Or, *woundeth*.

**Verse 10.** *{The uncircumcised}* Like the rest of the heathen and infidels, which are God's enemies: for circumcision was a sign of the Covenant of saving grace. Others, like to the ancient Canaanites, condemned to a curse, See Chap.32.21.

**Verse 12.** *{A lamentation}* As Chap.27.2, &c.

*{thou sealest up the sum}* Rule the matters in the affairs of merchandise. Or, you make up the account: a term which may seem to be taken from them which tell money.

*{full of wisdom}* He derides the vain opinion and confidence, that the king of Tyre had in his riches, strength and pleasures.

**Verse 13.** *{In Eden}* Like to a tree planted in an earthly paradise, Gen.2.8. See the like said of Ashur, Chap.31. verse 8,9.

*{Sardius}* Or, *Ruby*.

*{beryl}* Or, *Chrysolite*.

*{Emerald}* Or, *Chrysoprase*.

*{workmanship}* He has relation to that, that the art of music was always held in great esteem amongst the Tyrians, Chap.26.13. Esay 23.16.

*{in the day that thou wast created}* Even at the first founding of your state, music began to flourish in you, and all pleasures.

**Verse 14.** *{Anointed}* Endowed by God with several prerogatives.

*{Cherub}* Amongst the great ones of the world, which are God's ministers upon earth, you were exalted in glory, even as the heavenly Cherubims are.

*{covereth}* Being a secure defense to your people, covering them with your wings; which are attributed to this king, because he is called Cherub, whose shape and form is set down in the Scripture with wings, which shadowed the Ark.

*{the holy mountain}* Having likened him to a Cherub, he compares his seat, being full of magnificence, to Mount Sinai, where God appeared with millions of his angels, having a fiery pavement under his feet, Exod.24.10.

*{in the midst of the stones of fire}* To wit, among my people Israel, which shined as precious stones: or, clad with a garment of pearl and precious stones, glittering, and sparkling, as fire such as were in the breastplate of Israel's high priest; as appears by comparing with this the verse before. Or rather, as having power among, and over the fiery thunderbolts, and meteors.

**Verse 15.** *{Perfect in thy ways}* Which may be also understood of the state and condition, or of the enterprises, ver.12.

*{from the day that thou wast created}* From the time you were first a settled kingdom; as ver.13. He may haply seem to allude to the fall of wicked angels.

*{till iniquity was found in thee}* Whilst you have continued in justice and integrity, I have borne you up; but since you and your city have given yourselves to violences, deceits, and other manner of wickednesses, your ruin has been ready at hand.

**Verse 16.** *{I will cast thee}* That is, *I will subvert thy glorious state.*

*{the mountain of God}* Ver.14. your seat and state, which you did esteem so divine.

*{covering Cherub}* ver.14.

*{from the midst of the stones of fire}* See annotations on ver.14.

**Verse 17.** *{Thou hast corrupted thy wisdom}* Your glory and greatness have caused you to lose your understanding, and wise carriage, for which your predecessors were admired; and your own splendor has dazzled you.

**Verse 18.** *{Defiled thy sanctuary}* Your sacred places in your esteem; and your seats of justice, you have profaned with all manner of impieties, and iniquities.

*{a fire from the midst of thee}* He seems to intimate, that the occasion of the ruin of Tyre did proceed from itself, by some means, or accident, which the history does not specify.

**Verse 19.** *{Thou shalt be a terror}* Heb. *terrors*, Chap.26.21. and 27.36.

*{and never shalt thou be any more}* See annotations on Chap.26.4,14.

**Verse 21.** *{Set thy face against Zidon}* Isa.23.4,12. Jer.25.22. and 27.3.

**Verse 22.** *{I will be glorified}* Exod.14.4. in the manifestation of my justice, and my power, exercised over you.

*{be sanctified in her}* Lev.10.3. I shall give sufficient cause to be feared and worshiped; yea, blessed and praised.

**Verse 24.** *{A pricking brier}* Num.33.55. Josh.23.13.

**Verse 25.** *{when I shall have gathered}* This prophecy has in part, and according to the letter, a relation to the return from the captivity of Babylon; but perfectly and in spirit, to the spiritual reestablishing of the Church by the Messiah.

*{sanctified in them}* He shows for what cause God will assemble his Church, and preserve it still, though he destroy his enemies; to wit, that they should praise him, and give thanks for his great mercies.

**Verse 26.** *{Safely}* Or, *with confidence*.

*{despise}* Or, *spoil*.

## CHAPTER 29

**Verse 1.** *{In}* In this chapter are two grievous prophecies, against Pharaoh and Egypt. One in the time of Jerusalem's siege. Another about 16 years after Jerusalem was taken: with a promise, both of Egypt's restoration, though not in its former splendor and power, at the end of 40 years, after it had been laid waste by Nebuchadnezzar; and also of some good and comfort to God's people at that time.

*{the tenth year}* To wit, of the captivity of Jeconiah, or of the reign of Zedekiah; that is, one year, and two days, after the siege of Jerusalem was begun.

**Verse 2.** *{Pharaoh}* This was *Pharaoh Hophra*, Jer.44.30.

*{against all Egypt}* Isa.19. and 20. chapter, Jer.25.19. and 46. chapter.

**Verse 3.** *{Dragon that lieth in the midst}* Chap.32.2.8. Psal.74.13,14. Isa.27.1. and 51.9. The Hebrew word signifies any great water creature. Here is meant the whale, or Leviathan, Job 41. or the crocodile, the figure of Pharaoh.

*{my river is mine own}* Ver.9. I possess a most fruitful kingdom, and so strongly situated by reason of the river Nilus, that it cannot be taken from me by any strength.

*{I have made it for myself}* I do not depend upon any one: as I have gotten, and increased my kingdom of myself so am I sufficient to maintain it against all men.

**Verse 4.** *{But I will put hooks}* See this phrase, Chap.38.4. Esay 37.29.

*{I will bring thee up}* I will draw you out of the country which is so strong, together with all your subjects, which will follow you, to the end, that you being all slain, your bodies may lie for food for the fowls of the air, and the wild beasts of the field, Psal.74.14.

**Verse 5.** *{into the wilderness}* If we may give credit to the history of Herodotus, this is to be understood of the desert of Libya, near Cyrene; where the Cyreneans gave a great overthrow to Pharaoh, soon after he fled from Nebuchadnezzar's host, that left the besieging of Jerusalem, to go against him. Otherwise, if we understand it of the overthrow and conquest, made of him, by Nebuchadnezzar, we must refer it to a later time, according to that prophecy, particularly uttered against him, above sixteen years after this, set down in the latter part of this chapter. vers.17. to the end.

*{thou shalt fall}* Your army: For he escaped back to Egypt.

*{open fields}* Heb. *face of the field*.

*{nor gathered}* To be buried.

**Verse 6.** *{have been}* They have solicited my people to rebel against the Chaldeans, promising them assistance, and have violated their faith; and so have not been only unprofitable, but also very hurtful unto them, Esay 30.1, &c.

*{staff of reed}* vers.7. 2 King.18.21. Isa.36.6. Jer.37.7,8.

**Verse 7.** *{and madest all their loins to be at a stand}* The meaning seems to be this, You have left them to defend themselves with their own strength, and alone to bear the burden of the war, which they had undertaken for your advantage, or at your instigation.

**Verse 8.** *{Therefore}* Jer.43.8. and 44.30. and 46.13, &c. This seems to foretell their desolation by Nebuchadnezzar.

**Verse 9.** *{The river is mine}* vers.3. Thus God cannot suffer that man should arrogate anything to himself, or put his trust in anything, save in him alone.

*{and I have made it}* He speaks in the person of this king, or in general, of all the kings of Egypt, his predecessors, who with great charges and labor, had brought Nilus with channels, over all the land of Egypt.

**Verse 10.** *{utterly waste}* Heb. *wastes of wastes*.

*{from the tower}* From one end of Egypt to the other. See Chap.30.6.

*{Syene}* Heb. *Seveneh*. It has been, and is commonly thought, by reason of the likeness of the name, that it was the city of Syene, standing upon the south confines of Egypt, in Thebais, where there might be some tower, to mark the defect of shadows, in the very day of the summer solstice: as there was a well also to that purpose; as some write.

*{of Ethiopia}* Heb. *of Cush*. That is, to the borders of the Chushites, or Arabians, Gen.10.7. for between Seveneh and Arabia, is situated all Egypt: for Seveneh was south border of Egypt, confronting and joining to Ethiopia; and the Chushites or Arabians, the north border. So we are to understand, Chap.30.4,5,9.

**Verse 11.** *{Neither shall it be inhabited forty years}* vers.12. From the time it shall be destroyed by Nebuchadnezzar.

**Verse 13.** *{At the end of forty years}* Likely about the time, Cyrus took Babylon, Jer.46.26.

**Verse 14.** *{Pathros}* Gen.10.14.

*{habitation}* Or, *birth*.

*{base Kingdom}* Heb. *low*. Namely, subject and tributary to the Persian, who shall people it again. See Chap.17.6,14.

**Verse 16.** *{the confidence}* For any greatness and power the house of Israel shall see hereafter to be in them.

*{which bringeth}* By their former trusting and confidence in them, and not in me. And thereupon breaking their faith and oath to the Babylonians, Chap.21.23,24. and by complying with them also in their idolatry, Chap.8.10.14. and 23.19,20,21. as Ahaz did. 2 Chron.28.22,23.

**Verse 17.** *{Seven and twentieth year}* Counting from the captivity of Jeconiah. Of the order of these prophecies, and how the later sometimes stand before the former, see the annotations on Jer.27.1. This here placed is the last in time of all Ezekiel's prophecies. For that, Chap.40. &c. was almost two full years before this. Two years spent in subduing Moab, Ammon, and neighboring nations; and 13 years in besieging Tyrus, as Josephus relates, *libro 1. cont. Appionem Alexandrinum*, will rise up to this account of 27 years, in which year Tyre was destroyed.

**Verse 18.** *{Caused his army to serve}* Namely, to execute my judgments upon Tyre, and therefore I will requite him.

*{every head was made bald}* That is, they have suffered long and hard labors, and toils, and have worn out themselves about it.

*{for Tyrus}* Or, *from*, or, *out of Tyrus*.

**Verse 20.** *{Take her spoil, and take her prey}* Heb. *spoil her spoil, and prey her prey*.

**Verse 20.** *{For his labour}* Or, *for his hire*.

*{they wrought for me}* To execute my judgments, though they did not know my will, nor had any intent to obey it, Isa.10.7.

**Verse 21.** *{In that day}* When I shall beat down the Egyptians, then will I begin to raise up my people again in Babylon, by the exaltation of Jeconiah, Jer.52.31. which certainly caused a great deal of ease, and liberty to the Jewish prisoners. See Dan.2. 2 King.25.27, &c.

*{bud}* Psal.132.17.

*{I will give thee}* The like words see Chap.24.27. and 33.22. The meaning is, I will make it so, that you Ezekiel, as all the rest of the true believers, may freely, and with open mouths, praise the Lord in your assemblies.

## CHAPTER 30

**Verse 1.** *{Then the word}* A prophecy of the destruction and desolation of Egypt, and her helpers and confederates, by Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon. And again repeated, in the eleventh year, first month, seventh day of Jehojacin's captivity.

**Verse 2.** *{Howl ye}* Namely, you Egyptians and other nations, which are subject to, and confederates with you.

*{woe worth the day}* To wit, the day of the ruin of Egypt; whereof he speaks hereafter.

**Verse 3.** *{A cloudy day}* Of horrible and strange calamities: as rainy days are very rare and strange in Egypt.

*{heathen}* In which the profane nations, Egyptians, Chushites, and others, their confederates, shall be visited, and punished.

**Verse 4.** *{Pain}* Or, *fear*.

*{Ethiopia}* Heb. *Chush*. A neighboring and confederate nation with Egypt; a people of Arabia.

**Verse 5.** *{Libya, and Lydia}* Heb. *Phut and Lud*. Here are meant two nations, auxiliaries to the Egyptians; the one a people living in Asia; the other, in Africa, or if both in Africa, Lydia seems to be Mareotis in Egypt, or Cyrene, the southern parts of Africa. See Gen.10.6,13,22. Esay 66.19.

*{mingled people}* See Jer.25.20,24. auxiliaries likewise to the Egyptians.

*{Chub}* The name of some part of Africa, not mentioned elsewhere; yet most likely here are meant the inhabitants of the inner Libya, by the river Niger. See Ptolemy in the fourth book of his Geography.

*{men of the land}* Heb. *children*.

*{that is in league}* It might be some land that was confederate with Egypt: or some country of divers nations, which governed themselves in commonalties, which were confederate one with another.

**Verse 6.** *{They also that uphold}* Namely, those armies, or nations, which shall take their parts, or relieve them.

*{Syene}* Which was a strong city of Egypt, Chap.29.10. on the south border of it.

**Verse 7.** *{And they shall be desolate}* Chap.29.12.

**Verse 8.** *{Fire in Egypt}* By the Chaldeans indeed, but by my inward and secret working in them.

*{destroyed}* Heb. *broken*.

**Verse 9.** *{From me}* Namely, from Egypt; but by my secret instinct and providence.

*{in ships}* Upon the Red Sea, Isa.18.2.

*{as in the day of Egypt}* As formerly all neighboring nations were astonished at the report, of this plague that shall now come upon Egypt. Or, at the report of the Egyptians' plagues, and final discomfiture, of old, in the Red Sea, Exod.7,8,9,10,12,14. chapters.

*{it cometh}* To wit, the foresaid calamity.

**Verse 11.** *{the terrible}* Chap.26.7. and 28.7.

**Verse 12.** *{And I}* Isa.19.5.

*{will make the rivers dry}* Heb. *drought*. That which is most fruitful by reason of the abundance of waters which are in it, shall become barren, and desolate, as if it were scorched up with the sun. Or, I will make the rivers thereof to be no safeguard for it; but I will dry them up, to make the way plain and easy for the Babylonian to come, and destroy it. See Jer.50.38. and 51.32.

*{all that is therein}* Heb. *the fullness thereof*.

**Verse 13.** *{destroy the Idols}* Zech.13.2. Jer.43.12. and 46.25.

*{Noph}* Called *Moph*, Hosh.9.6. afterward by the ancients, *Memphis*; seated upon Nile in Egypt on the borders of Ethiopia. See Esay 19.13. Jer.46.14.

*{Prince of the land of Egypt}* Heb. *out of the land*; that is, a native, or Egyptians shall rule them; but strangers.

**Verse 14.** *{Pathros}* The name of a country in Egypt. See Chap.29.14.

*{Zoan}* Which is Tanais, one of the chief cities of Egypt. See Num.13.22.

*{No}* Jer.46.25. Nah.3.8. which is Alexandria.

**Verse 15.** *{Sin}* Which is Pelusium, a strong frontier city of Egypt, over against Arabia Petrea. Whence likely the wilderness of Sin has its name.

**Verse 17.** *{Aven}* Or, *On*. Which is the city Heliopolis, Gen.41.45.

*{Pi-beseth}* Called by authors *Bubastum*.

*{These cities shall go into captivity}* Ver.18. to wit, the multitude of people which is in them.

**Verse 18.** *{Tehaphnehes}* This is the city of Daphne, called here Thehaphnes, and Tahpanhes, Jer.43.7. likely from the name of that queen, 1 King.11.19. This seems to be as it were the gate of Egypt, at which the Chaldeans came in, Jer.43.7,8,9.

*{darkened}* Or, *restrained*. An extreme calamity, desolation, and confusion shall overtake them.

*{yokes}* Their imperious tyranny. See Lev.26.13.

*{a cloud shall cover her}* As if one should say, a sudden storm of evils, ver.3.

*{daughters shall go into captivity}* That is, the cities which are subject to the city royal, Chap.26.6.

**Verse 20.** *{eleventh year}* Of the captivity of Jeconiah, or Zedekiah's reign. In which year Jerusalem was taken, three months and two days after,

**Verse 21.** *{broken the arm}* This has relation, either to the first overthrow of the Egyptians under Neco at Carchemish, Jer.46.2. 2 Chr.35.20. 2 King.23.29. Or rather, to that newly received by the Cyreneans, as was showed upon Chap.29.5. After which loss they could never recover, nor defend themselves, against the Chaldeans. See Chap.29.14,15.

*{it shall not be bound up}* Jer.46.11. See Esay 1.6.

**Verse 22.** *{break his arms}* His forces and powers.

**Verse 24.** *{sword in his hand}* To execute my judgments.

**Verse 25.** *{when I shall put my sword}* Whereby we see, that tyrants have no power of themselves, neither can do any more harm, than God appoints; and when he will, they must cease, Esay 10.5.

## CHAPTER 31

**Verse 1.** *{And it came to pass}* In this chapter the Prophet confirms the truth of his former prophecy concerning the overthrow of Pharaoh, and Egypt, by the example of the like judgment upon the Assyrian; setting forth the Assyrian's pride, his downfall; and how others were affected with it: with an application of it to Pharaoh.

*{in the eleventh year}* Of Zedekiah's reign; or Jeconiah's captivity, Cap.1.2. one month, and 24 days, since the former prophecy, Chap.30.20. and one month and 8 days before the taking of Jerusalem, Jer.52.5,6.

**Verse 2.** *{whom art thou like}* Meaning, that the Egyptian was not like in strength to the king of the Assyrians, whom the Babylonians overcame. This again is repeated in the conclusion, ver.18. see Isa.23.13. where the example is to be understood, of the Assyrian formerly overcoming the Babylonian; as more appears, 2 King.17.24,30. and 10.12.

**Verse 3.** *{Cedar in Lebanon}* See Chap.17.3,22,23. Dan.4.10.

*{with fair branches}* Heb. *fair of branches*.

**Verse 4.** *{Waters}* The Assyrian was grown great through abundance of all prosperity.

*{made him great}* Or, *nourished*.

*{set him up}* Or, *brought him up*.

*{with her rivers}* The great sea of his happiness did seem to run round, as it does at the coming in of great rivers, at the foot of this tree: and he, the Assyrian out of his grace and plenty, seemed to send forth some small streams out of it to other kingdoms; alluding likely herein, to the waters of *Nilus*, Chap.29.3.

*{little rivers}* Or, *conduits*.

**Verse 5.** *{when he shot forth}* Or, *when it sent them forth*.

**Verse 6.** *{Fowls of heaven}* Dan.4.12. The greatest part of mankind, retired under dominion and protection of the Assyrian.

**Verse 8.** *{Cedars in the garden}* Gen.2. Signifying that there was no greater power in the world, than that was of the Assyrian.

**Verse 10.** *{Mighty one of the heathen}* Vers.12. Chap.28.7. and 30.11. that is of the Babylonian. Some understand this of Merodac-Baladan, who, as they say, overthrew Esarhaddon the Assyrian: but Ptolemy shows in his *Can. Regum*, that this Merodac died before Esarhaddon (otherwise called the great and noble Asnapper, Ezr.4.2,10.) reigned, and that Esarhaddon left Saosduchinus his successor in the Assyrian and Babylonian empire. This mighty one here is rather to be understood of Nebuchadnezzar, who after was the Babylonian monarch, and only ruler of the world.

*{he shall surely deal with him}* Heb. *in doing he shall do unto him*.

**Verse 12.** *{Strangers}* Hereby is signified the destruction of the power of the Assyrians by the Babylonians.

**Verse 13.** *{Shall}* Or, *did*.

*{fowls of the heaven}* The nations which were subject to the Assyrians, after the overthrow of the empire, did prey upon them with contempt.

*{Shall}* Or, *did*.

**Verse 14.** *{To the end that}* I have set forth the spectacle of my judgments upon Ashur, to this end, for to teach all princes that are grown great through my blessings, not to raise themselves against me, through pride; for they are all mortal by nature, and I can bring down their estates as I have raised them.

*{neither shoot up their top}* As the Assyrian did.

*{stand up in their height}* Or, *stand upon themselves for their height*.

*{for they are all delivered}* Psal.82.7.

**Verse 15.** *{Caused a mourning}* By this fall of Assyria, I raised a general horror, and terror in them all, seeing him on a sudden deprived of my help and blessing, by the means of which he had prospered so well.

*{to mourn for him}* Heb. *to be black*.

**Verse 16.** *{All the trees}* Ver.8,9.

*{shall be}* Or, *were*.

*{comforted in the nether parts of the earth}* To cause this destruction of the king of Assyria to seem more horrible, he sets forth other kings and princes which are dead, as though they rejoiced at the fall of such a tyrant. A figurative and poetical representation; as Isa.14.8,9,10. Ezek.32.31. Hab.1.6,7,8.

**Verse 17.** *{His arm that dwelt under his shadow}* That is, all under his power and dominion.

**Verse 18.** *{To whom art thou}* He applies the foresaid things by comparison to Pharaoh; as if he should say, Compare yourself to whom you will, you cannot avoid the ruin that I shall bring upon you; as you may see in the foresaid example of the Assyrian.

*{uncircumcised}* Chap.28.10.

*{This is Pharaoh}* All this allegorical speech has relation to Pharaoh.

## CHAPTER 32

**Verse 1.** *{And it came to pass}* In this chapter are the two last prophecies against Egypt: the one a fortnight after the other. In the former under the title, and by the similitude of a whale, is set down the fearful destruction of Egypt, by God's judgments; with the manner of them, and universal astonishment at them, and by whom to be executed. In the latter is set down Egypt's funeral solemnity, as it were, and manifold welcoming to the grave.

*{in the twelfth year}* Of the captivity of Jehojachin, Chap.1.2. one year, seven months, and 22 days after the city was taken, Jer.52.5,6. a month after the taking whereof, both Temple and city were burnt, Jer.52.12.

**Verse 2.** *{Lion of the nations}* In cruelty and violence. See Chap.19.2, &c.

*{whale}* Or, *Dragon*, or, *Crocodile*. See Chap.29.3. Thus the Scriptures compare tyrants to cruel and huge beasts, which devour all that be weaker than they, and such as they may overcome.

*{camest forth}* By reason of the convenient situation of your kingdom, Nilus at many ports running into the Midland Sea, you did undertake divers enterprises, annoying and invading other countries: a similitude taken from crocodiles which come up into the land by divers arms of Nilus.

*{the rivers}* The states of neighboring nations.

**Verse 3.** *{spread out my net}* Chap.12.13. and 17.20.

*{with a company of many people}* That shall be as it were my fishers, under the standard of the Babylonian.

**Verse 4.** *{Then will I leave thee}* Chap.29.5.

**Verse 5.** *{with thy height}* With heaps of the carcasses of your army, seem you in your own eyes never so high and haughty.

**Verse 6.** *{the land wherein thou swimmest}* Or, *the land of thy swimming*; that is, the land subject to your empire; namely, Egypt; and as Nilus overflows Egypt, so will I make the blood of your host to overflow it.

**Verse 7.** *{Put thee out}* Or, *extinguish*.

*{I will cover the heaven}* Joel 2.31. and 3.15. Mat.24.29. I will fill the world with horror, and terror, at your ruin. See Isa.13.10.

**Verse 8.** *{bright lights of heaven}* Heb. *light of the light in heaven*.

*{dark over thee}* Heb. *them dark*.

*{set darkness upon thy land}* The darkness of sorrow, the darkness of confusion. I will deprive you of all wisdom, and direction, and will bring all your state into confusion; extreme miseries, and desolation.

**Verse 9.** *{I will also vex}* Heb. *provoke to anger*, or, *grief*, with horror and fear, they seeing Egypt so mighty a kingdom, perish, and the king of Babylon grown so great.

**Verse 10.** *{Brandish my sword}* In their sight, against you, with such speedy and bloody execution.

*{moment}* Chap.26.16.

**Verse 12.** *{The terrible}* Chap.28.7. and 30.11. and 32.12. and 26.7.

**Verse 13.** *{All the beasts thereof}* Their horses, in the multitude whereof they trusted so much, Esay 31.3.

*{great waters}* From off the land of Egypt, which is abundantly watered, and therefore very fruitful.

*{neither shall the foot}* Chap.29.11.

*{trouble them any more}* A figurative description of a mighty desolation of a watery country, wherein there is neither man, nor beast, for to trouble the waters.

**Verse 14.** *{waters deep}* It seems to be understood of the Chaldeans, their enemies, which shall quickly enjoy all their commodities. Or else, of other nations, formerly, as waters, troubled with the feet of the Egyptians, as ver.2. which now henceforth should be quiet, as deep waters, and oil, runs smoothly and quietly, not turbulently.

**Verse 15.** *{destitute of that whereof it was full}* Heb. *desolate from the fullness thereof*.

**Verse 16.** *{The daughters}* The cities, and commonalties.

**Verse 17.** *{Twelfth year}* See vers.1. Fifteen days after the former prophecy: a kind of funeral dirge and exequy over Pharaoh and Egypt.

*{Month}* Namely, the twelfth month, ver.1.

**Verse 18.** *{wail for the multitude}* Make some kind of funeral exequies for Egypt, by lamentations, or mournful songs, according to the custom; and for the funeral of other nations likewise.

*{cast them down}* That is, prophesy, that they shall be cast down. Thus the Lord gives his Prophets power to plant and to destroy, by his word: Read Jer.1.10. Or, (as others) carry but as it were Egypt's funeral, and bear it to the grave.

**Verse 19.** *{whom dost thou pass}* O Egypt, what privilege have, or deserve you, more than all the other profane nations, and kingdoms? You must even perish, as all the rest have, and must do, Chap.31.18.

*{with the uncircumcised}* Ver.21–30,32. See Chap.28.10.

**Verse 20.** *{shall fall in the midst}* God will make a general destruction of such people by war. To do which, he has already given the Chaldeans a commission.

*{she is delivered}* Or, *the sword is laid*.

*{draw her}* Or, *they shall draw*; That is, Egypt; even as one would draw a dead carcass.

**Verse 21.** *{mighty shall speak}* To make the matter the more sensible, he uses a poetical and ironical representation, of the entertainment which other princes and warlike nations, and especially those which were Egypt's confederates, and were already dead, would give Pharaoh at his coming into hell or the grave. See Isa.14.9,10,11,15.

**Verse 22.** *{his graves}* Namely, the graves of his men which were slain in the wars. Or, the graves of the Assyrian compassing round about the grave of the new come Egyptian, surrounding him, and giving him the middle and chief place. See ver.23. and 25.

**Verse 23.** *{Caused terror}* Or, *caused dismaying*; who had also been violent and tyrannical in this world, like Egypt.

**Verse 24.** *{There is Elam}* Meaning the king and people of Persia, and Media; or of some part of it, Gen.10.22. They had been overthrown sooner after the beginning of the reign of Zedekiah, Jer.49.34, &c.

*{in the land of the living}* Whom, in his life, all the world feared.

**Verse 25.** *{round about him}* See ver.22.

*{he is put in the midst}* See ver.22.

**Verse 26.** *{There is Meshech}* Gen.10.2. that is, the Cappadocians, and Italians, or Spaniards; as Josephus writes.

*{Tubal}* Usually joined with Meshech. See Chap.27.13.

**Verse 27.** *{Not lie with the mighty}* These kings did not die gloriously, having overcome their enemies; neither are buried triumphantly with their arms, as it is the manner to honor warriors' burials; but these are dead basely, being overcome and discomfited by their enemies. Others thus; That though Meshech and Tubal were not dead, and lying with the mighty that are fallen of the uncircumcised; but did now domineer over Elam; yet they should fall before the time of the Egyptians' fall; and their graves should be round about his, as is here expressed.

*{with their weapons of war}* Heb. *with weapons of their war*.

*{their swords under their heads}* The ancient manner of warlike funerals. Not much unlike ours in these times. Other funerals content themselves with scutcheons, coat armor, and other ensigns of honor.

*{their iniquities shall be upon their bones}* They which have not suffered punishment for their faults, and tyrannies in this life; yet it shall be reserved for them, to be inflicted upon them after their death; by reason that no man dared undertake to punish them whilst they lived.

**Verse 28.** *{Thou shalt be broken}* Namely, you O king of Egypt. Some understand it of Meshech and Tubal.

**Verse 29.** *{All her Princes}* Gen.36.40.

*{laid}* Heb. *given*, or, *put*.

**Verse 30.** *{Princes of the North}* Of Tyre, Damascus, Syria, and others; those Chap.26. and 27. and 28. Jer.47. and 49.22,23, &c.

*{with their terror}* Notwithstanding, or together with, that great power, with which they kept men subject to their tyranny.

*{are ashamed of their might}* Because that it could not save them, but is at the last come to nothing.

**Verse 31.** *{shall be comforted}* As the wicked comfort themselves, when they see others partakers of their miseries: but a poor comfort.

**Verse 32.** *{I have caused my terror}* I will make the Egyptians afraid of me, as they caused others to fear them. Others, I have stayed their fury through my terrible judgments.

## CHAPTER 33

**Verse 1.** *{Again the word}* In this chapter God declares the duty of a watchman; applies and enjoins it to Ezekiel. Clears his justice against the cavil of the Jews, that his ways are not equal. And upon the Prophet's hearing of the taking of the city, God commands him to prophesy the further destruction of the remnant left in Judea; and shows him the hypocrisy of those with him in the captivity.

**Verse 2.** *{unto the Children of thy people}* Though for a time I did silence you from preaching to them, Chap.24.27. (And thereupon from that chapter to this, his prophecies are against foreign nations;) yet now the sign being come that I then foretold, Chap.24.26. and 33.21. According to my promise then made, I do again open your mouth, that you be no longer dumb and silent towards them: But I enjoin you again to speak and prophesy unto the children of your people. Attend therefore unto your function; to the duty, and necessity of it.

*{when I bring}* God the author of punishments, by wars, &c. Amos 3.6.

*{the sword upon a land}* Heb. *a land when I bring a sword upon her*: That is, war, with all the sudden invasions, inroads, and dangers belonging to it.

*{of their Coasts}* For, on the coasts and borders of the land, watches are set in times of war, and invasions.

*{watchman}* He minds, that the people ought to have continually governors, and teachers, which may have a care over them, and to warn them ever of the dangers which are at hand.

**Verse 4.** *{whosoever heareth}* Heb. *he that hearing heareth*.

*{his blood shall be upon his own head}* Only, not on the watchman's head; his death must not be imputed to any other, but to himself.

**Verse 6.** *{He is taken away in his iniquity}* Signifying, that the wicked shall not escape punishment, though the watchman be negligent; but if the watchman blow the trumpet, and then he will not obey, he shall deserve double punishment.

*{but his blood}* The watchman must answer for his part, for the blood of all that perish through his fault and negligence.

**Verse 7.** *{So thou}* God applies the former case to the Prophet.

*{I have set thee a watchman}* Chap. 3.17, &c.

*{at my mouth}* Which teaches, that he that receives not his charge at the Lord's mouth, is a spy, and not a true watchman.

**Verse 8.** *{When I say unto the wicked}* Chap. 3.18.

**Verse 10.** *{Thus ye speak}* Against me, and you, and your prophecies; and against themselves.

*{if our transgressions}* The meaning is, it is true that you Prophets have admonished us heretofore; and we having neglected your admonitions, do bear our punishment, and are now ruined past all relief; how should we then live? How can you now prophesy and promise life unto us? What good does it do then to speak to us any more of these preservations against the evil, into which we are already fallen? The Lord answers, I even then protested and promised life to the repentant, Chap. 18.23. And yet your ruin is not so extreme, but that you may rise again by true repentance; which comes always in time, either before, or after the punishments, so it come during life.

*{we pine away in them}* Levit. 26.39.

**Verse 11.** *{I have no pleasure in the death of the wicked}* The full answer of the former cavillation, Chap. 18.3,13,22. See 2 Sam. 14.14.

*{why will ye die}* Chap. 18.31.

**Verse 12.** *{The righteousness}* Chap. 18.24.

**Verse 13.** *{that he shall surely live}* If he persevere to the end; a condition necessarily annexed to all the promises of the Law.

*{all his righteousness shall not be remembered}* Or, *not any of all his righteousness shall be remembered.*

**Verse 14.** *{that which is lawful and right}* Hereby he condemns all them of hypocrisy, which pretend to forsake wickedness, and yet declare not themselves such by their fruits; that is, in obeying God's commandments, and by godly life.

*{lawful and right}* Heb. *Judgment and Justice.*

**Verse 15.** *{If the wicked restore the pledge}* Chap. 18.7. Exod. 22.26. Deut. 24.12.

*{give again that he had robbed}* Exod. 22.1,4. Levit. 6.2,4. Num. 5.6,7.

*{The statutes}* The observation of which statutes have the promises of life annexed to them, Lev. 18.5.

**Verse 17.** *{the way of the LORD}* Chap. 18.25.

**Verse 21.** *{twelfth year of our Captivity}* Chap. 1.2. when the Prophet was led away captive with Jeconiah. See Annotations on Chap. 24.26.

*{the city is smitten}* 2 King. 25. Taken by force, and sacked.

**Verse 22.** *{Now the hand}* I was endued with the spirit of prophecy, Chap. 1.3. and 8.1.

*{had opened my mouth}* He had again appointed me to speak to the people, after I had a while held my tongue by his command; and had revealed unto me, and inspired me with what I should say, according to the prediction, Chap. 24.26,27. And hereby is signified, that the ministers of God cannot speak till God give them courage, and open their mouths, Chapter 29. vers. 21. Ephes. 6.19.

**Verse 24.** *{Speak}* Proudly and presumptuously.

*{those wastes}* Ver. 27.

*{Abraham was one}* And yet God gave him this land by promise. We are many, and Abraham's heirs; Therefore we sure shall inherit it. And if out of Abraham alone could come so numerous an issue, as could conquer and people all this country; how much more may we hope that are left in great number, that we may be restored to our former estate, which is beaten down by the Chaldeans; and once again repossess, and be masters of the country? Thus the wicked think themselves more worthy to enjoy God's promises, than the Saints of God to whom they were made; and would bind God to be subject to them, though they would not be bound to him.

**Verse 25.** *{ye eat the blood}* Contrary to the Law, Gen. 9.4. Lev. 7.26. and 17.10. and 19.26. Deut. 12.16. which you transgress; and so declare you are not true sons of Abraham, Joh. 8.39.

*{and lift up your eyes}* Chap. 18.6.

**Verse 26.** *{Ye stand}* You men stand upon the sword, and force, or violence; and you women work abomination: meaning, that everyone rights himself, defends, and revenges himself, and wrongs others by violence; and justice, and good order, and honesty, are quite beaten down.

**Verse 27.** *{they that are in the wastes}* That inhabit those wastes of the land of Israel, vers. 24. those now left in the land, 2 King. 25.12,22. Jer. 40.5, &c.

*{to be devoured}* Heb. *to devour him.*

*{in the Caves}* In hidden and strong places, into which people did use to fly in time of war. See Judg. 6.2. 1 Sam. 13.6.

**Verse 28.** *{most desolate}* Heb. *desolation and desolation.*

*{the pomp of her strength shall cease}* Chap. 7.24. and 24.21. and 30.6,7.

**Verse 30.** *{still}* Still even after the city is taken.

*{and talking against thee}* Or, *of thee:* either in profane contempt and derision; or else through hypocrisy, or curiosity pretending fair, as if now, seeing the prophecy against Jerusalem to be fulfilled, they would be said by you, and ruled by your word from the Lord.

*{by the walls}* In all places.

**Verse 31.** *{as the people cometh}* Heb. *according to the coming of the people:* very frequently, and with a show of devotion, as it were, to an ecclesiastical assembly, Chap. 14.1.

*{they sit before thee as my people}* Or, *my people sit before thee.*

*{for with their mouth}* Isa. 29.13.

*{they shew much love}* Heb. *they make loves,* or, *jests.* This declares, that we ought to hear God's Word with such zeal and affection, that we should in all points obey it; else we abuse the Word to our own condemnation, and make of his ministers, as though they were jesters, to serve men's foolish fantasies.

**Verse 32.** *{A very lovely song}* Heb. *a song of Loves.*

**Verse 33.** *{when this cometh}* That, ver. 27,28,28. that is, the accomplishments of my threatenings in the last ruin of the whole nation.

*{That a Prophet}* Chap. 2.5.

## CHAPTER 34

**Verse 1.** *{And the word of the LORD}* A prophecy against the shepherds of Israel; their maladministration. The hurt of it to the sheep. God's judgments upon them for the maladministration. He will deliver his flock from them; gather them, bring them back, and be a good shepherd to them. He will judge between the sheep, bad and good. He will set up one shepherd; with many gracious promises thereto annexed.

**Verse 2.** *{Shepherds of Israel}* Jer. 23.1.

*{woe be to the shepherds}* By the shepherds, he means the King, the magistrates, Priests, and Prophets, all governors both ecclesiastical, and politic.

*{that do feed themselves}* Or, *that should feed them;* that is, the sheep of Israel.

*{should not}* Their duty.

**Verse 3.** *{Ye eat the fat}* Do the contrary to your duty. You seek to enrich yourselves by their commodities; and so spoil their riches, and substance, Zach. 11.5,16.

**Verse 4.** *{the diseased have ye not strengthened}* By their faults, and failings, he shows the office and duty of a good pastor; who ought to love and succor his flock, and not to be cruel toward them.

*{with force and with cruelty have ye ruled them}* 1 Pet. 5.3.

**Verse 5.** *{And they were scattered}* Spiritually, by running into errors, superstitious idolatries; vices, and disorders: or corporally by their dispersion out of their country by the ill leading of their guides. Here is the hurt the sheep received by their maladministration.

*{because there is no shepherd}* Or, *without a shepherd;* and so ver. 8. that is, no true and faithful shepherd. For lack of good government, and doctrine, they perished, Zech. 11.17.

**Verse 6.** *{and none did search}* As shepherds in duty ought to have done. See ver. 12.

**Verse 8.** *{As I live}* God, as it were, in a passion against these evil shepherds; to grate again upon their fault, breaks off his sentence, begun against them in the former verse; and resumes it, after this verse.

**Verse 10.** *{for I will deliver my flock from their mouth}* By destroying the covetous hirelings, and restoring true shepherds; whereof we have a sign, so oft as God sends true preachers, who both by doctrine and life, labor to feed his sheep in the pleasant pasture of his Word, ver. 22.

**Verse 12.** *{As a shepherd seeketh}* Heb. *according to the seeking.*

*{in the day that he is among his sheep}* When after they have been scattered by some sudden storm, the shepherd tells, and reckons up his sheep, to see whether any be wanting; and then seeks them out, and brings them back, and gathers them together, and feeds them, and folds them.

*{in the cloudy and dark day}* Which is full of calamities, and public desolations, Joel 2.2. And this promise is to comfort the Church in all dangers.

**Verse 13.** *{and I will bring them to their own land}* Corporally, and according to the letter, into the land of Israel, by delivering them out of captivity, Chap. 36.24. and spiritually, into the Church, which is the true sheepfold of all the elect, Joh. 10.16.

**Verse 14.** *{I will feed them in a good pasture}* Psal. 23.1,2.

*{upon the high mountains of Israel}* A country full of high and fruitful mountains, Chap. 36.1.

**Verse 15.** *{I will feed my flock}* This does not infer any abolishment of either ecclesiastical or politic government in the Christian Church: but does infer a more effectual and renewed presence of God in it, by his Spirit, providence, grace, and virtue; or the mystical and inward operation of Christ, true God, upon all the members of his body, in quickening, conducting, comforting, and defending them.

**Verse 16.** *{destroy the fat and the strong}* Those who in my Church shall abuse my benefits unto pride, and rebellion against me; and to contempt and oppression of their brethren, ver. 21. See Isa. 10.16. Amos 4.1. Deut. 32.15. Psal. 78.31.

*{with judgment}* That is, by putting a difference between the good and the bad, and so give to either according to his work; as in the next verse. See Psal. 62.11,12.

**Verse 17.** *{Behold I judge}* Vers. 22. Chap. 20.37,38. Zech. 10.3. Mat. 25.32.

*{cattle and cattle}* Heb. *small cattle of lambs and kids.*

*{he goats}* Heb. *great he-goats.*

**Verse 18.** *{Seemeth it a small thing unto you}* O you powerful and rich Jews, is it not sufficient for you to enjoy my blessings in abundance, unless you trouble the poor in the possession of their small portion?

*{to have eaten up the good pasture, but, &c.}* By good pasture, and deep waters may be meant the pure Word of God, and the administration of justice, which they did not distribute to the poor, till they had corrupted it.

**Verse 20.** *{the fat cattle}* Vers. 16,17,21.

**Verse 21.** *{Ye have thrust}* Vers. 16.

**Verse 22.** *{a prey}* Ver. 10.

**Verse 23.** *{I will set up one Shepherd over them}* Isa. 40.11. Joh. 10.11. Heb. 13.20. 1 Pet. 2.25. and 5.4.

*{my servant David}* Namely, Jesus Christ, the son of David, according to the flesh; and for this reason often called David, Jer. 30.9. Ezek. 37.24. Hos. 3.5. and the servant of God, by reason of his human nature, and office of Mediator, Isa. 42.1. and 49.6. and 53.11. Phil. 2.7. Joh. 10.16. 1 Chron. 17.19.

**Verse 25.** *{of peace}* Of grace, reconciliation, and blessing, Jer. 31.31. Esay 11.10.

*{and will cause the evil beasts to cease out of the land}* Isa. 11.6,8. and 35.9. Hos. 2.18. I will free the land of those wild beasts that overran the land in the time of captivity. Figurative terms to show the Church's safeguard against all her spiritual enemies.

**Verse 26.** *{I will make them and the places round about my hill, a blessing}* I will bless them, and their habitation, and their pastures abundantly, Chap. 36.8, &c.

*{showers of blessing}* Psal. 68.9,10.

**Verse 27.** *{And the earth shall yield her increase}* Psal. 85.12.

*{when I have broken the bands of their yoke}* Chap. 30.18. when I have brought them out of captivity; and so likewise, when I freed and redeemed them by the blood of my Son, from the spiritual captivity of the devil and sin.

**Verse 28.** *{but they shall dwell safely}* Jer. 30.10. and 46.27. Esay 43.5.

**Verse 29.** *{a plant}* Jesus Christ, glorious in his divine majesty and strength: being the true tree of life. See Isa. 11.1. Jer. 23.5. Or else, his Church, not of Jews only, but of Gentiles also, planted and rooted in him; and made famous through his gifts and graces.

*{of renown}* Or, *for renown.* Christ, and his Church likewise, shall be of renown, not only among the Jews, as in time past, but also abroad among the Gentiles, which is demonstrated in the last verse; and more copiously, Chap. 36.

*{consumed}* Heb. *taken away.*

**Verse 31.** *{my flock, the flock of my pasture}* Joh. 10.11. Psal. 100.3.

*{men}* As Esay 5.7. All the aforesaid things are to be understood spiritually, with a relation to the soul of the elect, and the salvation of it: so that the correspondency which is between the sheep, a brute beast; and the shepherd, which is man, is found in spirit between man, and his God.

## CHAPTER 35

**Verse 1.** *{Moreover}* God's judgments of desolation upon Mount Seir, for their hatred, bloodiness, envy, and anger against the Jews; for their greediness after their land and possession, and joy at their fall: and for their boasting and blasphemy against God.

**Verse 2.** *{Set thy face}* Chap. 6.2.

*{Seir}* Where the Idumeans dwelt, who came of Esau, Gen. 36.9.

*{Prophesy against it}* Chap. 25.11. Jer. 49.7,8. Amos 1.11. Obad. 11. Esay 34.5.

**Verse 3.** *{most desolate}* Heb. *desolation and desolation.*

**Verse 5.** *{perpetual hatred}* Or, *hatred of old.* Against Israel. See Ezek. 25.8,12. and 36.5. Amos 1.11. See the like hatred of the Philistines, Chap. 25.15.

*{and hast shed the blood of the children}* Heb. *poured out the children;* that is, have assisted the Chaldeans in making a horrible slaughter of my people, Psal. 137.7. Ezek. 25.12.

*{force of the sword}* Heb. *hands.*

*{in the time that their iniquity had an end}* Or, *brought an end.* At which time the measure of my people's sin being full, I also caused my judgments to fall violently upon them, Ezek. 21.25.

**Verse 6.** *{blood shall pursue thee}* Namely, the sword, and violent death.

*{sith thou hast not hated blood}* Psal. 109.17,18.

**Verse 9.** *{I will make thee perpetual desolations}* Jer. 49.10,13,17,18,21. Mal. 1.3,4.

*{and thy cities shall not return}* To wit, to their former estate.

**Verse 10.** *{These two Nations}* Of the Israelites, subdued by the Assyrian; and of Judah, by Nebuchadnezzar. You have persuaded yourself that you should possess the land of Judah, and of the ten Tribes.

*{and we will possess it}* Psal. 83.5,13. Chap. 36.2,3,5.

*{whereas the LORD was there}* Or, *though the Lord was there.* Though the Lord had taken it for his own proper habitation, placing his people there; and his Temple, with the signs of his presence. Thus they blaspheme God himself; as himself does challenge them for it, ver. 13.

**Verse 11.** *{And I will make myself known among them}* I will make myself known to my Church, by new trials of my justice and power against her enemies; and of my love and mercy towards her.

**Verse 12.** *{to consume}* Heb. *to devour.* See Psal. 137.7.

**Verse 13.** *{boasted}* Heb. *magnified.*

*{and have multiplied your words against me}* 1 Sam. 2.3.

**Verse 14.** *{when the whole earth rejoiceth}* Namely, when I shall cause all the world to rejoice at my people's deliverance, and their reestablishment in their country, then will I increase your desolation. This was partly verified in the Idumeans; but has been accomplished in the enemies of the Church; whose curse and condemnation increased, when salvation appeared to the elect, and believers through Christ. Or, as some do render and interpret the words; As that whole land rejoices, (that is, though all Edom rejoice now at Judah's fall) I will make you desolate; that is, yet for all your present joy, I will make you Edom desolate. And this seems best to agree with the next verse.

## CHAPTER 36

**Verse 1.** *{Also thou son of man}* A sum of the former Chapter; for God's judgments upon Edom, and the causes thereof, here repeated. And notwithstanding his people's sinning, and being therefore chastised and scattered among the nations, and yet continuing there to sin, nevertheless for his own name's sake, the Lord gives, and will make good unto them, many great and gracious promises, both spiritual, and temporal; chiefly in Christ.

*{the mountains of Israel}* Chap. 6.2. the men being now wasted and gone.

**Verse 2.** *{The enemy}* That is, the Idumeans, see Chap. 35.10. Chap. 25.3. and 26.2.

*{had said against you}* Has triumphed and scoffed at your desolation.

*{The ancient high places}* Heb. *the everlasting hills.* An epithet ordinarily given to high hills, Gen. 49.26. Deut. 33.15. Hab. 3.6. by reason of their immovable firmness; but by figure is chiefly meant the Church, founded upon God's promises, and therefore the chiefest of all the world, Deut. 32.13.

**Verse 3.** *{because}* Heb. *because for because.*

*{ye are taken up in the lips of talkers}* Or, *Ye are made to come upon the lip of the tongue.*

**Verse 4.** *{rivers and to the valleys}* Or, *bottoms,* or, *dales.*

**Verse 5.** *{which have appointed my land into their possession}* They appointed with themselves to have it; and therefore came with Nebuchadnezzar against Jerusalem for this purpose.

**Verse 6.** *{the shame of the heathen}* Because you have been a laughingstock unto them.

**Verse 7.** *{I have lifted up mine hand}* As Gen. 14.22. by making a solemn oath: read Chap. 20.5.

**Verse 8.** *{Ye shall shoot forth your branches}* You shall be endowed with new fruitfulness, which my people shall enjoy, being shortly to come out of captivity: a figurative description of the happiness which shall be in the world under the Messiah, in the behalf of the Church.

*{for they are at hand to come}* I will shortly bring back the Israelites unto you.

**Verse 9.** *{I will turn unto you}* Take care of you, Deut. 11. ver. 12.

**Verse 11.** *{Than at your beginnings}* Which was accomplished under Christ, to whom all these temporals did direct them.
