The prince's voluntary offering. The daily offering. The prince's inheritance, and his gifts to sons and servants, the places of boiling and baking for the priests. The boiling places of the Levites for the sacrifices of the people.

*{the gate of the Inner Court}* Next the Temple, Chap. 40.44, &c.

*{shall be shut}* Chap. 44.1, 2, 3.

*{but on the Sabbath-day}* and other festivals.

**V. 2.** *{shall stand by the post}* but not come into the court.

*{shall not be shut}* The reason of it is in the next verse.

**V. 4.** *{And the burnt-offering}* Much more, than was enjoined in the Law, Num. 28.9.

**V. 5.** *{an Ephah——}* See vers. 11.

**V. 6.** *{And in the day of the new Moon}* Less than was enjoined by the Law of Moses, Num. 28.11.

**V. 8.** *{and when the Prince shall enter}* The east gates of the courts allowed to the prince, for access, for ingress, and egress, even as for the priests and Levites. They are joined together and must join their strengths and powers together: they both must go the same way; not contrary ways.

**V. 9.** *{But when the people}* The north and south gates allowed to them; to come in at the one, and go out at the other. This may mind us of straight steps; to go still forward, Phil. 3.13, 14. not to look back, as Lot's wife, Luk. 9.62. And repenting, not to return back the same way we came, 2 Pet. 2.21, 22. Came we carnal? let us return spiritual.

**V. 10.** *{And the Prince}* Must observe the same time with the people, for coming to, and staying at God's public worship and service; must be an example to them; which will much prevail with them; *magnates* are *magnetes.*

**V. 11.** *{The meat-offerings shall be}* The particularities, and the quantities prescribed in God's offerings and worship.

*{Ephah}* See Chap. 45.11.

*{Hin}* As much as the shells of 72 hen's eggs do hold. These quantities and proportions differ from those in the Levitical Law, Num. 15. & 28. Exod. 29.40. There the tenth part of an Ephah; and the fourth part of an Hin: here, the sixth part of an Ephah; and the third part of an Hin: Likely, to imply, that as God's graces and blessings do abound under the Gospel, more than under the Law; so likewise should our service, thankfulness, and spiritual obedience abound.

**V. 12.** *{One shall shut the gate}* Not so on the Sabbaths, and new moons, vers. 2.

**V. 14.** *{the sixth part of an Ephah}* Differing from that, Exod. 29.40.

**V. 16.** *{If the Prince give}* It shall be his children's, and their seed for ever.

**V. 17.** *{to the year of liberty}* The jubilee, Lev. 25.10, 13, &c. and vers. 28. yea, the servant shall be then free, Exod. 21.5. Lev. 25.40, 41, 54.

**V. 18.** *{Prince shall not}* Chap. 45.8, 9.

*{be not scattered}* Which caused Naboth to refuse Ahab, 1 King. 21.3. Num. 36.7.

**V. 19.** *{at the side of the gate——}* Of that third and inmost court, whereinto the prince and people were in no wise to enter, Chap. 40.44, &c.

*{on the two sides westward}* Two little courts for the priests, on the two sides next the Temple, and looking westward. These courts, for boiling seem to be placed without that compass of 100 cubits wherewith the courts were bounded.

**V. 20.** *{This is the place}* for the priests only, to boil and bake in. The kitchens of the priests, in that inmost court, behind their chambers, westward.

*{into the utter court}* That belongs to the people, Chap. 42.14. & 44.19.

*{to sanctify the people}* See Chap. 44.19.

**V. 21.** *{utter court}* Or, *Middle Court,* for the Levites; called the *utter Court,* in relation to the inmost court, of the priests.

*{in every corner of the court, there was a court}* Four little courts for the Levites, in that middle court.

**V. 22.** *{Courts joined}* Or, *with chimneys.* Their spaciousness.

**V. 23.** *{in them round about——}* Buildings within the kitchens, fitted for boilings.

## CHAPTER 47

**Verse 1.** *{Afterwards}* We have here, The vision of the rise, progress, and healing virtue of the holy waters, and of the trees on the banks. The borders of the land of Canaan: which is to be divided by lot, to the 12 tribes, and to the sojourners among them.

*{he brought me}* The hand of the Lord, Chap. 1.3. & 3.22. The Spirit, Chap. 4.12, 14, 24.

*{door of the house}* The Temple.

*{waters issued}* This also shows this whole vision, Chap. 40. to the end of the book, to be allegorical and typical. These waters shadow out and show the Spirit of God, and the gifts of the Spirit bestowed on the Christian Church, to be small in their beginnings, but to grow very great after. And particularly, that the doctrine of the Gospel should have the like weak beginning, and wondrous progress and propagation afterwards, Apoc. 22.1. is a river of water of life, clear as crystal.

*{right side}* The right hand and side, is taken in Scripture to be toward the south, or for the south: Man, as has been said, being placed in posture with his face toward the east; as the front and porch of the Temple was. These waters issue from the Temple. Those, Apoc. 22.1. proceed out of the throne of God, and of the Lamb.

*{came down}* For the Temple was on a hill.

**V. 2.** *{the gate Northward}* for the east gate of the inmost court was shut, Chap. 44.1, 2, 3. & 46.1.

*{utter gate}* Of the utmost great compass and wall.

*{ran out}* As out of a vial.

**V. 5.** *{a river that,* &c.} Representing the abundance of spiritual graces in the Church of Christ, Ephe. 1.3. Joel 3.18. Zech. 14.8. Apoc. 22.1. Joh. 4.13, 14. & 7.38, 39.

**V. 8.** *{Go into the Sea}* Called the Salt Sea, or Sea of Sodom, the lake *Asphaltites,* or Dead Sea; because all living things die in it. Such are we, as the Dead Sea, Eph. 2.3.

*{the waters shall be healed}* See Joh. 5.4. The meaning here is, that they should be made wholesome for fish, and living creatures; as in the two next verses. And so the virtue and efficacy of spiritual blessings in the Gospel, the merits of Christ, and graces of the Holy Spirit, do heal, revive, regenerate and make fruitful men, formerly dead in trespasses and sins.

**V. 9.** *{the rivers}* Of those abundant waters. So in the end of this verse.

*{a very great multitude of fish}* The fecundity and fruitfulness of these spiritual waters; the exceeding great number of believers, Esay 66.7, 8, 12. & 60.3, 4, 5, 8. & 49.18, 20, 21, 22, 23. Apoc. 7.4, 9. Though yet they be few in comparison of them that enter in at the wide gate, and go the broad way that leads to destruction.

**V. 10.** *{The fishers}* That before had nothing to do for fishing in that Dead Sea; though they fished much in the Lake of Genezaret, and upward of Jordan.

*{Engedi,}* Or, *Emmisphat,* or, *Kadesh,* Gen. 14.7. Or rather, a town upon the Dead Sea, otherwise called Hazazon Tamar, 2 Chr. 20.2.

*{Eneglaim}* A place at the entrance of the Dead Sea, where Jordan runs into it; as some writers report.

*{according to their kinds}* No kind of fish not there; as in the great Sea. No kind of sinner excepted from this fishing, Gal. 3.28.

*{as the fish}* Where none were, or could be before; now so exceeding many.

**V. 11.** *{the miry places}* Some translate and expound it, that even those places of it, that were not healed, but given to salt, and in a manner impossible to be healed, should yet be healed, and abound in fish. Others, in an allegorical sense, to another meaning; That such as will rather choose to wallow, as swine, in the mire of their sins, without repentance, and not come to these healing waters, shall not be healed, but hardened, 2 Cor. 2.15, 16.

**V. 12.** *{all trees}* See Rev. 22.2. There is the tree of life, (as Gen. 2.9.) which bare 12 manner of fruits; and that every month. This here still shows all to be allegorical and typical. Let these trees mind us of our qualifications, and duties, wherein we are resembled to trees in Scripture, Psal. 1.3. & 92.13. Esay. 61.3. Jer. 17.8. We must not be barren trees, Luk. 73.7. nor bring forth wild grapes, Esay. 5.2. but fruit for meat, and leaves for medicine; and that without fading, or decaying: but still green and flourishing, 2 Pet. 1.5. &c.

*{because their waters,* &c.} The cause of their fruitfulness is not their own planting, or watering, 1 Cor. 3.6, 7. but God's blessing, issuing out of the Sanctuary of heaven, Joh. 15.5. 1 Cor. 15.10. Gal. 5.22. Jam. 1.17.

**V. 13.** *{This shall be the border}* Of the whole land, north, east, south, west.

*{Joseph shall have two portions}* That is, for Ephraim and Manasseh. So Deut. 33.13——18. See his right and title, Gen. 48.5, 6. and 49.22, &c. 1 Chron. 5.1, 2.

**V. 14.** *{one as well as another}* See vers. 22, 23. This may intimate an equal right of all sorts of persons in the kingdom of Christ, Gal. 3.26, 28. Col. 3.11. Act. 10.34. 1 Pet. 2.9, 10.

**V. 15.** *{from the great Sea}* The Mediterranean Sea. So ver. 19, 20. See Num. 24.7. from that Sea by mount Hor, (otherwise called Hermon, Shirion, Shenir, and Sion, Josh. 13.5. Deut. 3.9. and 4.43) to Hamath, &c.

*{the way of Hethlon}* Chap. 48.1. The west border of the kingdom of Damascus.

*{as men go to Zedad}* Num. 34.8. the east border of the kingdom of Damascus. So that the kingdom of Damascus seems to be taken in.

**V. 16.** *{Hamath}* East border, at the foot of Lebanon, Num. 13.22. and 34.8. 1 King. 8.65. Toi King of it, 2 Sam. 8.9.

*{Berothah}* a city of Hadad-Ezaz, 2 Sam. 8.8. bordering upon *Arabia deserta,* as Ptolomy says.

*{Sibraim}* Belonging likewise to *Arabia deserta.*

*{which is between}* All that scope of land pertaining to these two kingdoms.

*{Hauran}* a town like *Arabia deserta;* as geographers write, 2 Mac. 4.40.

**V. 17.** *{Hazar-Enan}* Chap. 48.1. Num. 34.9, 10. a town upon the northern borders of Judea, Damascus, and Hamath.

**V. 18.** *{From Hauran}* Vers. 16.

*{by Jordan}* Beyond Jordan; as Psal. 42.6.

*{unto the East Sea}* The Dead Sea, vers. 8. See Joel 2.20. Zech. 14.8.

**V. 19.** *{Tamar}* The last town of Judea, by Hengedi, at the Dead Sea.

*{waters of strife in Kadesh}* Num. 20.13. and 27.14. not in Rephidim, Exod. 17.7.

**V. 20.** *{over against Hemath}* to that place of the great Sea, from which lies a straight way towards Hamath eastward.

**V. 21.** *{according to the Tribes}* Little, or no land would remain for them, if the holy portion, cities, and prince's portion, Chap. 45. were to be measured by reeds, and not by cubits; unless we take all in an allegorical, and typical sense.

**V. 22.** *{and to the strangers}* It was otherwise in the division of the land by Moses, and Joshuah. But this might shadow out the right of the Gentiles, and their title and claim to the Church, and Gospel of Christ, and heavenly Canaan. See *Annotations* on ver. 14.

## CHAPTER 48

**Verse 1.** *{Now* &c.} Here are the several bounds, and portions of the land of Canaan, for the several tribes: for the Sanctuary, and priests; for the Levites; for the city, and suburbs; for the prince. The dimensions, gates, and name of the city.

*{from the North}* Begins there; and so goes on toward the south.

*{The way of Hethlon}* Chap. 47.15, 16, 17.

*{East and west}* Each tribe here runs his portion from east to west, the whole breadth of the land of Canaan: For so runs the breadth of that particular country; and the length of it from south to north: though all geographers in describing the whole earth, constantly reckon the longitudes, from east to west, and the latitudes from south to north. Christ's kingdom runs to the ends of the earth, Psal. 2.8.

*{for Dan}* The order of the tribes in their portion is this; viz. Dan, Asher, Naphtali, Mannasseth, Ephraim, Reuben, Judah, the portions for the priests, the Levites, the city, the prince, Benjamin, Simeon, Issachar, Zebulun, Gad.

**V. 8.** *{the offering which they shall offer}* For the priests, Levites, city, prince. Seven tribes lay northward; Five southward: and these portions in the middle.

*{of five and twenty thousand in breadth}* Reckoning so from north to south; as the breadth of the portions of all the tribes were reckoned: and reckoning in this sum, the priests, Levites, cities, and princes' portions.

*{and in length as one of the other parts}* In length from east to west, as one of the other parts of each several tribe; so taking up in length the whole breadth of the land.

**V. 9.** *{unto the LORD}* As distinguishing it from the other oblations, for the city and prince; and Levites some way too, as having the Sanctuary in it.

*{shall be five and twenty thousand in length}* Called *breadth* in the former verse; and there see the reckoning, and reason of it: here called *length,* in relation to the ten thousand; which are, as the far lesser number, justly here called the *breadth* of the priests' portion.

**V. 10.** *{Toward the North five and twenty thousand in length}* The meaning is, the northern border, limit, or line running east and west, was in length five and twenty thousand; and so the south border: and the east border, limit, or line, running north and south, being the breadth, was ten thousand; and so the west border: All which was the priests' holy portion, and the Sanctuary in the midst thereof.

**V. 11.** *{which went not astray}* Chap. 44.15, 16.

**V. 12.** *{And his oblation}* this part of the oblation.

**V. 13.** *{The Levites shall have}* Joining to the south of the priests' portion; and in length and breadth, as the priests' portion.

**V. 14.** *{not sell of it}* Lev. 25.23, 24. Num. 36.6. &c. See 1 King. 21.3.

**V. 15.** *{And the five thousand that are left in the breadth}* From north to south, to make up the ten thousand breadth of the priests, and the ten thousand breadth of the Levites, a perfect square, viz. five and twenty thousand; and so equal to the length aforesaid.

*{a profane place}* In opposition to that of Sanctuary, priests and Levites.

*{for the City}* Jerusalem, the suburbs, and the use of the inhabitants, and them that serve the city, as it is, vers. 16, 17, 18, 19.

*{in the midst thereof}* Of that last five and twenty thousand in length, and five thousand breadth. And so the city was to be distant from the Temple, about ten miles at least, if not twenty, or thirty, or sixty; according to the several measures, mentioned, vers. 10.

**V. 16.** *{The measures}* Thus out of the midst of the 25000 length, take 4500 square for the city; and 250 for the east suburbs of it, and 250 for the west suburbs of it; and this will take up 5000 of the length; and so will remain ten thousand of the length, on the east side, and ten thousand on the west side, for the use of the citizens, and them that serve the city. Thus the city is square. And so that new Jerusalem, and holy city, prepared as a bride, descending out of heaven from God, Apoc. 21.16. And the measure of that is, 12000 furlongs in length, breadth and height, which last is most wonderful. By these measures of Ezekiel's city, the compass of the walls is implied, viz. 4500 measures on each square; 18000 round about, vers. 35. The wall of the new Jerusalem, had twelve foundations, made of 12 several precious stones, and in them the names of the twelve Apostles: And above the foundations, the walls were made of jasper: the thickness of the walls, 144 cubits. And the city being 12000 furlongs, long, broad, and high, the walls of it must needs be, of that length and height, Apoc. 21.12, 14, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20. These are typical, and hieroglyphical descriptions.

*{And the South side four thousand five hundred}* Heb. מאות חמש חמש five five hundred. And here the Massorites note upon the second חמש, *Cetib velo Keri;* that is, it is written, but not to be read; as being redundant. And therefore they put no pricks or vowels, to that word in the text. As in the *Keri,* which they would have read, they put the pricks only, or vowels in the text, and the consonants in the margin.

**V. 17.** *{And the suburbs}* These suburbs, on the north and south side, fill up the breadth of five thousand; which is the whole breadth of the city's portion. and on the east and west, sides of the square, they fill up the length of five thousand; and so leave ten thousand east, and ten thousand west, for the use of the citizens, and them that serve the city.

**V. 18.** *{And the residue}* As is clearly showed, upon the two former verses. The breadth of each, the ten thousand long, is five thousand, being the breadth of the city's portion.

*{over against the oblation of the holy portion}* Joining to, and running in length, at the south side of the Levites' portion.

**V. 19.** *{Out of all the tribes of Israel}* And therefore the twelve gates are named, after the names of the twelve tribes, verse 31, 32, 33, 34.

**V. 20.** *{Four square, with}* That is, five and twenty thousand from east to west; and ten thousand of the priests, and ten thousand of the Levites, and five thousand of the city; in all, five and twenty thousand, running from north, to south, a perfect square.

**V. 21.** *{And the residue shall be for the Prince}* The residue eastward, from the east line, of five and twenty thousand, running from north to south, and comprehending the breadth of the portions, of the priests, Levites, and city; that residue from the east line eastward, to the east bounds of the land, shall be the prince's one portion. And the other equal and alike, from the west line of five and twenty thousand, running as aforesaid, and comprehending as aforesaid; that residue, from that west line westward, to the Midland Sea, shall be the prince's other portion.

*{in the midst thereof}* Between those two portions of the prince, in the midst of the priests' portion.

**V. 22.** *{Between the border of Judah}* All the portions for the prince, priests, Levites, city, lie between the portions of Judah, and of Benjamin.

**V. 28.** *{From Tamar}* See Chap. 47.19.

*{and to the river}* Sihor, which is before Egypt. Josh. 13.3. running by Pelza, in Arabia Petræa, into the lake Sirbonis, near the Mount Cesius, and divided between Judea, and Egypt.

*{toward the great sea}* The Midland Sea.

**V. 30.** *{North side, four thousand and five hundred}* See Annotations on vers. 16.

**V. 31.** *{And the gates of the City}* Three on each quarter, twelve in all; for the twelve tribes, and bearing their names; whereof one for Levi in this verse; and one for Joseph, comprehending Ephraim, and Manasses, in the next verse; which may show, that gates are open to all quarters of the world, for all men to come in to the Church of Christ, the city of God. The gates of the new Jerusalem were likewise twelve, three on each quarter; every several gate was of one pearl; and at the gates 12 Angels; and on the gates the names of the 12 tribes written. And these gates were never shut.

**V. 35.** *{round about, eighteen thousand}* That is, four times four thousand five hundred. All things in the holy city are four square; which may mere out the comeliness and constancy, the firm duration and perpetuation of the Church of Christ, to the end of the world.

*{the LORD is there}* *Jehovah Shammah,* Gen. 28.16. Jehovah is the proper and essential name of God. It is expounded, Exod. 3.14. & 6.3. Esay 42.8. Apoc. 1.4, 8. Jews in reverence will not name it. Surely we should not profane it; or by vain swearing, impious, forswearing, blaspheme it. What the ancient Jews write of this name, which they call *Tetragrammaton,* of four letters: For of other names or descriptions of God, which they call names of 12 letters, or of 42 letters, may rather serve for conviction of modern Jews, than otherwise be useful to us.

*{Shammah}* *Jehovah is there;* the founder, preserver, governor of it, Mat. 16.18. 1 Tim. 3.15. And this in its degree is true of every true Christian, Heb. 3.6. Eph. 2.19. 1 Cor. 3.9. & 14.25. & 6.19. Joh. 14.23. & 6.56. & 15.4. & 17.23. This shows our dignity, and our duty.

For the better clearing of the portions for the priests, Levites, city, and prince, look upon the delineation following.
